# dokufix — Proof of Concept, built from sources

A working prototype of the **dokufix** vision: one HTML file that is simultaneously the viewer, the editor, and the artifact for technical documentation written in Markdown, Mermaid and BPMN.

Open `dist/dokufix.html` in any modern browser. No install, no server, no account. For a first look open `dist/dokufix-showcase.html`: the same page with a short demo text that shows each core feature once.

That file is built from the sources in this folder with one command (see *Build*). It succeeds `poc/dokufix-poc.html`, which was written by hand and stays frozen as story 2.1 left it, with its README and its checks. The built file looks the same and does the same; what changed underneath is under *Build* and *Self-replication mechanism*. This README succeeds `poc/README.md`.

## What this PoC demonstrates

- **Markdown + Mermaid rendering** in the browser via `marked` and `mermaid` (loaded from CDN at pinned versions — production will inline these; see *Libraries*).
- **BPMN diagrams** — a fenced `bpmn` block with BPMN 2.0 XML and its coordinates, as a modeler writes it, becomes the diagram, drawn off-screen by `bpmn-js`; the document keeps only the SVG, with "Gezeichnet mit bpmn-js" below it, the name a link to bpmn.io. XML without coordinates, only lanes, steps and flows as a language model writes it, is laid out first, with Mermaid as the layout engine, and then drawn the same way. Every diagram, Mermaid or BPMN, stands in one figure named after the heading before it. See *Diagrams* below.
- **Large view of a diagram** — a click on a diagram shows it over the whole window, with its title, the zoom steps "Einpassen", 100, 150 and 200 % and "Schließen". By the document styles alone, so it works in every variant, `nur-lesen` without a script included; where a script runs, Escape closes it and `+` and `-` change the step. See *Diagrams*, the large view.
- **Downloads of a diagram** — below every drawn diagram two small buttons with a download symbol: the source, `.mmd` (the Mermaid text as written) or `.bpmn` (the XML as drawn, coordinates included, the computed ones where the author wrote none), a `data:` URL in the document that works without a script, in the editor, `Mit Editor`, `nur-lesen` and `kompakt`, and in `schlank` packed like the diagram, so only where its script runs; and, where a script runs, `.svg`, the picture as it is drawn, made at the click from the SVG in the figure. Files are named after the diagram's title. See *Diagrams*, the downloads.
- **Viewer ↔ Editor toggle.** Default mode is the reader experience; a discreet "Editor ↩" button in the corner reveals the editing UI.
- **IndexedDB persistence** — one record per dokufix file (keyed by UUID) holds the live markdown source, version history, and commit baseline. Image assets live in a separate object store, keyed by SHA-256 content hash. Documents that were saved by an older localStorage-based build of the PoC are migrated transparently on first open with the new code.
- **Image upload (paste, drag-and-drop, file picker)** — drop a file onto the editor, paste a screenshot from the clipboard, or use the `+ Bild` button. The image is decoded via `createImageBitmap` (with `imageOrientation: 'from-image'` so EXIF orientation is applied to the pixels), optionally downscaled to a max of 1600 px wide, re-encoded as WebP @ 0.85, hashed, and stored in IndexedDB. The canvas roundtrip is what strips EXIF metadata as a side effect; `createImageBitmap` itself doesn't strip anything. The markdown gets a `![alt](#asset-<hash>)` reference; the render pass swaps that reference for a Blob URL pulled from IndexedDB. Read-only exports replace `#asset-` and `blob:` URLs with inline `data:` URLs so the recipient has no IDB or runtime dependency. `Mit Editor` downloads include every asset referenced by the current source **or by any history snapshot** in a separate `<script type="application/json" id="dokufix-assets">` block — content-addressed, so a re-seed of an identical hash is a no-op.
- **Explicit save** — leaving edit mode does **not** auto-save; saving is a deliberate user action via the Download menu.
- **Self-replication** — the "Mit Editor" download produces a new HTML file with the user's content baked in (gzip-compressed). The receiver opens that file and starts from there.
- **Three read-only export tiers** — open / schlank / kompakt, each with different size-vs-portability tradeoffs.
- **Footnotes with hover previews** — GFM footnote syntax (`[^id]` / `[^id]:`) via `marked-footnote`, plus a preview that appears when the reader hovers or keyboard-focuses a marker, so an aside can be read without jumping to the bottom of the document. Pure CSS, so it works in the JS-free export. See *Footnotes* below.
- **Frontmatter metadata panel** — a leading YAML or JSON header block renders as a collapsible panel instead of leaking into the document as markup. Native `<details>`, so it collapses without JavaScript and survives the JS-free export. See *Frontmatter* below.
- **Callouts** — a blockquote that opens with `> [!NOTE]` or one of its four siblings, the alerts of GitHub Flavored Markdown, renders as a note or warning with a German label and a symbol. No new syntax, and no JavaScript in the exports. See *Callouts* below.
- **Status chips** — a code span that starts with a colour dot, a blank and a label, `` `🟢 Live` ``, renders as a small coloured label, in running text, in a table, in a heading. Five colours, each with a mark of its own shape and with its name as text for a screen reader, so the status does not hang on the colour. No new syntax, and no JavaScript in the exports. See *Status chips* below.
- **Cards and step lists** — a comment in the line above a list, `<!-- dokufix: cards -->` or `<!-- dokufix: steps -->`, turns a bullet list into a row of cards and a numbered list into a step list: a number tile per item and, where an item names one, who acts in it. A marker that cannot take effect becomes a warning at its place. No new syntax, and no JavaScript in the exports. See *Block markers, cards and step lists* below.
- **Tables** — every table stands in a wrapper that scrolls sideways on its own, so a wide table no longer widens the page. Emphasis directly after a line break in a cell is a subdued second line. And `<!-- dokufix: facets Spalte -->` above a table puts one control per distinct value of that column over it; choosing one hides the other rows, by fixed rules of the document styles. `<!-- dokufix: filter "Suchen …" -->` gives a table a search field with a row counter where a script runs: in the editor, in `Mit Editor`, `schlank` and `kompakt`; `nur-lesen` shows the complete table without it. No new syntax, and no JavaScript in `nur-lesen`. See *Tables* below.
- **Licence information** — every variant has a small text link "license information" above the document. It opens a view that names what the file loads or carries of others' work: `marked`, `marked-footnote`, Mermaid, `bpmn-js` and the Octicons, each with version, licence and copyright line, and the texts of the MIT licence and of the bpmn.io licence. A native `<details>`, so it opens in the JS-free export. See *Licence information* below.
- **Search in the reading view** — in read mode, in the editor and in a `Mit Editor` file, and in the read-only exports `schlank` and `kompakt`, the key `/` or a click on the magnifier at the top right opens a panel on the right that lists every paragraph, list item and heading holding a term, each with a part of its text and the term marked, grouped under the headings of its sections, which stay in view while the list scrolls, under a line such as "20 Treffer an 15 Stellen in 7 Abschnitten". Two switches make case count and ignore blanks, hyphens and dots, so `statuschip` finds "Status-Chip"; a term needs three letters or digits. While the panel is open every hit is highlighted in the text as well. A click scrolls to the place; the panel stays open. See *Search* below.
- **Heading numbering toggle** — opt-in 1.2.3 outline numbering via pure CSS counters.
- **Two-layer Table of Contents** — author-placed inline `[[toc]]` marker (renders as a static nested list inside the document, ships through every export variant) plus a JS-driven right-side scrollspy rail in read mode on wide viewports.
- **Dirty-state indicator** — a header badge (and a `●` prefix in the browser tab title) shows whether the editor content matches what is baked into the file. Resets to clean after a "Mit Editor" download.
- **In-file version history with per-version source snapshots** — each "Mit Editor" download and each "commit-only" action appends a `{v, t (ISO), m (message), s (gzipped source)}` entry to a JSON block embedded in the file (`<script type="application/json" id="dokufix-history">` — non-executing, just data). A clickable `v{N}` badge in the header opens a modal listing every saved version, newest first. The full source of any prior version is recoverable from the file alone — the artifact is auditable on its own. Read-only exports inherit a small `Version N · DD.MM.YYYY HH:MM · message · exportiert <date>` footer at the end of the document.
- **Per-document identity** — each saved file carries a UUID in its history JSON. The IndexedDB doc record is keyed by this UUID, so two dokufix files in the same browser no longer share storage. Files without a UUID (older or never-saved) use a deterministic `loc-{hash}` fallback derived from `location.origin + location.pathname` (URL hash and query are deliberately excluded so ToC anchor clicks don't relocate the storage on the next reload); on first save the fallback upgrades to a real `crypto.randomUUID()` and the IndexedDB record is rekeyed to the new identifier.
- **Mobile-friendly** — hamburger menu collapses the editor toolbar on narrow viewports.

## Download variants

| Variant | What's in the file | Receiver can re-edit? | JS required to open? | Size (demo text) | Size (reference document) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Mit Editor** | Full editor + gzipped Markdown source + immutable demo-text reset capability | ✅ Yes | ✅ (via CDN libs) | 304 640 B + libs | 319 801 B + libs |
| **Ohne Editor — offen** (`-nur-lesen.html`) | Pre-rendered HTML + inline SVG diagrams with their sources as `data:` links, no JavaScript at all | ❌ No | ❌ | 488 420 B | 524 883 B |
| **Ohne Editor — schlank** (`-schlank.html`) | Plaintext HTML + the SVG of each diagram and its source link gzip-compressed individually, tiny inline decoder, the reader bundle (table filter, search, keys of the large view, picture download) | ❌ No | ⚠️ For diagrams, their downloads, search fields, the search and the keys of the large view only — text remains readable | 269 705 B | 233 057 B |
| **Ohne Editor — kompakt** (`-kompakt.html`) | Entire body gzip-compressed + tiny decoder, the reader bundle (table filter, search, keys of the large view, picture download) | ❌ No | ✅ | 203 127 B | 160 859 B |

Story 5.6 took the built file from 268 348 to 273 371 B (`8dac536`), and story 2.10 added 8 468 B to that (273 371 → 281 839 B): 7 013 B of script, styles and reader bundle, and 1 455 B of demo text (see *Diagrams*, what story 2.10 did to documents); the sentences below describe the file before story 5.6. The built file is 134 995 B larger than the PoC and a `Mit Editor` file of the demo text 122 624 B: the photo of the demo text is 60 264 B of its asset block, and without it the built file would be 74 731 B larger than the PoC (71 774 B after stories 5.4 and 5.5, 62 585 B after story 2.9, 54 353 B after story 2.8 and stories 5.1 to 5.3, 7 930 B after story 2.7, before epic 5 and story 2.8); before story 2.7 it was 12 981 B smaller, nearly all of it the minifying of script and styles. The PoC's figures for the reference document are of a run on 2026-10-03 (`tests/out/27-poc`), before story 2.8 gave the document its section with BPMN without coordinates; the PoC shows its BPMN blocks as code. In the demo column the two files no longer export the same text: with story 2.3 the demo text of the built file got a section on status chips, with story 2.4 sections on callouts, cards and a step list, with story 2.5 a section on tables, with story 2.6 a search field over that section's facet table, every variant of its display features, special cases and a photo, with story 2.7 a section on BPMN and a special case, with story 2.8 a section with laid-out diagrams and laid-out special cases, with stories 5.1 to 5.3 a paragraph on the search, with story 2.9 a sentence on the large view, with stories 5.4 and 5.5 two sentences more on the search, and then special cases of the large view and the search and a corrected keys table, none of which the PoC's has. The PoC's figures for the reference document are of the same run on the document as it was then; it shows the callouts as quotations, the chips as code spans, the lists and tables behind a marker as plain lists and tables, and the tables without wrappers, and it has no search field. These changes moved these figures after the build: the split into modules added 972 B to the file (see *The script's modules*), story 2.15 added 2 800 B, the pass runner, the warning and the export path, the close button of the storage banner with the German footnotes heading added 571 B, story 2.2 added 5 930 B, the callouts: 4 634 B of document styles, 2 790 B of them the five symbols, and 1 296 B of script, and story 2.18 added 5 871 B, the licence information: 1 750 B of editor styles and 4 121 B of script, which holds the list, the text of the MIT licence and the frame rules for the exports, story 2.3 added 2 626 B, the status chips: 1 694 B of document styles and 932 B of script, the section on status chips in the demo text added 903 B, the darker labels of the chips another 122 B of document styles, and story 2.4 added 5 485 B, the block markers with cards and step lists: 1 458 B of document styles and 4 027 B of script; the three sections its demo text got added another 1 907 B; and the rule that keeps the page behind the editor from scrolling added 74 B of editor styles (see *Footnotes*, in the editor); story 2.5 added 8 941 B, the tables with their wrapper, sub-line and facet filter: 4 916 B of document styles, 3 245 B of them the hiding rule for 32 values, and 4 025 B of script; of the whole, 273 B came from the two fixes of its review (189 B of styles, 84 B of script) and 1 632 B from raising `FACET_MAX` from 16 to 32; the section its demo text got added another 2 113 B; story 2.6 added 3 249 B, the free-text filter: 671 B of document styles and 2 578 B of script; the search field its demo text got, with a paragraph and a row, added another 602 B; and with the demo text's variants, special cases and photo story 2.6 added another 66 489 B: 60 264 B the photo in the asset block, 5 550 B the text, and 544 B of script, which bakes the images of the demo text into every saved file; and the search field in `schlank` and `kompakt` (Ben, 2026-10-03) added 4 022 B: 3 407 B the bundle of the filter in its data block with its comment, 463 B of script, and 152 B of demo text that says where the field works now; story 2.7 added 20 911 B: the figure of step A 756 B (744 B of script, 20 B of document styles), then 5 456 B of script (the BPMN module, the credit, the licence entry of `bpmn-js` with the text of its licence), 827 B of document styles, 317 B the fourth script tag with its comment, and 13 555 B the demo text's section on BPMN and its special case, two diagrams of 7 736 and 2 971 B of XML; story 5.1 added 6 944 B, the search panel; story 2.8 added 29 033 B, BPMN without coordinates: 20 256 B of script (the layout module, its browser part in `bpmn.js`, the guards for a page without Mermaid) and 8 777 B the demo text's new section and special cases, four laid-out diagrams; story 5.2 added 1 576 B, the search's switches and the minimum length of a term; story 5.3 added 8 870 B: 8 430 B the reader bundle in place of the filter's (3 173 → 11 603 B), 280 B of script (the decoder's event, the root and read mode handed to the search), 134 B of demo text and 40 B the longer comment of the block, less 14 B of editor styles, whose search rules lost `body.mode-view`; every `schlank` and `kompakt` file now carries the 11 603 B bundle, a file of a document with a filter table 8 430 B more than before, one without the whole of it, and `schlank` 63 B more decoder where it has diagrams; story 2.9 added 7 913 B, the large view of a diagram: 4 208 B of document styles, 2 370 B of script, 1 025 B more reader bundle in its data block and 310 B the demo text's sentence (the Escape of the search, `62aa82f`, had added 319 B before it, 247 970 → 248 289 B); story 5.4 added 5 180 B, the results grouped under their headings: 2 584 B in the reader bundle (12 758 → 15 342 B), 1 617 B of script, 819 B of editor styles (the group headings in `search.css`) and 160 B of demo text; every `schlank` and `kompakt` file carries the 1 848 B more; the demo text's special cases of the large view and the search, and the corrected keys table: 2 957 B. A read-only export of a document without callouts, chips, markers and tables is 16 729 B larger than the PoC's: 14 823 B of that is its stylesheet, and 1 906 B the licence element. The build took 96 B out, because an export embeds the document styles and esbuild minifies them a little further than `compactCss()` did (the export's stylesheet: 10 533 → 10 437 B); story 2.15 put 408 B in, the style of the warning (10 845 B), story 2.2 4 634 B, the styles of the callouts (15 479 B), story 2.18 1 016 B, the frame rules of the link "license information" (16 495 B), story 2.3 1 694 B, the rules of the status chips (18 189 B), the darker labels 122 B, a colour of its own for each mark (18 311 B), story 2.4 1 458 B, the rules of cards and step lists (19 769 B), story 2.5 4 916 B, the rules of tables and facets (24 687 B, measured as 19 771 B before it), and story 2.6 671 B, the rules of the free-text filter (25 358 B). Outside its `<style>` every read-only export of the demo text was the one the PoC writes as long as the two demo texts were the same, in both browsers, once the ids Mermaid generates are masked and apart from two things: the footnotes heading, which reads "Fußnoten" where the PoC has "Footnotes", and the licence element, which the PoC does not have. The ids differ because since story 2.15 diagrams are drawn one at a time. That held for the reference document as well as long as it had no callout (see *Callouts*, what they did to documents). With its five callouts, its status chips, its section on cards, step lists and markers and its sections on tables and on the free-text filter an export of it is another 13 660 B larger than the PoC's outside the stylesheet (11 588 B before the section on the free-text filter came, 4 051 B before the section on tables, 1 938 B before the one on markers): the PoC writes the callouts as quotations that begin with their marker, which made 167 B before the chips came, the chips as code spans, every list and table behind a block marker as a plain list or table with the comment in front of it, without classes, numbers, controls or warnings, and every table without its wrapper. The PoC's own figures and how story 2.1 moved them are in `poc/README.md`.

Footnote hover previews add a material amount to every variant — roughly +30 % on the read-only ones for a document with three short footnotes, because each footnote's text is duplicated inline. Measured figures per variant are under [Footnotes → Size cost](#footnotes).

## Architecture notes

### Demo text and the file's document

Two distinct concepts, deliberately separated. Each lives in a data block of the page, a `<script type="application/json">` that is not executed:

- **`#dokufix-demo`** — the immutable original demo text. The "Demo zurücksetzen" button always restores from this. A save carries it along and never changes what it says.
- **`#dokufix-source`** — this file's document: what gets loaded on first open if no IndexedDB record exists yet for this document UUID. Empty in the built file. On `Mit Editor` download it is written with the user's current content.

A block holds `{"text": "…"}` or `{"gz": "…"}` (gzip, then base64), and the init reads either. The build writes the demo text as `{"text": …}` from `src/demo.md`; a save writes both blocks as `{"gz": …}`.

The showcase, `dist/dokufix-showcase.html`, carries its own demo text: `src/showcase.md`, short, each core feature once, without special cases, for a newcomer. It is the same page with that text in `#dokufix-demo`, so "Demo zurücksetzen" in it restores the showcase text. `src/demo.md` stays the text of the regression checks, with every variant and special case. The two files do not share a draft: without a UUID baked into the file each keys its storage by its own location (see *Per-document identity* above).

Loading order on open: IndexedDB doc record (live draft) > `#dokufix-source` (file's baked content) > `#dokufix-demo`.

In the PoC both were variables of the script, `DEMO` and `SAMPLE`. Why they moved is under *Self-replication mechanism*.

### Render passes

`render()` in `src/app/render.js` is four steps: parse the Markdown into the preview, run the document passes, run the run-time passes, rebuild the rail. A pass is one step that works on the rendered document, `{ name, run(root, context) }`. `root` is the element that holds the document; `context` is `{ frontmatter }`, what was split off the source before it was parsed; `run` may be async. The two lists stand in `render.js`, in the order they run:

| List | Pass (`name`) | Function |
|---|---|---|
| `DOCUMENT_PASSES` | `Metadaten` | `injectFrontmatterPanel()` |
| | `Hinweise` | `buildCallouts()` |
| | `Status-Chips` | `buildChips()` |
| | `Markierungen` | `applyMarkers()` |
| | `Tabellen` | `buildTables()` |
| | `Überschriften` | `assignHeadingIds()` |
| | `Inhaltsverzeichnis` | `processInlineToc()` |
| | `Fußnoten-Vorschau` | `attachFootnotePreviews()` |
| | `Fußnoten-Rücksprung` | `linkFootnoteReturnPaths()` |
| | `Diagramme` | `renderDiagrams()`, in `src/app/diagrams.js` |
| `RUNTIME_PASSES` | `Sprungmarken im Inhaltsverzeichnis` | `attachTocClicks()` |
| | `Tabellenfilter` | `attachTableFilters()` |
| | `Diagramm-Bilder` | `attachSvgDownloads()`, in `src/app/diagram-downloads.js` |
| | `BPMN-Ansicht` | `attachLiveViewers()`, in `src/app/live-viewer.js` |

- **This is where a component plugs in.** It adds its pass to a list, at its place in the order. Nothing else in `render()` changes. See *Where a new component goes*.
- **Two lists, not one.** A document pass produces the document: what a reader sees, and what an export takes along when it copies the preview. A run-time pass attaches what only a running page has: a listener, the search field of a table (`Tabellenfilter`, see *Tables*), the picture button below a diagram (`Diagramm-Bilder`, see *Diagrams*), the live viewer in the large view of a BPMN diagram (`BPMN-Ansicht`, see *Diagrams*). The run-time passes run after every document pass: the search field finds its table in its wrapper, and a cell already holds the preview of a footnote cited in it. Nothing a run-time pass does is part of the document, and an element it adds is transient (below).
- **Each pass is contained.** `runPasses()` in `src/app/passes.js` runs a list. A pass that throws, or whose promise is rejected, is logged to the console, and the passes after it still run. When the list is through, each failed pass gets one warning at the top of the document that names it: "Der Schritt „Inhaltsverzeichnis“ ist fehlgeschlagen. Das Dokument kann unvollständig sein." What the pass had done before it failed stays. The warning of a failed run-time pass is marked transient: the document is complete, so no export carries it.
- **The rail is rebuilt at the end of every render, whatever failed before it.** The browser runs take "the rail was rewritten" as "the render is finished". Markdown that cannot be parsed puts a warning in place of the content, and the rail is then rebuilt empty. Until story 2.15 that case returned before the rail and showed a box that only the editor had a style for.
- **One render at a time.** A render requested while another runs starts when that one has finished, and reads the source then; the preview ends as the last one's. The promise `render()` returns is fulfilled when its render is done and is never rejected. Before, two renders interleaved on the preview: the first built its rail from headings that were no longer in the page, and its Mermaid run failed on detached nodes.
- **One diagram at a time.** `mermaid.initialize` sets `suppressErrorRendering`, so Mermaid throws instead of drawing its error picture into the block, which is what 12.0.0 does by default and what every export then carried. The pass draws each diagram on its own, Mermaid or BPMN; one that fails becomes a warning in its place, and the others are drawn. Running per diagram changes the ids Mermaid takes from the clock and nothing else. The pass comes after `Hinweise` and `Status-Chips`, because a diagram's title is the heading before it as the table of contents shows it (see *Diagrams*).
- **Which headings count** is said once: `documentHeadings()` in `src/app/toc.js`. Heading ids, the inline table of contents, the rail and the rail of an export ask there, each when it runs. A heading inside a callout does not count, which is why `Hinweise` runs before `Überschriften` (see *Callouts*).
- **Tables come directly after the block markers.** A marker finds its block as the element directly behind it (`judgeMarker()` compares the tag), and the wrapper every table gets would stand between a marker and its table. The facet filter has put its controls above its table by then, so the wrapper holds the table alone. Like everything that changes a document, the pass runs before the footnote previews, which copy a definition as it stands (see *Tables*).
- **What a heading is called** is said once as well: `headingLabelText()` in the same module. The anchor, the entry in the inline table of contents and the entries of both rails are made from it. It is the heading's text without two things that stand in the heading for other readers: the preview of a footnote, and the word a status chip carries for assistive technology. `Status-Chips` runs before `Überschriften`, so that a chip in a heading is a chip when its entry is written (see *Status chips*).

**The warning** (`buildWarning()` in `src/app/warning.js`, class `dokufix-warning`) is a document construct: its style is in `src/doc.css`, so it reads the same in the preview and in all four variants. It is recognisable without colour, because the word "Warnung:" stands in its markup. Its text is German; the detail below it is the message of whatever failed, as it came. Four things produce one: Markdown that cannot be parsed, a pass that failed, a diagram that cannot be drawn, and a block marker that cannot take effect (see *Block markers, cards and step lists*). The last is one line and has no detail: there is no message of something that failed, only a marker that names itself.

**Transient elements.** An element that exists only while the page runs carries the attribute `data-dokufix-transient`. `Mit Editor` removes every such element from its clone of the page, wherever it stands; the read-only exports remove them from their copy of the preview (`removeTransient()` in `src/app/transient.js`). A component that puts something into the page that is not the document marks it, and touches neither download. The search field of the free-text filter is such an element, and so are the search panel and its magnifier, the picture button below a diagram and the container and hint of the live viewer in the large view of a BPMN diagram. What cannot be marked has to be named in the clean-up of the clone. Today that is three things: `.mermaidTooltip`, which Mermaid appends to `<body>` with the first diagram it draws; `.bjs-powered-by-lightbox`, which `bpmn-js` appends to `<body>` when its logo in the live viewer is clicked; and the class `djs-cursor-*` that `bpmn-js` puts on `<body>` while the diagram is dragged. `removeRendererLeftovers()` in `src/app/diagrams.js` names them, so the save does not know which library drew a diagram; the live viewer takes the last two out by the same names when it closes (`removeViewerLeftovers()`). The host `bpmn-js` draws into is transient and gone after each diagram.

**The export path.** The three read-only exports get their content from one function, `buildExportBody()` in `src/app/downloads/export-body.js`. It renders, takes a detached copy of the preview, runs the export steps on the copy in order, and returns title, body and rail. A step is a function that gets the copy and changes it; the live preview is never touched. `EXPORT_STEPS` holds what every export needs: transient elements out, every row of a free-text filter shown (`showFilteredRows()` in `src/app/filter.js` takes the class `dokufix-filter-out` out), images as `data:` URLs. An export hands in what only it does: `schlank` gzips the SVG container of each diagram's figure and the source link below it (story 2.10), and `nur-lesen`, which has no script and so no search field, takes the mark of a filter table out (`removeFilterMarks()`, the attribute `data-dokufix-filter`); `schlank` and `kompakt` keep it, because the filter they run finds its tables by it. The export modules keep their templates and nothing else. Whatever a component has to strip or change when a document leaves the page is one step in that list, not a line in three files. A step that throws ends the export; a file with a step left out would be a wrong file that looks right. The menu catches whatever a download throws, logs it, and tells the user in a dialog that the download failed and why; the buttons are enabled again.

**What story 2.15 did to documents** is under *The script's modules*.

### Document styles (one source)

Every style that travels with a document is written in one place: `src/doc.css`. The build puts it, minified, into `<style id="dokufix-doc-css">`, the first stylesheet in `<head>`. From there on nothing changed against the PoC:

- The **preview** uses the block as an ordinary stylesheet.
- **`Mit Editor`** clones the whole document, so the block travels unchanged.
- The three **read-only exports** embed the block's text at the moment the file is written: `readonlyCss()` reads it from the page, removes comments and whitespace (after the build there is hardly any left), and appends the export frame. Nothing is derived when the file is opened, so `nur-lesen` still contains no script.

Until story 2.1 each of these rules existed twice, written by hand: `#preview`-prefixed in the app stylesheet and unprefixed in a string called `READONLY_CSS`. Nothing compared the two, and in epic 1 the anchor positioning of the footnote preview sat in the first and was missing from the second, so every read-only export shipped without it.

**Where a rule goes.**

| The rule styles … | It goes into … | Written as … |
|---|---|---|
| rendered document content: headings, tables, footnotes, a new `dokufix-` component | `src/doc.css` | `.dokufix-doc h2{…}`, `.dokufix-doc .dokufix-callout{…}` |
| the look of the rail | the same file | `.dokufix-rail a{…}`, unscoped, because the rail sits beside the content container |
| the editor interface, the preview pane as a box, where the rail stands in the editor, the link "license information" in the editor | `src/app.css`, the app stylesheet below the block | `#preview{…}`, `body.mode-view #preview{…}`, `.dokufix-rail a.active{…}`, `.header-actions>.dokufix-licences{…}` |
| the search panel, in the editor and in `schlank` and `kompakt` | `src/search.css`, behind the app stylesheet and in the reader bundle (see *Search*) | `.search-panel{…}`, `.search-result{…}` |
| the page of a read-only export: reset, margins, footer, rail grid, the link "license information" | `READONLY_FRAME_CSS` in `src/app/downloads/export-body.js` | `body{…}`, `.reader-body{…}`, `.dokufix-meta{…}`, `.dokufix-licences{…}` |

`.dokufix-doc` is the class on the content container: `#preview` in the editor, `<main class="reader-body dokufix-doc">` in an export. That class is the only change to the exported markup.

**The check.**

```
node tests/check-doc-styles.mjs
```

No dependencies, no browser. It reads the sources, because that is where a rule is written: the block is `doc.css`, the app stylesheet is `app.css`, the search panel's is `search.css`, the export frame and the export functions are in the modules under `src/app/downloads/` (the check reads every `.js` under `src/` and names the module a problem is in), the block's element and the preview are in `index.html`. It exits 1 and names file, line and selector

- when a document rule sits outside `doc.css`. In the export frame that is any selector other than the nineteen the frame has today, which the check lists one by one (`FRAME_SELECTORS`): ten of the page and nine of the link "license information". `.reader-body h5{…}` fails like a bare `h5{…}`, and so does `.dokufix-licences h5{…}`. In `app.css`, in `search.css`, in a `<style>` an export writes itself and in a `<style>` somebody adds to the page it is a `.dokufix-doc` selector, a `#preview <descendant>` selector, a `dokufix-` name, or any class or attribute name that a selector of the block uses (`.footnotes li{…}`, `.dokufix-diagram-svg svg{…}`, `a[data-footnote-ref]{…}`);
- when a selector in `doc.css` does not start with `.dokufix-doc`, `.numbered .dokufix-doc` or `.dokufix-rail`; a pseudo-class of the container itself counts as its start (`.dokufix-doc:has(…)`, the lock of the preview pane under the large view of a diagram). One selector stands as it is, listed with its reason in `BLOCK_EXCEPTIONS`: `html:has(.dokufix-diagram-toggle:checked)`, the page that does not scroll behind an open large view (see *Diagrams*); another root rule, or that one with a descendant, fails;
- when a read-only export no longer builds its stylesheet with `readonlyCss()` or its `<main>` lost the class;
- when the block's element is missing from the page or doubled, or holds anything but the slot for `doc.css`, and when `READONLY_CSS` comes back;
- when the built file does not have exactly one block, or an empty one. That is all the check reads from the built file: there the script is bundled and minified, and the names it looks for are gone.

The `dokufix-` prefix alone is not the test. dokufix's own components carry it, but what `marked` and `marked-footnote` produce does not (`.footnotes`, `[data-footnote-ref]`), so the check takes the class and attribute names from the block's own selectors. Three short allowlists name what a frame may use anyway: the frame names `.dokufix-meta`, `.dokufix-rail-pending`, `.dokufix-licences` and `.dokufix-licences-view`, each listed, so that `.dokufix-licences-x` still fails; `.dokufix-rail` together with `.has-items` or `a.active`; and five selectors that share a name with the block but style something else (`.version-modal[open]`, and in the `<noscript>` style of `schlank` `.dokufix-diagram-svg[data-gz]` with its `::before`, and `.dokufix-diagram-downloads` with `.dokufix-diagram-downloads+.dokufix-diagram-credit`, which hide the packed downloads and set the credit back to its own line (story 2.10); they may use the block's `dokufix-` names because they stand only where scripts are off). The link has no rule for its open state: `.dokufix-licences[open]` would use an attribute the block uses and is not on that list. What the check cannot see: a rule that reaches document content through another ancestor and names none of these, such as `.pane-preview h5{…}`, and rules written with CSS nesting, which its reader does not unfold. Run it after every change to a style.

**Two things that are load-bearing.**

- *Order.* In `src/index.html` the block stands before the app stylesheet, and `readonlyCss()` puts the frame after the block. Where a frame rule and a document rule have the same specificity, the frame has to win: `.dokufix-meta p` against `.dokufix-doc p` in the exports, `.dokufix-rail a.active` against `.dokufix-rail .rail-h4 a` in the editor.
- *Specificity moved.* Document selectors dropped from id to class specificity in the preview and rose from element to class specificity in the exports. That was verified by screenshot, not assumed (below). One consequence needed a frame rule: the "needs JavaScript" notice of `kompakt` sits outside the content container, so `p{margin-bottom:1em}` no longer reached it and the rail beside it stood 17 px higher with JavaScript off; `noscript p{margin-bottom:1em}` in the frame restores that.

**What the change did to documents** (2026-10-02, `tests/vergleich.mjs`, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153, light and dark, 1400 and 1600 px, at rest and with every state switched on):

- Demo text, which has no image: all 68 screenshots are pixel-identical before and after.
- Reference document: `Mit Editor` and the preview pane are pixel-identical. The three read-only exports differ, and only by the decided image margin: the one source carries `margin:8px 0` on images, which the preview always had and the exports never did, so each of the two images in the reference document moves what follows down by 16 px. Control: the same build with that one declaration removed gives read-only exports that are pixel-identical to before.
- `object-fit:contain` on the missing-image placeholder was the other difference between the twins; it existed only in the exports and is now everywhere. It changes no pixel in the preview.
- With JavaScript switched off, the three read-only exports render as before, again apart from the image margin (checked in Chromium at 1400 and 1600 px).

### Libraries

Four libraries come from the CDN, each at a fixed version, each by a fixed script tag of the page:

| Library | Version | URL below `https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/` |
|---|---|---|
| `marked` | 18.0.14 | `marked@18.0.14/lib/marked.umd.js` |
| `marked-footnote` | 1.4.0 | `marked-footnote@1.4.0/dist/index.umd.min.js` |
| `mermaid` | 12.0.0 | `mermaid@12.0.0/dist/mermaid.min.js` |
| `bpmn-js` | 18.31.0 | `bpmn-js@18.31.0/dist/bpmn-navigated-viewer.production.min.js` |

**`bpmn-js`** (story 2.7) is the navigated viewer, the build with zoom and pan, global `BpmnJS`: 194 602 B, 54 624 B gzipped. It is loaded on every opening of an editor file, whether the document holds BPMN or not, and it is carried in no file: neither embedded in the built file nor loaded on demand (decision of 2026-10-02). It is used unmodified, and no read-only export carries it; they carry only the output of its export function (see *Diagrams*). The lint knows its global, and its licence is on the list of *Licence information*. Loading it costs the comparison run one thing: the library takes one value of `Math.random` when it loads, so the run's deterministic stand-in hands Mermaid other values, and Mermaid 12 draws the outline of a rounded node a few pixels otherwise (see *Diagrams*, what the story did to documents). In a browser `Math.random` is random anyway.

Until story 2.1 the URLs carried no version. That did not mean "latest": `marked/marked.min.js` resolved to 15.0.12 and stayed there, because `marked.min.js` is no longer shipped from version 16 on, while the Mermaid URL followed every release. A file saved as `Mit Editor` carries these URLs with it, so an unpinned file renders differently next year without anyone having touched it.

**Raising a version** is a change to the look of every document. Change the URL and the version of its entry in `src/app/licences.js`, then run `tests/vergleich.mjs` against the state before and attribute every difference (see *Checks*). `npm test` fails as long as URL and entry disagree, and names the entry (see *Licence information*).

**Mermaid is also the layout engine of BPMN without coordinates** (story 2.8, see *Diagrams*), and that layout depends on `swimlane-beta`, a beta keyword of Mermaid that may change between releases. So raising Mermaid means re-running the layout's regression check as well: the comparison run on `tests/referenz.md`, whose section "Übergabe an die Tourenplanung" is the reference input, in both browsers, with its counts and the properties of a clean drawing (see *Comparison run*). `MERMAID_LAYOUT_VERSION` in `src/app/bpmn-layout.js` names the version the layout and its checks are made with, `12.0.0`; `tests/licences.test.mjs` fails while the Mermaid URL of `src/index.html` names another, so the pin changes only together with that constant, and the constant only after the check.

**A page without Mermaid** (its script tag failed) runs on: `mermaid.initialize()` in `src/app.js` is called only when Mermaid is there. Each Mermaid diagram is then the warning "Ein Diagramm konnte nicht gezeichnet werden." with the detail "Die Bibliothek Mermaid wurde nicht geladen.", each BPMN diagram without coordinates the BPMN warning with "Die Bibliothek Mermaid wurde nicht geladen; sie ordnet BPMN ohne Koordinaten an.", and BPMN with coordinates is drawn. Before story 2.8 the whole script stopped at that call.

**Mermaid runs with `securityLevel: 'strict'`** instead of `'loose'`. Measured on 12.0.0 with a flowchart that uses everything `loose` allows:

| In the diagram source | `loose` | `strict` |
|---|---|---|
| `click X call fn()` — a click runs a JavaScript function of the page | runs | does nothing |
| `click X href "https://…"`, `"mailto:…"`, a relative address — a click follows an ordinary link | link | link |
| `click X href "javascript:…"` or `"data:…"` | link with that target | no target: the `href` is removed |
| HTML in a label (`<b>`, `<i>`, `<br>`, `<a>`, `<img>`) | rendered, sanitised | rendered, sanitised |
| `onerror=` and other event attributes in a label | removed | removed |

So `strict` removes two things: the JavaScript callback, and `javascript:` and `data:` link targets. Ordinary links stay, and nothing else that was measured changes. Only flowchart and sequence diagrams have been looked at under `strict`; other diagram types are unchecked (tracked in `_bmad-output/initiative-dokufix/deferred-work.md`).

**What the two changes did to documents** (2026-10-02, reference document and demo text, all four variants, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153): all 136 screenshots are pixel-identical before and after. `marked` 18.0.14 produces byte-identical HTML for both documents. `strict` changes one thing in the output: Mermaid's sanitiser trims the whitespace inside `class` attributes of the SVG (`class="node default  "` becomes `class="node default"`), which makes `nur-lesen` 22 B smaller for the reference document and 24 B for the demo text. With that whitespace normalised, the exports before and after are identical byte for byte. `Mit Editor` grows by the 547 B that the longer URLs and their comments add to the file itself.

### Diagrams

Every diagram stands in one figure, whatever draws it (`src/app/diagrams.js`, story 2.7), with the controls of its large view around the SVG container (story 2.9, below):

```html
<figure class="dokufix-diagram dokufix-diagram-bpmn" aria-label="Rückgabe" style="--dokufix-diagram-width:812px">
  <input type="checkbox" class="dokufix-diagram-toggle" id="dokufix-diagram-1-open" aria-label="Rückgabe groß anzeigen">
  <input type="radio" class="dokufix-diagram-zoom" name="dokufix-diagram-1-zoom" value="fit" id="dokufix-diagram-1-fit" checked>
  <input type="radio" class="dokufix-diagram-zoom" name="dokufix-diagram-1-zoom" value="100" id="dokufix-diagram-1-100">
  <!-- … 150, 200 -->
  <div class="dokufix-diagram-view">
    <div class="dokufix-diagram-bar">
      <span class="dokufix-diagram-name">Rückgabe</span>
      <span class="dokufix-diagram-steps" role="group" aria-label="Zoom">
        <label class="dokufix-diagram-step" for="dokufix-diagram-1-fit">Einpassen</label> <!-- … 100 %, 150 %, 200 % -->
      </span>
      <label class="dokufix-diagram-close" for="dokufix-diagram-1-open">Schließen</label>
    </div>
    <label class="dokufix-diagram-stage" for="dokufix-diagram-1-open">
      <div class="dokufix-diagram-svg"><svg role="img" aria-label="Rückgabe" …></svg></div>
    </label>
  </div>
  <div class="dokufix-diagram-downloads" role="group" aria-label="Herunterladen">
    <a download="Rückgabe.bpmn" href="data:application/xml;charset=utf-8,…" title="BPMN-XML herunterladen: Rückgabe.bpmn">.bpmn</a>
    <button type="button" data-dokufix-transient title="Bild herunterladen: Rückgabe.svg">.svg</button> <!-- where a script runs -->
  </div>
  <figcaption class="dokufix-diagram-credit">Gezeichnet mit <a href="https://bpmn.io">bpmn-js</a></figcaption>
</figure>
```

A Mermaid figure is the same with `dokufix-diagram-mermaid`, a link to `.mmd`, and without the credit.

- **The title** is the label of the last heading of the document before the block, at any level, as the table of contents shows it (`headingLabelText()`), or "Diagramm" where none comes before it. A heading inside a callout is no heading of the document, so it does not count. The title is the figure's accessible name; in the column it is not shown, the bar of the large view shows it. The files of the downloads are named after it (below).
- **Whatever handles diagrams goes by the figure** and its SVG container: the document styles (`.dokufix-diagram` keeps the 24 px margin and the centring a Mermaid diagram had, in place of a figure's `1em 40px`), `schlank`, which gzips the SVG container and nothing around it, the save, and the checks. Only `diagrams.js` knows which library drew a diagram; Mermaid's class `mermaid` is no longer set.
- **A kind** is a fenced block's language, `mermaid` or `bpmn`, with a renderer that draws into the SVG container or throws, the text of its warning, and, for BPMN, the credit below the diagram. All blocks become figures first; then the diagrams are drawn one at a time, in the order of the document. One that throws becomes the warning, without any control of a large view, and the others are drawn.

**The large view** (story 2.9). In the column a diagram is an overview; its labels are about 7 px high. A click on it shows it over the whole window: a bar with its title, the zoom steps "Einpassen", "100 %", "150 %" and "200 %", and "Schließen". It opens at "Einpassen", the whole diagram in the window, in width and height, at most at 150 % of its width as drawn; the other steps draw it at 1, 1.5 and 2 times that width, and the view scrolls where it is larger. "Schließen" or a second click on the diagram closes it. The page behind does not scroll while it is open. Where the app script runs, a BPMN diagram shows its view in the live viewer of `bpmn-js` instead (story 2.11, below).

- *Document content, no script.* The controls are made by the pass `Diagramme` with the figure, so every variant carries them, and the document styles alone open, zoom and close the view: the checkbox and the four radio buttons stand before the view as its siblings, and `.dokufix-diagram-toggle:checked ~ .dokufix-diagram-view` is `position:fixed`, `inset:0`, `z-index` 100, above the rail (40), the search panel (45), "Editor ↩" (50) and the editor's toolbar and menus; `.dokufix-diagram-zoom[value="150"]:checked ~ .dokufix-diagram-view svg` gives a step. No `:has()` is needed for that, no `<style>` is generated, the SVG stays once in the file, and `nur-lesen` stays without `<script>`. The ids are `dokufix-diagram-<n>-open`, `-fit`, `-100`, `-150`, `-200`, `n` the diagram's place in the document, as `diagram.index`.
- *The width as drawn.* After a diagram is drawn the pass writes its width onto the figure, `style="--dokufix-diagram-width:812px"`: the SVG's `max-width`, which Mermaid and `finishBpmnSvg()` both write, else the width of its viewBox (`drawnWidth()`). The steps multiply it. That inline `max-width` of the SVG is why the rules of the view set theirs with `!important`.
- *In the column* the view is a plain box, its bar not shown, and the stage a label around the SVG container, `cursor:zoom-in`. Neither the figure nor anything around the diagram is positioned: a footnote preview is placed against the page. The controls are boxes of no size and no content. A click on a label focuses its control, and a browser scrolls a focused control into view, the page behind an open view too; so the checkbox is `position:sticky` at the middle of the window (`top:50%`) while its figure reaches past it, and a click anywhere on the diagram, its top above the window or not, finds it in view and scrolls nothing, the page or the preview pane. Not at the top: Firefox scrolls to a box of no size on the very edge of the window. Tab, to a figure out of view, still scrolls the diagram in. While the view is open the checkbox and the radio buttons stand `position:fixed` in the window, so a click on a step or on "Schließen" scrolls nothing either; closed, the radio buttons are not shown. Closed, the column is pixel for pixel what it was (below).
- *The page behind.* `html:has(.dokufix-diagram-toggle:checked){overflow:hidden}`: in read mode and in the exports the page scrolls, and only its root can stop that. It is the one rule of `src/doc.css` that does not start with `.dokufix-doc`, and the style check names it (`BLOCK_EXCEPTIONS`). In edit mode the preview pane scrolls, which is the content container itself: `.dokufix-doc:has(.dokufix-diagram-toggle:checked){overflow:hidden !important}`, against the editor's `#preview{overflow:auto}`. In edit mode the view lies over the toolbar too.
- *Keyboard.* Tab reaches the checkbox; its focus ring stands on the diagram. Space opens the view, and the ring moves to "Schließen", because Space on the checkbox closes it. Tab goes on to the steps, a group of radio buttons, and the arrows choose a step, the ring on its label. Where a script runs, the page in the editor and in `Mit Editor` and the reader bundle in `schlank` and `kompakt`, three keys work while a view is open: Escape closes it and nothing else, the first step in the order of Escape (`largeViewStep` of `src/app/large-view.js`, run by `src/app/escape.js`, see *Keys and events*); `+` and `-` choose the next and the previous step, held at the ends, and not with Ctrl, Alt or Meta; where the focus is on a step it moves to the step chosen, so an arrow goes on from there; `/` opens no search over it, in either mode, and is held back from the browser. `nur-lesen` has the mouse, Tab, Space and the arrows.
- *What travels.* `checked` is an attribute of "Einpassen" alone; clicks and keys set the property, never the attribute. A file written while a view is open, saved as `Mit Editor` or exported, opens closed, at "Einpassen". A render replaces the figure: Ctrl+Enter or the render of a read-only export closes the view, and the page scrolls again. In print there is no control, and the diagram stands in the column (the rules of the open view are inside `@media not print`).
- *`schlank` with scripts off* opens the view all the same, with the notice "[Diagramm — JavaScript erforderlich, um es anzuzeigen]" in place of the packed diagram. The export packs the SVG container wherever it stands in the figure (`.dokufix-diagram .dokufix-diagram-svg`).
- *Limits.* No focus trap: Tab leaves the open view for the page behind it, the credit link of a BPMN diagram and the controls of the next diagram (which opens its view over this one; Escape closes the last one). A view keeps its step while the page runs: closed at "150 %" and opened again, it opens at "150 %", in every variant, since the styles cannot set a radio button back; only a render or a new opening of the file starts at "Einpassen". The stage is a label: a click on it closes the view, and a link the author put into a Mermaid diagram is followed, not a close; in the live viewer of a BPMN diagram (below) a click moves nothing and closes nothing. Safari has not been opened: WebKit does not start on this machine (`deferred-work.md`).

**What story 2.9 did to documents** (2026-10-03, `npm run pruefung` with `tests/vergleich.mjs --compare --strict` against the built file before the story, `62aa82f`, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153, runs in `tests/out/29/final/` against `tests/out/29/vorher` and `tests/out/29/vorher-demo`). Reference document: all 34 screenshots per browser are pixel-identical, the large view closed, and every assertion is green. Demo text: the 34 screenshots per browser differ by the one new sentence on the large view below the first diagram, which moves what follows it down by about 100 px; control, the new sources built with the demo text as it was (`tests/out/29/dokufix-alte-demo.html`): all 34 per browser pixel-identical. The comparison run on the file before the story fails on the controls it now expects (12 assertions per browser), and the pictures of the open view (`grossansicht/`) have no baseline there and are not compared. `npm run pruefung`: reference document 647 and 642 assertions green (Chromium, Firefox), demo text 682 and 677, save round trip 150, failure cases 848. The built file grows by 7 913 B (248 289 → 256 202 B): 4 208 B of document styles, 2 370 B of script (the controls, the width, the keys), 1 025 B more reader bundle in its data block (11 733 → 12 758 B, 4 743 → 5 033 B gzipped), and 310 B the demo text's sentence. A read-only export carries the 4 208 B of styles and about 1 330 B of controls per diagram:

| Variant, Chromium | Reference document, 6 diagrams | Demo text, 8 diagrams |
|---|---|---|
| `Mit Editor` | 252 033 → 259 800 B (+7 767) | 237 876 → 245 807 B (+7 931) |
| `nur-lesen` | 286 520 → 298 720 B (+12 200) | 276 056 → 291 354 B (+15 298) |
| `schlank` | 143 389 → 156 614 B (+13 225) | 173 763 → 190 086 B (+16 323) |
| `kompakt` | 106 021 → 113 026 B (+7 005) | 147 236 → 153 885 B (+6 649) |

Firefox differs by the same few hundred bytes as before (see *Download variants*).

**The live viewer** (story 2.11, `src/app/live-viewer.js`). Where the app script runs, the editor and every `Mit Editor` file, opening the large view of a BPMN diagram starts the navigated viewer of `bpmn-js` in place of the picture, in either mode: Ctrl+wheel (Cmd+wheel) zooms at the pointer, between 0.2 and 4 times; the wheel alone and a drag with the left button move the diagram, and so do Ctrl+arrow keys once the canvas has the focus (a click gives it); on a touch screen, where `bpmn-js` hears nothing, one finger moves the diagram and two fingers zoom it at their middle, between 0.2 and 4 times as the wheel (story 2.23: `touchStep()`, listeners of dokufix on the container, which carries `touch-action:none`, so the browser neither scrolls nor zooms the page under it). The fingers are all touches of `e.touches` that began in the container, paired with those of the move before by their identifier (`countedFingers()`): `e.targetTouches` holds only the touches on the element the event's touch began on, so two fingers that begin on two shapes would be two lists of one finger each, and the diagram would jump. The two fingers that count stay the two while they touch, whatever the order of the list; a third finger changes nothing; a finger more or less below two, or one of the two lifted, starts the move afresh, so the diagram does not jump. Two fingers on one point only move it; the logo of bpmn.io stands at the bottom right of the viewer, fully visible and above everything else of the view. The bar shows the hint "Strg + Mausrad: zoomen · Ziehen: verschieben" (Ben, 2026-10-03) before "Schließen"; in a narrow window it shrinks and is cut off with an ellipsis, and below 520 px it is not shown, so "Schließen" always stays in the window. The zoom steps set the viewer's scale: "Einpassen" the whole diagram with 24 px around it, at most at 1.5, centred; "100 %", "150 %" and "200 %" the scales 1, 1.5 and 2, a diagram that fits centred, one that does not from its top left with 20 px, where a process begins. The wheel does not move the radio button: the step chosen last stays marked, and choosing a step, the same one too, applies it again. `+`, `-` and Escape work as in the static view.

```html
<div class="dokufix-diagram-view">
  <div class="dokufix-diagram-bar">
    … <span class="dokufix-diagram-hint" data-dokufix-transient>Strg + Mausrad: zoomen · Ziehen: verschieben</span> <label class="dokufix-diagram-close" …>Schließen</label>
  </div>
  <div class="dokufix-diagram-live" data-dokufix-transient> … what bpmn-js puts into its container, the logo included </div>
  <label class="dokufix-diagram-stage" …> … the picture, hidden while the container stands before it </label>
</div>
```

- *What it draws.* The XML of the figure's source link (story 2.10), decoded from its `data:` URL (`sourceXml()`): the XML the picture was drawn from, kept once in the figure, not a second time. The viewer gets the configuration of the picture (`BPMN_VIEWER_CONFIG`), and every element the classes of its type (`addBpmnTypeClasses()`, the one loop the picture uses too), so it has the colours of the picture, from the same rules of `src/doc.css` (light scheme only). Where the author's XML had no coordinates, the link holds the laid-out XML, whose diagram part dokufix put after the author's, possibly empty, one; the viewer opens the first `BPMNDiagram` that holds a `BPMNShape` (`diagramToOpen()`), so the laid-out diagram, never an empty one.
- *Where it stands.* A container `div.dokufix-diagram-live` before the stage, in the view: the stage is a label of the checkbox, and a click inside it would close the view, so the viewer stands outside it, and the document styles hide the stage behind it (`.dokufix-diagram-live + .dokufix-diagram-stage{display:none}`, inside `@media not print`). The container fills the stage's place, the rest of the window below the bar, a definite height for `bpmn-js`; it is not positioned itself. Its canvas takes the focus at a click, without a ring. No CSS of the library is loaded (`diagram-js.css`, `bpmn-js.css`), and the library is used unmodified; it injects no `<style>`. In print the container is not shown and the picture stands in the column.
- *How it follows the view.* The run-time pass `BPMN-Ansicht` (`attachLiveViewers()`) listens to `change` on the checkbox of every BPMN figure and to `change` and `click` on its radio buttons. Every way of changing them fires `change`: a click, Space and the arrows through the browser, Escape and `+` and `-` because `src/app/large-view.js` dispatches it after setting the property (see *Keys and events*). Opened, it makes the container, starts `new BpmnJS({ container, …BPMN_VIEWER_CONFIG })`, imports the XML, sets the classes and the chosen step, and puts the hint into the bar. Closed, in any way, it destroys the viewer, takes container and hint out, and removes by name what `bpmn-js` puts outside its container and its `destroy()` leaves: the lightbox its logo opens in `<body>` (`.bjs-powered-by-lightbox`) and its cursor class on `<body>` (`djs-cursor-*`). A view closed while the viewer is still importing destroys the viewer when the import ends. Every render destroys a viewer still running, with its lightbox and cursor class: `render()` calls `stopLiveViewers()` first, before it parses, so a render that ends early (Markdown that cannot be parsed) does too, and the pass calls it again before it follows the new figures. A read-only download renders, so it destroys the viewer too.
- *What travels.* Nothing. The container and the hint are transient, the preview of a `Mit Editor` file is emptied, and the save names the lightbox and the cursor class (`removeRendererLeftovers()`), so a file saved with the viewer open, its lightbox open and a drag going on, is the built file but for the same listed differences every saved file has (`tests/speichern.mjs`, step 9, compares it with the built file, as it does every other save). The read-only exports carry no trace of it (`tests/vergleich.mjs`).
- *When it cannot start.* No `BpmnJS` in the page (its script tag failed, or the library was taken away after the render), a constructor that throws, an import that fails: the container goes, and the view is the static view of story 2.9, the picture with its zoom steps. One line on the console, "BPMN viewer failed, the large view shows the picture: …"; no warning for the reader (`tests/durchlaeufe.mjs`, case 14). Import warnings of the viewer go to the console as "BPMN viewer, import warning: …".
- *Where it does not run.* In `nur-lesen`, `schlank` and `kompakt`: the reader bundle carries nothing of it, and no read-only file carries `bpmn-js`; a BPMN diagram opens in the static view there, and so it does with scripts off. A Mermaid diagram keeps the static view everywhere.
- *Design notes, chosen by the planner, each Ben's to change.* A click on the diagram no longer closes the live view; "Schließen", Escape and Space on the checkbox do. The wheel does not move the radio button. One event for every way of changing a control. The XML comes from the link, not from a copy in the figure.
- *Limits.* Nothing can be edited. A hover or a click on an element marks nothing (seen in Chromium 153): the library marks them by the CSS that is not loaded. The canvas has no focus ring. Tab leaves the view as before. Safari has not been opened (see *the large view*, limits).

**What the live viewer costs** (2026-10-03, `npm run pruefung` in `tests/out/211-v2/`, Chromium 153). Of the viewer itself no file carries anything at run time: no container, no hint, nothing of `bpmn-js`. What the story adds to the files is code and rules: the built file and every `Mit Editor` file carry the script, every file the document styles, `schlank` and `kompakt` the reader bundle. The built file grows by 4 534 B (281 839 → 286 373 B): 3 470 B of script (the module, the pass, the class loop as a helper, the `change` of the keys), 696 B of document styles (the container, the stage behind it, the hint, the rules of the steps narrowed to the SVG container), 126 B more reader bundle in its data block (20 526 → 20 652 B, the `change` the keys dispatch there too) and 242 B of demo text. A `Mit Editor` file grows by the script, the styles and, for the demo text, its sentence; a read-only file by the 696 B of styles, `schlank` and `kompakt` by the 126 B of the bundle as well. The library is the one story 2.7 loads: the navigated viewer, 194 602 B, which was chosen over the viewer that only renders (185 955 B) for this story; the 8 647 B of its zoom and pan are used now, and the script that uses them is 3 470 B.

| Variant, Chromium | Reference document | Demo text |
|---|---|---|
| `Mit Editor` | 282 809 → 287 205 B (+4 396) | 270 628 → 275 128 B (+4 500) |
| `nur-lesen` | 377 183 → 377 879 B (+696) | 392 646 → 393 584 B (+938) |
| `schlank` | 191 426 → 192 248 B (+822) | 236 565 → 237 629 B (+1 064) |
| `kompakt` | 136 197 → 137 019 B (+822) | 183 105 → 184 039 B (+934) |

Firefox differs by the same few hundred bytes as before (see *Download variants*).

**What story 2.11 did to documents** (2026-10-03, `npm run pruefung -- --out tests/out/211-v2 --control altdemo=…`, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153). The standard runs are green: reference document 782 and 777 assertions (Chromium, Firefox), demo text 803 and 798, save round trip 166, failure cases 910; `npm test` 584. The check of the scale of "Einpassen" allows 0.005: measured twice, it came out 0.836 and 0.835 in an earlier run, since the box of the drawing is measured anew each time. The demo text as it was before the story, on a build of the new sources (`tests/out/211/dokufix-alte-demo.html`), against the built file of `5538af3` with `--strict` (baseline `tests/out/211-alt-demo`): every assertion green, and all 38 screenshots per browser pixel-identical, those of the open large view (`grossansicht/`) included, which show the first diagram, a Mermaid one: the viewer starts only when a BPMN diagram is opened, and the rules of the steps, narrowed to the SVG container, draw the static view as before. The run on the built file of `5538af3` fails on the checks of the viewer, the first of them in the editor and in `Mit Editor` (2 per browser; the rest cannot run without a viewer), which shows they see it. Controls: on a build whose save does not name the leftovers of `bpmn-js` (`tests/out/211/dokufix-ohne-reste.html`), the save round trip fails step 9 in Chromium, the saved file carrying `djs-cursor-grab` on `<body>` and the lightbox. Ben's test by hand with a real mouse and keyboard in Chromium and Firefox (AC7) is open.

**The downloads** (story 2.10, `src/app/diagram-downloads.js`). Below every drawn diagram stand two small buttons, centred, in the credit's 12 px and `#6e6e73`, each with a download symbol and the extension of its file: `[⤓ .mmd] [⤓ .svg]` below a Mermaid diagram, `[⤓ .bpmn] [⤓ .svg]  Gezeichnet mit bpmn-js` below a BPMN diagram (Ben, 2026-10-03: a symbol instead of a word, the extension with its dot, each a small button; below a BPMN diagram the downloads and the credit in one line). In the markup the line of the downloads stands between the view and the credit, whose markup stays as story 2.7 wrote it; the document styles make both inline boxes, centred together and vertically aligned, so the credit stands to the right of the buttons and wraps below them only where the column is too narrow for both. In print, without the downloads, the credit stands alone, centred. The line is a group named "Herunterladen"; each button names its file in its `title`. The symbol is the Octicon `download` (16 px, `@primer/octicons` 19.38.0), its path data as a `data:` URI on the `::before` of each button, drawn at 12 px, as the callouts carry theirs; the Octicons are on the licence list already. In print there is no line.

- *The source* is a link, `<a download="Rückgabe.bpmn" href="data:…">`, document content: the pass `Diagramme` writes it after the diagram is drawn, so it reaches all four variants and works without a script, in `nur-lesen` too. In `schlank` it is packed like the diagram (Ben, 2026-10-03): the export gzips its `href` into `data-gz-href` (base64) and takes the `href` out, and the decoder that unpacks the diagrams sets it back. So a `schlank` file carries no source as text; that is the room the Markdown of the whole document will take, which epic 3, entry 11, puts into a file. With scripts off `schlank` offers no download, as it shows no diagram: its `noscript` style hides the line, and the credit of a BPMN diagram stands alone, centred. `.mmd` is the block's text as written (as marked hands it on, with the line feed at the end), `text/plain;charset=utf-8`; `.bpmn` is the XML `bpmn-js` drew, `diagram.xml`: the author's XML where it had coordinates, the laid-out XML of story 2.8 where it had none, `application/xml;charset=utf-8`. A diagram that becomes a warning has no line.
- *The encoding* (`sourceDataUrl()`): the text as UTF-8, percent-encoded only where it has to be: `%`, `#`, the characters an attribute or the HTML around it would escape (`<`, `>`, `"`, `&`, `'`), tab, line feeds and the other control characters, which a URL parser drops, every character beyond ASCII, and blanks at the very end, which a URL parser strips. So serialising the attribute escapes nothing, and the file a browser saves is the text byte for byte. Measured on the spike's BPMN files: base64 is a third larger than the text, `encodeURIComponent()` more than half, this encoding about a quarter (12 217 → 15 299 B for `uebergabe.bpmn`).
- *The picture* is a `<button type="button">` that a run-time pass adds to the line, `Diagramm-Bilder` in the page (the editor, a `Mit Editor` file) and the reader bundle in `schlank` and `kompakt` (`attachSvgDownloads()`). It is transient: no download takes it along, and `nur-lesen` has none. A click makes the file from the SVG that stands in the figure at that moment (`pictureOf()`), so the SVG is in no file twice; in `schlank` the decoder fills the container after the reader bundle ran, and the button reads it at the click. The copy gets every `var(--…)` replaced by what the live element computes, so a BPMN picture opened alone has the colours it has in the document and needs no rule of `src/doc.css`; it gets `width` and `height` from its viewBox in place of `width="100%"` and the `max-width` of the column, an XML declaration, and goes to the browser as a Blob of type `image/svg+xml`. Where the figure has no SVG, as in `schlank` when a diagram could not be unpacked, nothing is saved and the console says "SVG download: no SVG in the figure". A Mermaid picture keeps its `<style>` and its HTML labels (`foreignObject`), which a browser shows; another program may not.
- *Names.* The title of the diagram, sanitised as the name of a download is (`safeFilenameBase()`: `<>:"/\|?*` and control characters become `-`, blanks `-`, at most 80 characters), and `%` becomes `-` as well, which Firefox saves as `_` and Chromium keeps, "Diagramm" where no heading comes before it. Two diagrams with the same title, compared without regard to case, get `-2`, `-3` in the order of the document: `Ablauf.mmd`, `Ablauf-2.bpmn`. All diagrams of the document count, those that become warnings too, so a name does not change with whether another diagram is drawn. Picture and source of one diagram share their name: `Ablauf-2.bpmn`, `Ablauf-2.svg`.
- *Not in the view, not in the search.* The line stands outside the stage label and outside the view, so a click on it opens no view, and with a view open it lies beneath it. The search reads of a figure only the `svg` in its SVG container (`diagramReading()` in `search-places.js`, story 5.7, see *Search*, hits in diagrams), so it reads neither the line nor the file names. The button has no key of its own (see *Keys and events*).
- *What a variant offers.* The source in all four: as a plain link in the editor, `Mit Editor` and `nur-lesen`, inside the packed body in `kompakt`, packed on its own in `schlank`, where it works once the decoder has run. The picture in the editor, `Mit Editor`, `schlank` and `kompakt`; not in `nur-lesen`, which stays without `<script>`. `schlank` with scripts off offers neither.

**What the downloads cost.** A source link is the minimal encoding of its text plus about 230 B of markup: in the `nur-lesen` file of the reference document (2026-10-03, Chromium 153, `tests/out/210-v4/`) the links of its two Mermaid diagrams of story 2.7 are 386 and 414 B for 157 and 179 B of text, those of its BPMN diagrams 15 435 B for 11 952 B of XML ("Rückgabe am Automaten", with coordinates), 5 942 for 4 479 B, 14 222 for 11 087 B ("Übergabe an die Tourenplanung", laid out, the computed diagram part included) and 3 573 for 2 646 B; the encoding adds 14 to 17 % to Mermaid text, 25 to 29 % to BPMN XML, and 35 % to a text full of `#`, `%`, `&` and umlauts. All nine links of the document are 43 061 B for 32 408 B of text, the eleven of the demo text 37 021 B for 27 210 B. `nur-lesen` carries them as they are; `schlank` gzips each `href` on its own and carries it in base64 (Ben, 2026-10-03), so a link there is about the size of its gzipped text plus a third; `kompakt` gzips them with the body; a `Mit Editor` file carries none, since it renders anew when it opens. The picture costs no byte in any file. The built file grows by 8 468 B, against `8dac536`, after story 5.6 (273 371 → 281 839 B): 1 525 B of document styles (the line and its symbol), 1 873 B more reader bundle in its data block (18 653 → 20 526 B, 6 938 → 7 692 B gzipped), 3 615 B of script (554 B of it the packing of the source links in `schlank`, its decoder and its `noscript` rules), and 1 455 B of demo text.

**What story 2.10 did to documents** (2026-10-03, `npm run pruefung` in `tests/out/210-v3/`, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153, with the source packed in `schlank`, on the sources before story 5.6, built on `687d3a2`). The standard runs are green: reference document 741 and 736 assertions (Chromium, Firefox), demo text 756 and 751, save round trip 162, failure cases 900. On top of story 5.6 (`8dac536`, `tests/out/210-v4/`) they are green as well: reference document 756 and 751, demo text 777 and 772, save round trip 162, failure cases 900; nothing had to change for the two to work together. The demo text as it was before the story, on a build of the new sources (`tests/out/210/dokufix-alte-demo.html`), against the built file of `687d3a2` with `--strict` (baseline `tests/out/210-alt-demo`): all assertions green, and 34 of 38 screenshots per browser differ, every one from the first line of downloads on (row 2148 at 1400 px), each page 156 px longer: the line below each of the four Mermaid diagrams, and below each of the six BPMN diagrams the line with the credit beside it, a few pixels higher than the credit alone; the 4 identical ones are the pictures of the open large view, which covers the line. Control, on the build before the packing in `schlank` (`tests/out/210-v2/`, same screenshots): the same build with the line hidden and the credit set back to its block of story 2.7 by two rules more (`tests/out/210/dokufix-alte-demo-ohne-zeile.html`): all 38 screenshots per browser pixel-identical to the baseline, so every difference is the line; the comparison run fails there on every check of the downloads that needs the line to be seen (54 assertions in both browsers), which shows those checks see it. The files of that old demo text grow, Chromium: `Mit Editor` 257 709 → 264 722 B (+7 013, the script), `nur-lesen` 334 986 → 373 038 B (+38 052, the source links), `schlank` 209 283 → 225 193 B (+15 910, the packed links and the bigger reader bundle; with the links as plain text it was 249 208 B, `210-v2`), `kompakt` 166 657 → 178 707 B (+12 050). Firefox differs by the same few hundred bytes as before.

**The files elsewhere** (AC7). Read back on 2026-10-03 in Chromium 153 from files downloaded in the editor (`tests/out/210/fuer-ben/`): the `.bpmn` of story 2.7 ("Prozessmodell (BPMN)") and of story 2.8 ("Prozessmodell ohne Koordinaten", laid out) each imported anew by `bpmn-js` with no warning, 37 and 26 elements; the `.mmd` of the special case with `#`, `%`, `&` and umlauts parsed by Mermaid as `flowchart-v2`; each `.svg` opened alone renders, in the comparison run in both browsers. Opening the `.bpmn` in the Camunda Modeler, the `.mmd` in a Mermaid editor and the `.svg` in a browser by hand is Ben's; not done yet.

**BPMN.** A fenced block with the language `bpmn` holds BPMN 2.0 XML with its diagram part (BPMN-DI), as a modeler such as the Camunda Modeler writes it, or without it (*BPMN without coordinates*, below). `src/app/bpmn.js` draws it with `bpmn-js`:

1. The library has to be there and the XML has to parse. Otherwise the diagram is the warning "Das Diagramm „Rückgabe“ konnte nicht gezeichnet werden." with the reason as its detail: the library's message for XML that does not parse, "Die Bibliothek bpmn-js wurde nicht geladen." in a page whose script tag failed. XML without a `BPMNShape` is laid out first, or refused with the layout's reason (below). The rest of the document renders, Mermaid included. The failure is a line on the console as well.
2. The viewer draws into a host of its own: a `<div>` in `<body>`, outside the document, `position:fixed` 10 000 px left of the window, so that it neither shows nor makes the page larger, marked transient and `aria-hidden`. After each diagram the viewer is destroyed and the host removed, whether the diagram was drawn or not.
3. Each element gets a class by its type before the export: `dokufix-bpmn-pool`, `-lane`, `-task` (with `-usertask`, `-servicetask` …), `-callactivity`, `-subprocess`, `-event` (with `-startevent`, `-endevent` …), `-gateway` (with `-exclusivegateway` …), `-sequenceflow`, `-messageflow`.
4. `saveSVG()`, the viewer's export function, gives the SVG. It is parsed, which drops the comment and doctype bpmn-js writes in front of it, and finished (`finishBpmnSvg()`): the hit areas the viewer uses for the mouse go, the SVG gets `role="img"` and the title as its name, `width="100%"`, no height and its drawn width as `max-width`, as Mermaid writes its SVG; every id gets the prefix `dokufix-bpmn-<n>-`, `n` the diagram's place in the document, and so does every reference to it, so that two diagrams never share an id. An SVG with a `foreignObject` is refused; `bpmn-js` writes none.
5. Import warnings of `bpmn-js` (an element it does not know) go to the console only; the diagram is drawn.

**BPMN without coordinates** (story 2.8, `src/app/bpmn-layout.js`). An author, or a language model writing the whole document, writes only the process: lanes, steps, flows. Coordinates written by guessing are how arrows end in mid-air. So where the XML has no `BPMNShape` (one in a comment or a CDATA section does not count), dokufix lays the process out itself. Mermaid is the layout engine, not the writing format and not what draws:

1. `readProcess()` reads the XML into one pool's lanes, flow nodes and sequence flows. It walks the elements and compares local names with their prefix taken off, `laneSet` for `<bpmn:laneSet>` or `<laneSet>`; it never looks up by namespace, which `linkedom` does not do as a browser does.
2. `mermaidSource()` writes that as Mermaid `swimlane-beta LR` text: a `subgraph` per lane, a node per flow node, an edge per flow. Every id is Mermaid's own, `n1…` for nodes and `l1…` for lanes, so ids such as `end`, `subgraph` or `style`, which break Mermaid's parser, never reach it; labels lose `"`, `<`, `>`, `&`, `#`, the backtick and the backslash. A process without lanes gets one lane for Mermaid that is not drawn, and so does a lane without an id; an empty lane gets a stand-in node, so that it keeps a row of its own. A node only an outer lane names stands in that lane's first inner lane.
3. Mermaid renders that text into the transient off-screen host BPMN uses (`mermaid.render(id, text, host)`), with a render id that is new for each layout and free of `-flowchart-`, by which a node is found: Mermaid 12.0.0 gives a node no `data-id`, only `id="<render id>-flowchart-n1-0"`. From its SVG only the positions are taken: each node's centre (`translate`) and box (`getBBox()`), each lane's box (`g.cluster.swimlane[data-id]` with its `rect.swimlane-body` and `.swimlane-title`), each flow's points (`path[data-edge][data-id]`, base64 `data-points`; two flows between one pair are `L_n2_n4_0` and `L_n2_n4_2`). The host goes in any case; Mermaid's picture never reaches the document.
4. `layoutGeometry()` scales both axes piecewise: each column to the width of its largest BPMN symbol (task 120 × 80, gateway 50, event 36, with 84 px kept beside an event for its label), each gap to 36 to 70 px; orders and right angles stay. Doubled points, and points less than a pixel apart, go first; an end piece that runs beside its symbol keeps 12 px from its side, and a flow keeps 12 px to its own symbol before it turns: a run Mermaid laid just beside its own, larger node, which scaled lies on the BPMN symbol's edge or inside it, moves 12 px off the side, the other end left where it is (story 2.20). The flows are docked on the smaller symbols, a circle and a diamond on their axis, a task anywhere on its side, an end closer than 12 px to the middle of a side at the middle (story 2.25); a slanted end piece, as Mermaid ends a flow on a diamond's side or draws a flow back as a diagonal, docks on the side facing the point before it, with a corner put in (a diagonal from end to end with two corners halfway).
