The demo text is part of the file, and the story changed it; so for the demo text the file after is a build of the new sources with the demo text as it was (`node build.mjs --src … --out …`, as the failure-case run builds its copies). With the new demo text in the file the same document, typed in, counts as changed, and the exports carry another footer. Then the reference document got its section on cards, step lists and markers, and the demo text a section each on callouts, cards and a step list (Ben, 2026-10-02: the demo text shows new functions from the start), which made the file another 1 907 B larger (93 239 → 95 146 B). With both, all assertions are green in all four variants and both browsers; the sizes are under *Download variants*. `tests/speichern.mjs` and `tests/durchlaeufe.mjs` are green in both browsers. Three controls, each a copy of the sources built and run in Chromium on the new reference document: without the rules of cards and step lists in `src/doc.css`, the run fails in every variant on the five checks of their look; without the pass in `render.js`, on the four counts, with every marker still a comment; and with the step numbers counted from 1 whatever the list says, the colon left in the actor and any bold text taken for a title, on the titles, on the numbers and actors, on the start value and on the actor's label. These three were run before the review of the story changed how the tile is placed. A fourth after it: with the step item positioned and the tile placed against it, as both first were, the run fails in every variant on the check of the tile and on the place of the preview of a footnote cited in a step.

**Known limits.**

- *The actor needs its colon inside the emphasis.* `*Website:*` names an actor, `*Website*:` does not. The rule is the spike's, where emphasis alone was taken for an actor at first and `1. *Nie* ohne Backup starten.` lost its first word to a label (its review, row 13).
- *A title is bold text and nothing around it.* `***Titel***`, which is emphasis around bold, and a link around bold text give a card without a title.
- *A list written as HTML with `reversed`* counts upwards as a step list. `marked` never emits one.
- *Not every renderer hides the comment;* see the convention above.

### Tables

Three things for tables, all document content: a wrapper that scrolls, a sub-line in a cell, and a facet filter.

```markdown
<!-- dokufix: facets Typ -->
| Merkmal | Typ | Wofür |
|---|---|---|
| `ORT`<br>*Pflichtfeld* | Text | Zuordnung zu einer Tour |
| `STATUS` | Kategorie | Wo der Kunde steht |
| `ERLAUBNIS` | Ja/Nein<br>*je eines* | Ob wir schreiben dürfen |
```

**The wrapper.** `buildTables()` in `src/app/tables.js`, the document pass `Tabellen`, puts every table into `<div class="dokufix-table">`, once: a table of Markdown and one written as HTML, at any depth, in a list item, a quotation, a callout, a footnote, a cell of another table. A second pass changes nothing. The wrapper is the scroll container and nothing else, `overflow-x:auto` in `src/doc.css`: a table wider than the text column scrolls inside it, and the page stays as wide as the window. The table's bottom margin stands on the wrapper, as `14px`, which is what `1em` was at the table's font size, so that it still collapses with what follows; inside a callout, a card or a step the rules that take the last child's margin away reach the wrapper as they reached the table. A facet filter puts its table into a group (below); there the margin stands on the group and not on the wrapper inside it, because the wrapper's margin would collapse through the group, which is the last child those rules reach, and stay below the callout's text (measured: 22 px against 8 px in a callout, 27 against 13 in a card).

- *Not positioned.* The wrapper, and the group of a facet filter, carry nothing that makes a containing block: no `position`, `transform`, `filter`, `contain`, `container-type`, `will-change`. The preview of a footnote cited in a cell is placed against the page and reaches out of the table uncut (see *Footnotes*, in a table cell). `tests/facets.test.mjs` reads the rules, the comparison run the computed styles.
- *No `tabindex`.* Not built: it would make every table a stop of the Tab key. Chromium and Firefox make a box that scrolls reachable by keyboard themselves; Safari, by the plan of the story, does not (not measured here).

**The sub-line.** Emphasis directly after a line break in a cell is a second line of the cell, subdued: `<em class="dokufix-cell-sub">`, upright, 12 px, in the grey of the step's actor. Which element that is the pass decides, by the class; the document styles style the class and never ask where an `<em>` stands.

| Cell | Sub-line |
|---|---|
| `**Name**<br>*Zusatz*` | "Zusatz" |
| `Name  <br>  *Zusatz*`, blanks around the line break | "Zusatz" |
| a header cell; `*a*<br>*b*<br>*c*`, each emphasis behind a line break; emphasis around bold text | each of them |
| `*nur kursiv*`, `Name<br>normal *spät*`, `*vorn*<br>hinten` | none: no line break directly before it |
| `a<br>**fett**`, emphasis inside bold text or a link behind the break | none: no emphasis that is a child of the cell |
| emphasis behind a line break in a paragraph, a list item, a heading | none: no cell |

**The facet filter.** `<!-- dokufix: facets Spalte -->` above a table, a block marker like `cards` (see *Block markers*), puts one control per distinct value of that column above the table, and one for all rows, chosen. Choosing one hides the other rows. The component is `buildFacets()` in `src/app/facets.js`, its entry `FACETS` in the list of markers. The markup:

```html
<div class="dokufix-facets">
  <fieldset class="dokufix-facet-bar">
    <legend>Typ</legend>
    <div class="dokufix-facet-controls">
      <label><input type="radio" name="dokufix-facet-1" class="dokufix-facet-0" checked>Alle <span class="dokufix-facet-count">3</span></label>
      <label><input type="radio" name="dokufix-facet-1" class="dokufix-facet-1">Text <span class="dokufix-facet-count">1</span></label>
      …
    </div>
  </fieldset>
  <div class="dokufix-table"><table> … <tr class="dokufix-facet-row dokufix-facet-1"> … </table></div>
</div>
```

- *The column* is named after `facets`, with or without double quotes. It is found by the first line of a header cell, white space collapsed, case ignored; the first match wins. The header row is the first row of `<thead>`, or else the table's first row when all its cells are `<th>`. A cell that spans columns names the first of them, so the column's place counts `colspan`; `rowspan` is not followed. The legend is the heading as the table writes it.
- *The data rows* are the rows of the table's own `<tbody>`s and rows that stand directly in the table: never a row of a table nested in a cell, never one of `<tfoot>`. A row without a cell at that place has the empty value.
- *The value of a cell* is its text up to the first `<br>`, without the word a status chip carries for assistive technology and without a footnote marker, white space collapsed. So `` `🟢 Live` `` is "Live", a green and a red "Live" are one value, and `Ja/Nein<br>*je eines*` is "Ja/Nein". Values are compared exactly, case included: `C`, `C++`, `C#` and `c` are four. An empty value is a value; its control reads "(leer)".
- *The controls,* in the order of the first appearance of their value, each with its number of rows; before them "Alle" with all rows, chosen. They are radio buttons in their labels, one group per table: `name` is `dokufix-facet-<n>`, counted through the document. A control and the rows of its value share a key, the class `dokufix-facet-<k>`; `k` is 0 for "Alle". Everything is made with `createElement` and `textContent`: what an author wrote into a heading or a cell is text in legend and control, `\<img src=x onerror=…\>` included. A table without data rows gets "Alle 0" alone.
- *The rules that hide* are fixed, in `src/doc.css`, one selector per key from 1 to `FACET_MAX`, in one rule: `.dokufix-doc .dokufix-facets:has(.dokufix-facet-7:checked) .dokufix-facet-row:not(.dokufix-facet-7){display:none}`. Nothing is generated per table: no `<style>`, no inline style, no id; the spike wrote a `<style>` per table with the table's id in every selector (its review, rows 7 to 9). `tests/facets.test.mjs` holds the rules and `FACET_MAX` together, and checks that the built file carries them.
- *Without `:has()`* the controls would do nothing, so the bar is `display:none` and shown only inside `@supports selector(:has(a))`; there every row stays. *In print* the bar is not shown and no row is hidden, whatever is chosen: the hiding rule and the bar stand inside `@media not print`. Nobody prints a filtered table without noticing.
- *The keyboard.* The radio button is hidden from the eye, `opacity:0` and one pixel, not `display:none`: Tab reaches the chosen control of a group, and the arrow keys choose. It is placed against its label, which is `position:relative` for that alone, as the chip is for its hidden word; a label holds text only, never a footnote marker. Placed against the page it would not scroll with the preview pane.
- *The look:* pills in the greys of the document styles, a ring before the text. The legend floats left; the controls are one flex container beside it, a formatting context of their own, so every line of controls starts at the same place beside the legend, and not, from the second line on, at the left edge below it. The comparison run measures the start of every line, at 1400 and at 600 px. The chosen control has a dark border, a darker tint and a filled ring, so it is not told by a colour alone; the focused one has the outline of the document's link colour. No colour of its own.
- *A chosen facet does not travel.* A download writes the markup, and `checked` is an attribute of "Alle" alone; the choice is a state of the page. Every file opens with all rows and "Alle" chosen; `tests/durchlaeufe.mjs` writes all four with a value chosen. A read-only download renders first, as it always did, and a render puts the page's own filter back on "Alle".
- *In a footnote.* A facet filter in a footnote definition works in the list of footnotes; the preview of that footnote, which is one run of text, holds the table's text and none of its controls (`buildFootnotePreview()` in `src/app/footnotes.js` leaves the bar out).

**How many values.** `FACET_MAX` is 32 (decided by Ben, 2026-10-03; the planner of the story had proposed 16). One value costs one selector in the minified document styles, in every file of every document: 100 B for the keys 1 to 9, 102 B from 10 on. Measured on the minified styles with esbuild 0.28.2, as the build minifies them; the table is the record of the choice:

| `FACET_MAX` | 8 | 12 | 16 | 24 | 32 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| bytes of the hiding rule | 799 B | 1 205 B | 1 613 B | 2 429 B | 3 245 B |

With 32 the hiding rule is 3 245 B, 1 632 B more than with 16, and the minified document styles are 22 630 B. The planner had proposed 16, about what the rules of the status chips cost, with which sixteen controls and "Alle" fill about two lines at reading width; 32 fill about four, and the free-text filter of story 2.6 serves a column with more. The example handbook needs 5 (its column `Typ`). Not taken: a binary key, 12 rules for 64 values, which puts six classes on every row of every facet table and is hard to read in the markup. A column with more distinct values is refused with a warning that names both numbers.

**What a marker comes to.** The warnings are the product's one warning, at the marker's place, naming the marker as written; the table stays a table in its wrapper.

| Source | The warning reads |
|---|---|
| `<!-- dokufix: facets Tpy -->`: no such column | Die Markierung „dokufix: facets Tpy“ nennt eine Spalte, die die Tabelle nicht hat. |
| `<!-- dokufix: facets -->`, `facets ""`: no column named | Die Markierung „dokufix: facets“ nennt keine Spalte. |
| a table without a header row | Die Markierung „dokufix: facets Typ“ braucht eine Tabelle mit Kopfzeile. |
| more distinct values than `FACET_MAX` | Die Markierung „dokufix: facets Regal“ trifft auf 33 verschiedene Werte in ihrer Spalte; mehr als 32 kann sie nicht filtern. |
| a table inside a table that already filters | Die Markierung „dokufix: facets I“ steht in einer Tabelle, die schon gefiltert wird. One group inside another would hide the rows of both. |
| `facets` before a list, a paragraph, nothing | Die Markierung „dokufix: facets Typ“ erwartet direkt danach eine Tabelle. |
| `cards` and `facets Typ` before one table, in either order | `facets` acts, `cards` warns: … erwartet direkt danach eine Aufzählung. |
| `facets` twice before one table | the first acts, the second: … steht mehr als einmal vor demselben Block. |

**What tables did to documents** (2026-10-03, `tests/vergleich.mjs --strict`, the built file before story 2.5 against the one after, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153, with the reference document and the demo text as they were before the story). Demo text: all 68 screenshots are pixel-identical, and all assertions are green; its three tables stand in their wrappers now. Reference document: all 68 screenshots differ, each in three lines and nowhere else: the three warnings that list the known markers read „Bekannt sind: cards, steps, facets.“ where they read „Bekannt sind: cards, steps.“ (744 px per screenshot in Chromium and 736 in Firefox; 696 and 729 in the screenshots of edit mode). That is the one visible difference, and it comes from the list of markers, not from the tables. Control: the same sources with `facets` left out of the list in those warnings give all 68 screenshots pixel-identical to before. So the wrapper changes no pixel of either document; neither has a table wider than its text column. Outside its `<style>` each `nur-lesen` and `schlank` export is the file it was apart from `<div class="dokufix-table">` around each table and those three texts, in both browsers (`kompakt` packs the same body); the stylesheet in it grew by 4 916 B (19 771 → 24 687 B), the rules of tables and facets, 3 245 B of them the hiding rule for 32 values. The file itself and every `Mit Editor` file grew by 8 941 B (95 220 → 104 161 B): 4 916 B of document styles and 4 025 B of script. 273 B of that came with the two fixes of the story's review, the container of the controls beside the legend and the bottom margin on the group of a facet filter: 189 B of styles and 84 B of script; and 1 632 B with `FACET_MAX` raised from 16 to 32 (Ben, 2026-10-03), the hiding selectors for the keys 17 to 32. For the demo text the file after is a build of the new sources with the demo text as it was, as for story 2.4. Then the reference document got its section on tables and the demo text a section with a facet filter, sub-lines and a table that scrolls (Ben, 2026-10-02: the demo text shows new functions from the start), which made the file another 2 113 B larger (104 161 → 106 274 B). With both, all assertions are green in all four variants and both browsers; the sizes are under *Download variants*. `tests/speichern.mjs` and `tests/durchlaeufe.mjs` are green in both browsers. Three controls, each a copy of the sources built and run in Chromium on the new reference document: without the hiding rule in `src/doc.css` the run fails in every variant on the checks of choosing, by every control, by click and by keyboard, in print, without `:has()` and with scripts off; without the pass `Tabellen` in `render.js`, on the eight checks of wrapper, margin, scrolling, sub-lines, the group and its look, and the footnote in a cell; with the wrapper positioned, on the check that it is not and on the place of the preview of a footnote in a cell. In Playwright's Firefox the comparison run does not judge that place once the page is scrolled (see *Comparison run*); on a short document that does not scroll the preview stands 90 px above its marker in both browsers, before the story and after it.

**The free-text filter.** `<!-- dokufix: filter "Merkmal suchen …" -->` above a table, a block marker like `facets`, gives the table a search field with a row counter where a script runs; typing hides the rows that do not contain the text. The component is `markFilter()` in `src/app/filter.js`, its entry `FILTER` in the list of markers; the field is the run-time pass `Tabellenfilter`, `attachTableFilters()` in the same module, and in `schlank` and `kompakt` the same function, run by the file when it opens (below).

```markdown
<!-- dokufix: filter "Merkmal oder Zweck suchen …" -->
<!-- dokufix: facets Typ -->
| Merkmal | Typ | Wofür |
```

- *Where a script runs, and only there* (decided by Ben). The field is in the editor, in `Mit Editor`, and, since Ben's decision after the first build (2026-10-03), in the read-only exports `schlank` and `kompakt`, which run a script anyway. `nur-lesen` shows the complete table without a field, and so do print and `schlank` with scripts switched off. That is the documented exception to NFR1, every function in all four variants, and NFR2, every function in the JS-free `nur-lesen` (FR18: where there is no script, the table is complete and unfiltered). `nur-lesen` stays without `<script>`.
- *In `schlank` and `kompakt`.* The build makes a second bundle, the reader bundle: `src/reader.js` with `src/app/filter.js`, `src/app/search.js`, `src/app/large-view.js`, `src/app/escape.js`, `src/app/diagram-downloads.js` and what they import, an IIFE that runs `attachTableFilters()` and `attachSvgDownloads()` on the content container, `main.reader-body`, starts the search there (see *Search*, in `schlank` and `kompakt`) and registers the keys of the large view of a diagram and the Escape listener with its three steps; `attachSvgDownloads()` puts the picture button below every diagram (see *Diagrams*, the downloads). It is minified, also by `--dev`, 26 736 B (9 810 B gzipped) since story 5.9 (how it grew is under *Search*, in `schlank` and `kompakt`), and stands in the page as a data block that does not run, `<script type="text/plain" id="dokufix-reader-js">`, filled through the slot `{{slot:reader.js}}`; the build refuses it when it contains `</script` or `<!--`. `readerScript()` in `src/app/downloads/export-body.js` reads that block, and every `schlank` and `kompakt` file carries it, with a filter table or without (story 5.3; until then only a file with a filter table carried the filter's bundle, 3 173 B). `schlank` writes it as a `<script>` behind its document, inside `<main>`, in front of the decoder of its diagrams; `kompakt` writes it as a data block (`id="f"`) that its decoder reads before it unpacks, because an element of the document could carry the same id, and runs after the document is in the page. Field, counter, facet interplay and Escape behave as in the editor; there is no read mode, so Escape in the field only empties it. What it costs per file is under *Download variants*; `nur-lesen` carries nothing of it.
- *The document and the page.* In the document the marker only marks its table: `<table data-dokufix-filter="Merkmal oder Zweck suchen …">`. The field is no part of the document: the run-time pass puts it into the page, marked `data-dokufix-transient`, so no download takes it along (see *Render passes*, transient elements). A read-only export renders, copies the preview and runs the export steps: `showFilteredRows()`, one step in `EXPORT_STEPS` after the transient elements, takes the class of a hidden row out of the copy for all three, and `removeFilterMarks()`, which `nur-lesen` alone hands in, the table's mark. `Mit Editor` empties the preview of its clone, and the opened file renders and builds its field anew.

  ```html
  <div class="dokufix-filter" data-dokufix-transient>
    <input type="search" class="dokufix-filter-input" placeholder="Merkmal oder Zweck suchen …" aria-label="Tabelle filtern" autocomplete="off">
    <span class="dokufix-filter-count" role="status">14 Zeilen</span>
  </div>
  <div class="dokufix-table"><table data-dokufix-filter="Merkmal oder Zweck suchen …"> … <tr class="dokufix-filter-out"> … </table></div>
  ```

- *Where the field stands: directly above the wrapper, outside it* (chosen by the planner, not decided by Ben; the story's AC1 said "inside the wrapper", written before story 2.5 built the wrapper as a scroll container). Inside it the field would scroll sideways with a wide table and leave the window, and the wrapper's `overflow-x:auto` would cut its focus outline. Above it the field never scrolls, nothing is positioned, the wrapper holds its table alone and keeps its margins. Beside a facet filter it stands in the group, between the controls and the wrapper, so the counter stands directly above the rows it counts. If the pass `Tabellen` failed, it stands directly above the table.
- *The placeholder* is what follows `filter`, with or without double quotes; with nothing there, `""` or blanks alone it is "Suchen …". The field's name for assistive technology is "Tabelle filtern". Everything is made with `createElement`, `setAttribute` and `textContent`: markup in the placeholder is text.
- *What matches.* The term, blanks collapsed and none at its ends, is looked for as one phrase, case ignored (`toLocaleLowerCase('de')`), in the text of each data row: its cells joined by a blank, a line break as a blank, without the word a status chip carries for assistive technology, a footnote marker with its preview, the controls of a facet filter, anything transient, and an `svg`, `style`, `script` or `template` in a cell, which hold no text a reader reads (`filterRowText()`; the last since story 5.16, finding N2 of the audit, together with the search, so a cell's CSS is no hit). So `PK_B` finds a cell `PK_A<br>PK_B`, `ort text` finds a row whose first two cells read "ORT" and "Text", and "grün" does not find a green chip. A table nested in a cell is text of its cell. The data rows are the facet filter's (`rowsOf()` in `src/app/facets.js`); the header row and `<tfoot>` are never hidden. An empty field shows every row.
- *The counter* says "14 Zeilen" when every row is shown, else "3 von 14 Zeilen"; "1 Zeile" and "0 von 1 Zeile" for a table of one row. It is `role="status"`, so a change is announced.
- *Beside a facet filter* (decided by Ben) both may stand before one table, in either order. A row is shown only when both hold: the free-text filter hides by its class, the facet filter by its rule. The counter counts the rows that match and, where a value is chosen, have it, out of all rows; it reads both from the classes and the chosen control, and is updated on `input` in the field and on `change` in the group. The facet counts stay those of the whole table.
- *Escape* in a field that holds text empties it and goes no further, so read mode stays; in an empty field it leaves read mode, as everywhere.
- *Not kept.* A render builds a new, empty field, as a facet filter returns to "Alle"; a read-only download renders first and so empties the page's field.
- *In print* the field is not shown and no row is hidden, whatever is typed: both rules stand inside `@media not print`.
- *Not positioned.* Neither the field nor what is in it is positioned or made a containing block: the preview of a footnote cited in a cell is placed against the page and stands where it stands without the filter (see *Footnotes*, in a table cell). A filter table in a footnote definition gives its table, and its field, in the list of footnotes; the preview of that footnote, one run of text, holds the table's text and no field.
- *The look:* a field of at most 320 px with the greys of the facet filter and its pill shape, the counter beside it in 12 px grey, the focus outline in the document's link colour. No colour of its own.

| Source | What it comes to |
|---|---|
| `<!-- dokufix: filter "Suchen …" -->` before a table | the field with that placeholder above the wrapper, the counter of all rows; the comment is gone |
| `<!-- dokufix: filter -->` before a table | the same with the placeholder "Suchen …" |
| `filter` and `facets Typ` before one table, in either order | both act; the field stands between the controls and the wrapper |
| `filter` before a list, a paragraph, nothing | Die Markierung „dokufix: filter“ erwartet direkt danach eine Tabelle. |
| `filter` twice before one table | the first acts, the second: … steht mehr als einmal vor demselben Block. |

**What the free-text filter did to documents.** (2026-10-03, `tests/vergleich.mjs --strict` through `npm run pruefung`, the built file before story 2.6 against the one after, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153, with the reference document and the demo text as they were before the story, neither of which has a `filter` marker). Demo text, on a build of the new sources with the demo text as it was: all 68 screenshots are pixel-identical, and all assertions are green. Reference document: all 68 screenshots differ, and only by the three warnings that list the known markers, which read „Bekannt sind: cards, steps, facets, filter.“: 574 px per screenshot in Chromium and 647 in Firefox; in the screenshots of edit mode the longer text takes a second line in one warning, and what stands below it moves down by 23 px in Chromium and 22 in Firefox. Control: the same sources with `filter` left out of the list in those warnings give all 68 screenshots pixel-identical to before; that run fails, as it has to, on the check of those warnings' words, once per variant. Outside its `<style>` each `nur-lesen` and `schlank` export is the file it was apart from those three texts, in both browsers; the stylesheet in it grew by 671 B (24 687 → 25 358 B), the rules of the free-text filter. The file itself grew by 3 249 B (106 274 → 109 523 B): 671 B of document styles and 2 578 B of script. Then the reference document got its section on the free-text filter and the demo text a search field over its facet table, with a paragraph and a row (Ben, 2026-10-02: the demo text shows new functions from the start), which made the file another 602 B larger (109 523 → 110 125 B). After Ben had seen the build, the demo text got every variant of its display features, a section of special cases and a photo (see *Download variants* and *Image assets*); the file was 176 614 B then. Then Ben decided that `schlank` and `kompakt` get the field as well (see the free-text filter above), which made the file 180 636 B. With it, `npm run pruefung` (2026-10-03, four standard runs side by side, 71 s): reference document 427 and 423 assertions green, demo text 422 and 418, save round trip 116, failure cases 540. Control, Chromium, reference document: with `filterScriptFor()` returning nothing, so that neither export carries the bundle, the run fails on eight checks, four each in `schlank` and `kompakt`: the fields, the group of the facet filter, the bundle in the file and the footnote in a cell of a filter table. With both, all assertions are green in all four variants and both browsers; the sizes are under *Download variants*. `tests/speichern.mjs` and `tests/durchlaeufe.mjs` are green in both browsers; the seven runs side by side took 149 s. Three controls, each a copy of the sources built and run in Chromium on the new reference document: without the pass `Tabellenfilter` in `render.js`, the run fails on the checks that need a field: the term typed while the files are written, the empty field after a read-only download, one field per filter after a render, the field in the group of the facet filter, the fields of `Mit Editor`, and the footnote in a cell of a filter table, which it no longer finds; without the rule that hides a row in `src/doc.css`, on typing, on the hidden words, beside a facet filter and in print, where the counter is right and every row stays; with the field put inside the wrapper, before the table, on the wrapper that no longer holds its table alone, on the group of the facet filter, on the field's place, on the counter beside a facet filter and on the footnote in a cell of a filter table.

**Known limits.**

- *`rowspan` is not followed.* A cell that spans rows counts for its first row; the rows below it read the cell at that place in their own markup. Markdown cannot write one.
- *A table nested in a cell* gives its text to the value of that cell: "einsIpq" for "eins" with a table of "I", "p", "q" in it.
- *The texts of two controls can be alike:* a cell that reads "Alle" or "(leer)" gets a control of that name beside the one of the filter.
- *A browser without `:has()`* shows no controls; the table is all there. *`kompakt` without JavaScript* has no table to filter: its whole document is packed.
- *The preview of a footnote in a cell, in an engine without anchor positioning,* is cut at the wrapper's edges (see *Footnotes*, in a table cell). Deferred.
- *A footnote cited in a cell that a wide table has scrolled out of the window* makes the page wider while its preview is hidden: the hidden preview stands at its marker, past the window's right edge. Measured on the demo text with the marker in the last column of a seven-column table, Chromium 153: the page 1 023 px wide in a window of 1 000, 998 in one of 900. So the demo text cites its footnotes in the first column of a wide table. Found while writing the demo text, 2026-10-03; not changed, open for Ben.
- *The free-text filter needs a script.* Neither `nur-lesen` nor print has the field, nor `schlank` with scripts switched off; that is the decided exception, not a limit to remove.
- *The columns of a filter table may change their width while typing:* the table lays out the rows that are shown, as a browser does for any table.
- *The free-text filter searches the text as it stands,* without folding accents or `ß`: "strasse" does not find "Straße".

### Licence information

A dokufix file uses work of others, and the MIT licence, which most of it is under, permits that on one condition: the copyright notice and the permission notice are included in all copies or substantial portions. The bpmn.io licence of `bpmn-js` (story 2.7) is the MIT licence with one more condition: the code that shows the bpmn.io watermark must not be removed or changed, and where the software is used in a website or application, the watermark stays fully visible. So every variant has a small text link, "license information", and behind it a view with the notices.

**The list** is `NOTICES` in `src/app/licences.js`, the one place the notices stand in:

| Entry | Version | Licence | Copyright lines, as published | How it gets into a file |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `marked` | 18.0.14 | MIT | "Copyright (c) 2018+, MarkedJS (https://github.com/markedjs/)" and "Copyright (c) 2011-2018, Christopher Jeffrey (https://github.com/chjj/)" | the editor loads it from the CDN |
| `marked-footnote` | 1.4.0 | MIT | "Copyright (c) 2023-2024 Stilearning (https://stilearning.com)" | the editor loads it from the CDN |
| Mermaid | 12.0.0 | MIT | "Copyright (c) 2014 - 2022 Knut Sveidqvist" | the editor loads it from the CDN |
| `bpmn-js` | 18.31.0 | bpmn.io License | "Copyright (c) 2014-present Camunda Services GmbH" | the editor loads it from the CDN |
| Octicons (`@primer/octicons`) | 19.38.0 | MIT | "Copyright (c) 2026 GitHub Inc." | the path data of seven icons: six in the document styles, the five of the callouts and, since story 2.10, the download symbol below a diagram, and since story 5.10 the magnifier of the search, in `src/app/search.js` |

- **What is on it** is what a file loads or carries. The tools of the build (esbuild, ESLint, Playwright, linkedom) are in no file and are not listed.
- **The lines** were fetched on 2026-10-02 from jsDelivr and GitHub. The npm package of `marked-footnote` has no licence file; its line is from the file `license` of its repository, `bent10/marked-extensions`. The licence file of `marked` also carries the notice of the original Markdown ("Copyright © 2004, John Gruber"); that one is not shown (decision of 2026-10-02).
- **The view** shows the five entries, each with name, version, licence and copyright lines, one sentence on how they get into a file, and below them each licence text once, in its wording (`LICENCE_TEXTS`): the MIT licence and the bpmn.io licence. The bpmn.io text is the file `LICENSE` of `bpmn-js@18.31.0` without its copyright line, fetched 2026-10-03 from jsDelivr; the file has no title, so the view calls it "bpmn.io License". `tests/licences.test.mjs` holds the hash of its text. The words around the entries are English, like the link.
- **A read-only export loads nothing from the CDN.** It carries what the libraries produced, and the icons. The view is the same in all four variants all the same: it says what the editor loads, and an export is written by that editor.

**A story that adds a library** adds one entry to `NOTICES`: `name`, `package` (its name on npm), `version`, `licence`, `copyright` (one line per holder, as published) and `use` (`'cdn'` or `'embedded'`). If its licence is not the MIT licence, the text goes into `LICENCE_TEXTS` under the key the entry names; the view shows every licence text once. Nothing else has to change: the editor and the three exports take the markup from `licencesHtml()`. `tests/licences.test.mjs` compares the list with what the sources pin: every jsDelivr npm URL in `src/index.html`, whatever tag and attribute it stands in (`<script src>`, `<link href>`), and the Octicons version in the comment of `src/doc.css`. A URL without an entry, an entry without a URL, a URL without a version and two versions that differ each fail a case, which names the entry. What the test does not see: a library loaded from another host, and one the script itself imports or loads; nothing does either today, and a story that starts to has to extend the test. An entry the test does not know fails until its published lines are added there as well, a second copy on purpose. Story 2.7 adds `bpmn-js` this way.

**The element** is `<details class="dokufix-licences">`: its `<summary>` is the link, a `<div class="dokufix-licences-view">` the view. Opening and closing is the browser's. There is no listener: no Escape, no click outside. Every text in it is escaped, and it contains no script, so `nur-lesen` stays without one.

**Where it stands.**

| Where | The element | The link | The view |
|---|---|---|---|
| `nur-lesen`, `schlank`, `kompakt` | written by the export directly after `<body>`, outside `<main>` | in the empty top margin, above the first line, its right edge on the right edge of the text column. In a window up to 820 px, where the magnifier of the search stands in the corner of `schlank` and `kompakt` (story 5.10), left of that corner, 59 px from the right, in `nur-lesen` as well | a box below the link, right-aligned with it, at most 560 px wide and 70 % of the window high, scrolling inside; it lies over the document. In a narrow window it reaches back to the right edge of the text column |
| `Mit Editor`, read mode | made by the script at load, in `<body>` before "Editor ↩" | the same place. In a window up to 820 px, where "Editor ↩" and the magnifier left of it stand in that corner, left of both, 148 px from the right | the same; in a narrow window it reaches back to the right edge of the text column |
| `Mit Editor`, edit mode | made by the script at load, the first of the toolbar's actions | in the dark toolbar, a flex item of the row | below the toolbar, at its right edge |
| `Mit Editor`, edit mode, window up to 820 px | the same element | in the hamburger panel, its first line | inline in the panel, like the download menu; at most half the window high. The panel ends at the window's lower edge and scrolls inside itself, so with the view and the download menu both open every action stays in reach |

- **It takes no room.** In read mode and in the exports the element is `position:absolute`, so it is out of the flow, and in the rail grid it is no grid item. The document starts where it starts without it; the comparison run measures that with the element taken out, closed and open.
- **It is placed against the page, not against `<body>`.** No ancestor of it is positioned. A positioned `<body>` was the first design and was measured on 2026-10-02: it becomes the containing block of every footnote preview too, and at 390, 600 and 900 px their fallbacks then put them elsewhere (in Chromium 153, 15 of 45 placements). So the right edge of the text column is worked out from the page's width, and the numbers of the layout stand in these rules once more: `max(32px, 50% - 443px)` for the column of 950 px with its padding of 32 px, in a narrow window left of the corner of "Editor ↩" and the magnifier, and beside a rail `max(0px, 50% - 781px) + 530px`, the grid of at most 1562 px with padding, rail and gap. Change the layout, and these change with it; the comparison run fails when they do not, at thirteen widths from 320 to 1900 px.
- **In the toolbar it costs about 110 px of the row.** To make up for that the hint "Strg+Enter rendert" is hidden up to 1300 px, where before it was hidden up to 820 px. Measured in Chromium 153, pixel by pixel from 1400 down to 821: before the link the toolbar stayed in one row down to 1160 px, and "Lesen" stayed inside the window down to 995 px. With the link and the hint as it was, those limits are 1271 and 1106 px; with the hint hidden up to 1300 px they are 1111 and 989 px. Not taken: leaving the hint alone, which costs a window between 995 and 1105 px its "Lesen" button.
- **It prints with the page.** There is no print rule.

**Why it is frame, not document.** The link belongs to the page a document is shown in, like the export's footer. It is no part of what the author wrote, no document pass produces it, and `src/doc.css` has no rule for it (decision of 2026-10-02; the alternative was a document pass with styles in `src/doc.css`). The cost is accepted: its styles stand twice, in `src/app.css` for the editor and in `READONLY_FRAME_CSS` for the exports, kept alike by hand. The style check treats the two places differently (see *Document styles*). In the export frame it lists the link's selectors one by one, and any other selector fails, `.dokufix-licences h5{…}` included. In `src/app.css` it only allows the two names, `.dokufix-licences` and `.dokufix-licences-view`: a rule there that uses them passes whatever else it selects, `.dokufix-licences h5{…}` too, and fails only when it uses another `dokufix-` name or a name of the document styles. And nothing compares the two copies with each other: a rule changed in one place and not in the other passes every check, and shows only where the comparison run measures the link.

**An open view does not travel.** In the editor both elements are made by `registerLicences()` in `src/app/editor.js` when the page loads, and both carry `data-dokufix-transient` (see *Render passes*). `Mit Editor` removes them from its clone like every transient element, so the save needs no line of its own, a saved file stays the built file with other data blocks, and the list exists once in the file, in the script. The exports write the element fresh from `licencesHtml()`, so it is closed whatever the editor shows. In `kompakt` it stands outside the packed body: it is there before the decoder has run, and with JavaScript off.

**What the licence information did to documents** (2026-10-02, `tests/vergleich.mjs`, the built file before story 2.18 against the one after, reference document and demo text, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153): all assertions are green, and each of the 136 screenshots differs from the one before in one place, the link: rows 60 to 70 and about 100 columns that end at the right edge of the text column, in read mode and in the three exports, and rows 28 to 39 in the toolbar of the edit-mode screenshot. Control: the same sources with one rule more, `.dokufix-licences{visibility:hidden}` at the end of `src/app.css` and of the export frame, compared with `--strict` by the comparison run as it was before the story: all 136 screenshots are pixel-identical to before. Outside its `<style>` each of the twelve read-only exports is the file it was plus the one element, once the ids Mermaid generates are masked. The element is 1 906 B and the frame rules of the link 1 016 B, so every export grew by 2 922 B; `kompakt` by the same, because the element stands outside what it packs. The file itself and every `Mit Editor` file grew by 5 871 B (78 232 → 84 103 B). `tests/speichern.mjs` and `tests/durchlaeufe.mjs` are green in both browsers. Controls (Chromium): on the build with the invisible link the comparison run fails in every variant, on every check that needs the link to be seen, and still writes its screenshots and sizes; built without the transient mark on the editor's two elements, the save round trip fails in every save on the comparison with the built file, and shows the element, and the failure-case run fails on the check that the saved file has no licence element.

### Table of Contents (two layers)

Two complementary mechanisms, deliberately separate:

- **Inline `[[toc]]` marker** — write `[[toc]]` (default H2+H3) or `[[toc:N]]` for `N` in 1–6 (depth) on its own line in the markdown. The marker only fires when the paragraph contains nothing but the literal text — wrapping it in inline elements (e.g., `` `[[toc]]` `` to *talk about* the marker) leaves it as visible content. At render time, the matching paragraph is replaced with a nested `<nav class="dokufix-toc">` list. The list is **static HTML** — it travels through every export variant including the JS-free `nur-lesen` one, and the numbering toggle reaches it via a dedicated `tocH2/tocH3/tocH4` counter scope (no double-counting with body headings).
- **Right-side rail** — auto-generated from H2/H3/H4 headings, visible above ~1500 px viewport when the document has ≥ 4 such headings. At wide viewports the body switches to a 2-column **grid**: a 1000 px content column (centered in its track) and a 425 px rail column, sharing the same horizontal budget with a 73 px gap instead of overlapping. The body caps at 1562 px (1000 + 73 + 425 + 64 padding), so from 1562 px upward both columns hit their exact target widths; between 1500 and 1561 px the content track flexes a little while the rail stays pinned. The rail uses `position: sticky; top: 80px` so it stays in view during scroll while still participating in the grid for sizing. Two flavors of the same UI:
  - **Live (editor / `Mit Editor` downloads):** scroll-based "reading line" scrollspy picks the last heading whose top edge sits at or above 25 % of the viewport — so there is always exactly one active entry (the first heading before scrolling, the last after the document ends, the just-scrolled-past one in between). The active link is auto-scrolled into view inside the rail's own scroll container so it stays visible in long tables of contents. Smooth-scrolls on click. Visible only in read mode.
  - **Static (read-only downloads — `nur-lesen`, `schlank`, `kompakt`):** pre-built HTML emitted at export time with the same heading list and CSS, but no JS dependency. Clicking jumps via native anchor — no scrollspy, no smooth scroll. Survives the JS-free `nur-lesen` variant.

Heading IDs are slugified deterministically, from the text the heading's entries show (`headingLabelText()`; see *Render passes*). German `ä/ö/ü/ß` (and uppercase variants) are explicitly spelled out, then `String.prototype.normalize('NFKD')` folds the remaining Latin diacritics (`é → e`, `à → a`, `ñ → n`, `ç → c`) before stripping combining marks. CJK and other non-Latin scripts that don't decompose under NFKD collapse to `section`, deduped with `-N` suffixes. Anchor links remain stable across re-renders for the same heading text.

### Search

In read mode the key `/` opens a search panel (epic 5, story 1, the tracer the later stories of the epic build on), and so does a click on the magnifier at the top right (story 10, below): in the editor, in a `Mit Editor` file, and since story 5.3 in `schlank` and `kompakt` (below). The reader types a term; the panel lists one result per place of the document that holds it, in the order of the document, grouped under the headings of its sections (story 4, below), and above the list a line says how many hits there are at how many places in how many sections: "20 Treffer an 15 Stellen in 7 Abschnitten", "1 Treffer an 1 Stelle in 1 Abschnitt", "Keine Treffer", nothing for an empty field, "Zu kurz: mindestens drei Buchstaben oder Ziffern" for a term too short. Two switches below the field change how the term is compared (story 2): "Groß- und Kleinschreibung beachten" and "Leerzeichen, Bindestriche und Punkte ignorieren". A result is a part of the place's text, at most 160 characters around the first hit and cut at blanks, with every hit in it marked (`<mark>`), a table row's after "Tabelle: " (story 6, below), a diagram's after "BPMN-Diagramm: " or "Mermaid-Diagramm: " (story 7, below), the metadata panel's after "Metadaten: " and a code block's after "Code: " (story 8, below); a click scrolls the document to the place (`scrollIntoView({ block: 'center' })`, a diagram to its start, the metadata panel and a code block to their first hit), and the panel stays open with its results. While it is open every hit is highlighted in the document as well (story 5, below).

**Three modules.**

| Module | What it does | Pure |
|---|---|---|
| `src/app/search-match.js` | `findHits(term, text, { caseSensitive, fuzzy })`: the ranges `{ start, end }` of every hit in a place's text, in order and not overlapping; `tooShort(term, { fuzzy })`: whether a term is too short to be searched; `excerpt(text, hits, max)`: the part a result shows, with the hits in it | yes, strings in, numbers out |
| `src/app/search-places.js` | `collectPlaces(root)`: the places under the root, `[{ el, text, map, kind }]`, in the order of the document, `map` the text node and offset each unit of the text came from, `kind` `"Tabelle"` for a table row (`KIND_ROW`), `"BPMN-Diagramm"` or `"Mermaid-Diagramm"` for a diagram (the labels of `DIAGRAM_LANGUAGES` in `src/app/diagram-kinds.js`), `"Metadaten"` for the metadata panel (`KIND_META`), `"Code"` for a code block (`KIND_CODE`) and none for any other place; `nodeRanges(map, { start, end })`: the ranges of the document a hit covers, `[{ startNode, startOffset, endNode, endOffset }]`; `groupResults(root, results)`: the results, `[{ el, hits }]`, under their group headings, `{ groups, sections }` | yes, works on the root it is handed and changes nothing in it |
| `src/app/search.js` | the panel, the key `/`, the magnifier, `openSearch()`, `closeSearch()`, `registerSearch({ root, inReadMode })`, the wait for the decoder of `schlank`, the highlight of the hits in the document | no, it builds the panel and the magnifier in `<body>` and reads the root its caller hands it: `#preview` from `src/app.js`, `main.reader-body` from `src/reader.js`; it imports no element of the page |

**What a hit is.** With both switches off the term is compared as the free-text filter compares it: blanks collapsed and none at its ends, case ignored by `toLocaleLowerCase('de')`. `findHits()` takes the switches as options, not as the panel's state, so that the table filter can use it as well (story 11): `caseSensitive` makes case count, nothing is lowered; `fuzzy`, light fuzzy, ignores white space, the hyphens `-`, U+2010 and U+2011, the soft hyphen U+00AD and the dot `.`, in the term and in the text, so `statuschip`, `Status Chip` and `status.chip` all find "Status-Chip". Nothing else: no umlaut folded, `oe` is no `ö`, no letters swapped (FR46). Both switches start off, both together are allowed (`Status-chip` then finds no "Status-Chip"), and flipping one searches again at once. Lowering can change the length of a text (`İ` becomes `i̇`, two UTF-16 units), and light fuzzy leaves characters out, so the text is compared one character at a time and each unit compared remembers the character it came from: a range is always one of the original text, and covers whole characters of it. A fuzzy hit spans from its first to its last matched character, the ignored characters between them included and none before or after it. `tests/search.test.mjs` pins that with `İ`, the capital sharp s and an emoji before the term. The match runs on a place's text, one string over all its text nodes, so light fuzzy finds a word that emphasis breaks (`Sta<em>tus</em>-Chip`); `nodeRanges()` lays such a range back over the text nodes it came from (story 5, below).

**The minimum length** (FR47, Ben 2026-10-03). A term needs three letters or digits (`\p{L}`, `\p{N}`; an umlaut is a letter), unless it holds another character, an emoji, `#`, `§`, or a `-` with light fuzzy off, or is `ae`, `oe`, `ue` or `ss` in any case. A blank is neither a letter nor another character: `a b` is too short. With light fuzzy on the rule applies to what is compared: `S-C` is too short, and `-.-`, which leaves nothing, as well. A term too short lists nothing, and the summary says "Zu kurz: mindestens drei Buchstaben oder Ziffern".

**What a place is.** Every `p`, `li`, `h1` to `h6`, `tr` and `pre` of the root, every diagram's figure, `figure.dokufix-diagram`, and the metadata panel, `details.dokufix-frontmatter`: the preview, or the content container of an export. A place's text is read as `filterRowText()` reads a row: a line break as a blank, without anything transient, without a footnote marker and its preview, without the word a status chip carries for assistive technology, without the controls of a facet filter, white space collapsed; and without a footnote's return arrows "↩", which are no text of the footnote, and the number tile of a step. A heading's text is the one its entries show (`headingLabelText()`), with the same things left out. A place inside a place is a place of its own and no text of the outer one: in a loose list the paragraph is the place and the item adds none; an item with a nested list is read without it, and each nested item is a place; an item with a table is read without it, and each row of the table is a place. A table row is read as a whole (story 6, below), and so are a diagram (story 7, below), the metadata panel and a code block (story 8, below); a code block in a list item is no text of the item and a place of its own, while a `pre` in a table cell is text of its row, as everything a row holds, and no place. A place without text is none. Text that raw HTML puts where Markdown never does (story 5.16, finding N1 of the audit, where it was not searched at all): any element outside a place that holds a text node of its own, not only white space, is a place of its own, read as a paragraph is, without the places, lists, tables and code blocks in it and with every other element in it as its text; nothing inside it, nor inside any other place, is such a place. So text directly in a `div`, a `dt` and a `dd` (a `dl` is none), the `summary` of an author's `details` (the `details` is none, a Markdown paragraph in it a place as always), a `figcaption`, a bare `blockquote` without `p`, a `caption` is found, and `<div><strong>Wort</strong></div>` is the `strong`; an image's `alt` is not, nor the content of a `noscript` or an `iframe`, which the page keeps as one raw text node, tags and all, and never shows: both are left out of every place as an `svg` or a `script` is (a row reads them as `filterRowText()` does). Nothing the page makes is such a place: what is left out below stays out, and the field of a table filter with its counter is transient. `tests/vergleich.mjs` checks the six blocks of the audit's probe in raw HTML put into the document. No place, nor anything inside one: a warning, the metadata in the footer of an export (`footer.dokufix-meta`), the inline table of contents, whose entries are the headings, the heading "Fußnoten" of the list of footnotes, which the document styles hide, and whatever is transient; so a table or a code block inside a warning holds no place either. So a heading listed in `[[toc]]` is a result once, and a footnote's text is a result at its definition and not at its marker.

**Grouped under the headings** (story 4). The group headings are exactly the headings the rail lists: those of `documentHeadings()` of the levels H2 to H4, labelled as their entries are (`headingLabelText()`, with what a heading's place text leaves out left out as well). A result belongs to the last of them before it in the order of the document, so a heading that holds the term is a result in its own group, a result under an H5 or H6 belongs to the H4 (or H3, H2) above it, and a heading inside a callout, which the rail does not list, is a result of the group the callout stands in. What stands before the first group heading forms a group of its own, first and at the level of an H2, labelled with the text of the H1 before it, or "Am Anfang" where there is none; an H3 or H4 before the first H2 is its child. A group is listed when it holds a result or a group below it does, nested as the headings are; its own results come before the groups below it, so the list stays in the order of the document. Each group heading carries the numbers of its branch, its own results and those below it ("12 Treffer an 5 Stellen"), so a parent listed only for a group below it reads its numbers and not "0 Treffer"; the summary counts as sections only the groups with results of their own. `groupResults()` goes by the order of the document alone, one walk over the root, not by the kind of a place: whatever stories 6 to 8 make a place falls into its group unchanged. A group heading is text, not a button, one line of 28 px, its label cut with "…" where it is too long and its numbers on the right; it sticks at the top of the list while its group scrolls under it (`position: sticky`), the headings stacked by depth, the outermost at the top and each one below 28 px lower, and it leaves with the last result of its group; an outer heading lies above the ones below it (`z-index` 3, 2, 1 by depth), so a heading that leaves slides under its parent's and not over it. Group headings and results are indented by the level of their heading. No group is collapsed and nothing is ranked. For assistive technology a group heading is a heading of the level of its heading in the document, `role="heading"` with `aria-level` 2 to 4, so a screen reader can go from section to section, and both lists say `role="list"`, which keeps them lists although `list-style: none` takes their markers: Safari and VoiceOver drop the semantics of a list without markers otherwise (story 5.16, finding N4 of the audit). Nothing of it changes how the panel looks.

```html
<ol class="search-results" role="list">
  <li class="search-group search-group-h2">
    <div class="search-group-head" role="heading" aria-level="2"><span class="search-group-title">Status-Chips</span><span class="search-group-count">1 Treffer an 1 Stelle</span></div>
    <ol class="search-group-list" role="list">
      <li class="search-group search-group-h3">
        <div class="search-group-head" role="heading" aria-level="3">…</div>
        <ol class="search-group-list" role="list"><li><button type="button" class="search-result">… in <mark>Tabelle</mark>n …</button></li></ol>
      </li>
    </ol>
  </li>
</ol>
```

**The hits in the document** (story 5). While the panel is open, every hit of every listed place is highlighted in the reading view, all of them and not only the first of a place, in the yellow of the marks in the results (`#ffe066`). The highlight is drawn with the CSS Custom Highlight API: one `Highlight` under the name `search-hit` in `CSS.highlights`, of `StaticRange`s, styled by `::highlight(search-hit)` in `src/search.css` and not printed. Nothing is wrapped, no `<mark>`, no class or attribute is set in the document: its DOM is the one it is with the panel closed, so a `Mit Editor` file saved and a `nur-lesen`, `schlank` or `kompakt` file written while the panel is open are the files they would be without it, and no save or export path cleans anything up. The ranges come from the place's text: `collectPlaces()` reads each place's text and, in the same walk, the map of it, for every unit of the text the text node and offset it came from (null for a blank the reading puts in itself, a line break or a chip's word in a heading), so the two cannot drift; a heading is walked as it stands, with the rule of `headingLabelText()` for a chip's word, not read from a copy. `nodeRanges()` turns the range of a hit in the text into ranges of the document: one range for a hit with nothing left out inside it, however many text nodes it runs over (emphasis, a link, a line break, light fuzzy over `Sta<em>tus</em>-Chip`), and one per stretch between what the text leaves out otherwise (a footnote marker, a chip's word, a step's tile, a nested list), so that none of those is highlighted; white space the text collapsed lies inside a range, never at its ends. Every search replaces the highlight, so it follows a changed term and a flipped switch; a term too short, an empty field and closing the panel ("×", Escape, leaving read mode) take it away. A render that replaces the document's nodes leaves ranges over nodes no longer in the document, which draw nothing, until the next search; a click on a result then searches again. The API is in Chromium since 105, Safari 17.2 and Firefox 140, within NFR7; where `CSS.highlights` is missing the results are listed without highlight and nothing fails. Diagrams are places since story 7, but by decision none of their hits is highlighted: a hit in a diagram has no range (see *Hits in diagrams*). The metadata panel and code blocks are places since story 8, and their hits are highlighted like any other, on screen in dark text (see *Hits in metadata and code*); no result is emphasised as the current one.

**Hits in tables** (story 6). Every table row is a place, `<tr>`, the header row and a row of `<tfoot>` included: a term only in a header cell is found as well. Its text is the row as the free-text filter reads it, `filterRowText()` in `filter.js`: the cells joined by a blank, a line break as a blank, without anything transient, a footnote marker with its preview, the word a status chip carries for assistive technology, the controls of a facet filter and, since story 5.16, an `svg`, `style`, `script` or `template` in a cell (the tags the search leaves out everywhere, `EXCLUDED_TAGS`; until then a row read them, the filter as well, so that a cell's CSS was a hit), white space collapsed; nothing else is left out. A table nested in a cell is text of its outer row, its cells joined by a blank, and its own rows are no places; a paragraph or list in a cell is text of the row as well. `collectPlaces()` reads a row in a walk of its own (`rowReading()`) that keeps the rules of `filterRowText()` and makes the map in the same walk, as for every place (story 5); `filter.js` is not changed, and `tests/search.test.mjs` holds the two together: over a varied table, with a sub-line, chips, footnote markers, a transient element, a nested table and a facet bar in a cell, `<thead>` and `<tfoot>`, every row's place text is `filterRowText()` of the row. A hit in a row is highlighted with `search-hit` like any other, one range per cell it touches: the reading counts its part up at every cell's edge, so no range runs from one cell into the next, and `HinweisBlock` with light fuzzy over the cells "Hinweis" and "Block" is one hit with two ranges. A row carries the kind `"Tabelle"`; the panel puts it before the result, `<span class="search-kind">Tabelle: </span>`, in the grey of the numbers, and it is no text of the place: the hits, their count, the part a result shows and the groups see the row's text alone, so "Tabelle" never finds a row by its kind. Story 7 gives a diagram a kind of its own the same way, and story 8 the metadata panel and a code block theirs. A click on a row's result scrolls the row to the middle of the window (`block: 'center'`), and the panel stays open.

A row a table's filter hides (Ben, 2026-10-03, option b) is listed like any row, with `<span class="search-hidden"> (ausgeblendet)</span>` after its text, italic and in dark red (`#a40e26`, the label of the red status chip), so it is not overlooked (Ben, 2026-10-03). Hidden is a row with the class `dokufix-filter-out` of the free-text filter, or a row of a facet table (inside `.dokufix-facets`) without a box (`getClientRects()` empty), which is how a facet filter hides it: by CSS alone, through `:has()` in `src/doc.css`, with no class on the row. A row without a box anywhere else is hidden by no filter and counts as shown, and so is every row in a closed `<details>` of the author, a facet table's included (story 5.16, finding N8 of the audit, where the rows of a facet table in a closed `<details>` could read "(ausgeblendet)" and their click go to facet buttons that showed no more than they did). Whether a place stands in a closed `<details>` is read from the DOM, outside its `summary`, not from a box: Chromium 153 gives a box to what a closed `<details>` holds (its content is `content-visibility: hidden`), so a row there has one, and `scrollIntoView()` on it showed nothing. A click on the result of a place in a closed `<details>` opens every one it stands in first (`open`), as the reader would, and they stay open, as the metadata panel does (*Hits in metadata and code*): the one change the search makes to the document, and no part of a file, since `Mit Editor` saves from the source and an export renders anew. Then the row is read again: one a facet filter hides goes to the facet buttons. A place in a `summary`, which shows while its `<details>` is closed, opens nothing. `tests/vergleich.mjs` puts the first facet table of the document into a closed `<details>` for the moment and checks both. A click on a hidden row's result scrolls to its table's filter controls instead, the facet buttons (`.dokufix-facet-bar` of the facet group around the table's wrapper), else the free-text filter's field above the wrapper, else the table, and the filter stays as it is: the search never changes what a reader chose. Whether a row is hidden is read when the search runs and again on the click, so a click on a row that a filter shows again by then goes to the row. While the panel is open and holds a term, a change of a table filter in the root, a term typed into its field or a facet value chosen, runs the search again after the same 150 ms as typing (`registerSearch()`, one listener for `input` and `change` on the document; see *Keys and events*), so the marks follow the filter. That a row was hidden is no part of the file: a download shows every row (`showFilteredRows()`).

The same holds in the editor, in `Mit Editor`, in `schlank` and in `kompakt`; `nur-lesen` has no search. `tests/vergleich.mjs` checks it in the three: a term only table rows hold, in the demo text "Zitronenfalter", lists one result per row with "Tabelle: " before the text, a click centres the row and its hits are highlighted in its cells, each range inside one cell; with a facet value chosen, a row it hides is marked "(ausgeblendet)", a click goes to the facet buttons and leaves the value chosen; "Alle" chosen while the panel is open runs the search again, the mark goes and the click goes to the row; a term typed into the table's own field marks the rows it hides; and in the demo text's table of keys, which has a free-text filter and no facet filter, a row its field hides is marked, and a click on it brings the field into the window with its term unchanged.

**Hits in diagrams** (story 7). Every diagram is one place, its figure `figure.dokufix-diagram` (see *Diagrams*), whatever it shows; the kind comes from its class, `"BPMN-Diagramm"` for `dokufix-diagram-bpmn`, `"Mermaid-Diagramm"` for `dokufix-diagram-mermaid`, and the panel puts it before the result as it puts a row's. Its text is the labels the reader sees in its SVG, never its source: `collectPlaces()` reads it in a walk of its own (`diagramReading()`) and does not walk into the figure, so a `<p>` of a Mermaid label in a `foreignObject` is no place of its own. Of the figure only the `svg` in its SVG container is read: not the bar of the large view (its title, the zoom steps, "Schließen"), not the line of its downloads (its file names and the picture button, story 2.10), not the credit "Gezeichnet mit bpmn-js", not the toggle and the radio buttons. Nor the live viewer of a BPMN diagram (story 2.11): its container stands before the stage, outside the SVG container, and draws the diagram a second time, and its hint stands in the bar; both are transient, and the lightbox of its logo stands in `<body>`, outside the root. With the viewer running and after it ran the search lists the same places with the same texts as with the view closed (seen in Chromium 153, 2026-10-03; no check covers it). In the `svg` only what a `text` or a `foreignObject` holds, not a `style`, `title` or `desc`: Mermaid's CSS (`fill`) is no hit. A blank stands around every `text` and `foreignObject`, and around a `p` or `div` in a `foreignObject`; a `<br>` is a blank, so Mermaid's `Zeile eins<br>Zeile zwei` reads "Zeile eins Zeile zwei". The lines of a `text`, its `tspan` children, are joined in their order (Ben, 2026-10-03, option a): at a line's end nothing is added after a blank or a hyphen (`-`, U+2010, U+2011), one blank otherwise; white space collapsed. bpmn-js breaks "Fernleihe bestellen" as "Fernleihe " over "bestellen", "Vormerkung gemeldet" as "Vormerkung" over "gemeldet", a label with a hyphen at it, "Rückgabe-" over "Automatenbedienung": all three are found as written with light fuzzy off. Accepted: a word bpmn-js splits because it is wider than its shape reads split, and "Hin-" over "und Rückweg" reads "Hin-und Rückweg"; light fuzzy finds both. What a hit counts is what the reader sees: a sequence diagram draws each actor at the top and at the bottom, so its name holds a term twice. A figure without a drawn `svg`, its container still holding the source while the diagram is drawn, or a `schlank` file's SVG still packed, has no text and is no place; a diagram that failed is a warning and no figure. In `schlank` the search waits for the decoder anyway (story 3), so it reads the diagrams unpacked, and `kompakt` and the editor read the same SVG. Nothing inside a diagram is highlighted: every unit of its map maps to no node, so `nodeRanges()` makes no range of a hit in it. A click on a diagram's result scrolls the top of its figure to the top of the window (`block: 'start'`), and the panel stays open. `diagrams.js`, `bpmn.js`, the large view and the render passes are not changed: the search reads the figure they make.

`tests/vergleich.mjs` checks it in the editor, in `Mit Editor`, `schlank` and `kompakt`: every diagram drawn is one place of the kind its class names; a label of a BPMN and of a Mermaid diagram, in the demo text "Abholbereit" and "nachfordern", lists the diagram once with its prefix, none of its hits is highlighted and no range lies in an `svg`, and a click puts the figure's top at the top of the window; a BPMN label bpmn-js draws over two lines, at a blank, glued or at a hyphen, whichever the document has, is found with light fuzzy off ("Fernleihe bestellen" and "Ausleih-Statistik" in the demo text, "Vormerkung gemeldet" in the reference document); a document without a drawn diagram lists no result as a diagram; a word only in a diagram's source (`isExecutable`, in the demo text "Stempeln" of a subprocess that is not drawn open and `Prozess_Neu`) or in its frame ("Einpassen", "Schließen", "Gezeichnet") lists no diagram for it, only one whose labels hold it ("Akte schließen").

**Hits in metadata and code** (story 8). The metadata panel, `details.dokufix-frontmatter` (see *Frontmatter*), at most one and before the H1, is one place of the kind `"Metadaten"` (`KIND_META`), and every code block, a `pre` outside the panel, a warning and a table row, is one place of the kind `"Code"` (`KIND_CODE`); the panel puts the kind before the result as it puts a row's. `collectPlaces()` reads both in a walk of their own (`ownReading()`) and does not walk into them, so a list item of the panel is no place of its own. The panel's text is the author's data: every key (`dt`) and value (`dd`, the items of a list, a nested list of keys), with a blank and a new part at the edge of each, as at a cell's edge in a row, so `<dt>title</dt><dd>Willkommen</dd>` reads "title Willkommen", never glued, and no range of a hit runs from a key into its value; a block that could not be read is searched in its raw text, `pre.dokufix-fm-raw`. Not read: the summary, the label "Metadaten" and the digest, which repeats rows of the panel and would count the title twice, and the mark of an empty value "—". The panel stands before the first H2, so its result is in the first group, labelled with the H1. A code block's text is its text as it stands, white space collapsed as everywhere; inline `code` stays text of its paragraph, and a `pre` in a table cell (raw HTML) text of its row (story 6), no code block. A fenced `mermaid` or `bpmn` block is no code: it is the source of a diagram (story 7); `collectPlaces()` takes the languages from `src/app/diagram-kinds.js`, the one source of their names and labels, which `diagrams.js` keys its renderers by (story 5.15), and not from `DIAGRAM_KINDS`, which would bring the renderers into the reader bundle. It names the panel's classes itself, so as not to bring `frontmatter.js` along. A warning's detail (`pre.dokufix-warning-detail`) and the footer of an export (`footer.dokufix-meta`) stay no places. Hits in both are highlighted with `search-hit`. On the dark background of a code block (`#1c1c1e`, its text `#f5f5f7`) the light text would not read on the yellow, so on screen the highlight sets a dark text colour, `@media screen{::highlight(search-hit){color:#1c1c1e}}`, the colour of the panel's own text; in print nothing of the highlight is drawn, as before. A click on the result of the panel or of a code block brings its first hit to the middle of the window, so a hit deep in a tall block is in view: `centreHit()` in `search.js` makes a `Range` of the first range `nodeRanges()` gives for the first hit, scrolls a code block sideways where the hit lies outside its box, and scrolls the window by the hit's distance from the middle, as near as the page scrolls; without a range it centres the element. The panel, a closed `<details>`, is opened first (`open = true`) and stays open afterwards, as if the reader had opened it; no listener is added and Escape is unchanged. Its `open` is no part of a file: `Mit Editor` saves from the source, and an export renders the document anew, with the panel closed. `frontmatter.js`, `diagrams.js`, `warning.js` and the render passes are not changed.

`tests/vergleich.mjs` checks it in the editor, in `Mit Editor`, `schlank` and `kompakt`: the panel is one place of its kind and every code block outside it, a warning and the footer one of its kind, a diagram's source none; a value of the panel, in the demo text "Beispiel-Autorin", with the panel closed, lists the panel once with "Metadaten: " in the first group, its hit highlighted in it, and a click from the end of the document opens the panel and brings the hit to the middle of the window, or as near as the page scrolls, while the search stays open; a key, "gueltig_bis", lists the panel; "Metadaten" lists no panel, and the title the digest repeats counts once in the panel and never in its summary; a word of a code block, "renderMermaidIn", lists the block once with "Code: ", its hits highlighted, and a click brings the first to the middle, in a text colour that reads on the yellow at 4.5 : 1 where the block's own does not; a code block in a list item is no text of the item, "Rückbuchungsbeleg" lists the block alone; `isExecutable`, only in a diagram's source, lists no code block. `tests/speichern.mjs` (step 10): a `Mit Editor` file saved after a click opened the panel is the built file as every other and opens with the panel closed, and `nur-lesen`, `schlank` and `kompakt` written after such a click are byte for byte the files written with the panel closed.

**The panel** is frame, not document. `registerSearch()` makes it once, at load, appended to `<body>`, outside the content (the magnifier is appended after it: in the editor the two are the last children of `<body>`; in `schlank`, where the bundle runs inside `<main>` while the file is parsed, the rail follows them), with `data-dokufix-transient`: a `Mit Editor` file saved while it is open has neither the panel nor the term (`tests/speichern.mjs`, step 8). Its elements are made with `createElement` and `textContent`; the document's text reaches it as text, never through `innerHTML`. No element of it has an id (story 5.16, finding M3 of the audit): an id of the document could be the same, as the anchor `search-input` of a heading "Search Input" was the id of its field, so that the label named the heading and a click on it did not focus the field. The field stands in its label instead, `<label class="search-label"><span class="search-label-text">Im Dokument suchen</span><input type="search" class="search-input" …></label>`, which names it without an id, and the close button "×" stands at the top right of the panel, beside the label's text, the field below both as before; in the DOM the button comes first, before the label, so Tab goes from the field to the switches and the results as it did. `tests/vergleich.mjs` checks it with a heading "Search Input" put into the document: a click on the label's text focuses the field. Nothing of the search changes the DOM of `#preview`, the highlight included, and no render pass knows of it: the search runs when the reader types, 150 ms after the last key, over the preview as it is then, outside every render, and a failure in it says "Die Suche ist fehlgeschlagen." in the panel and on the console and breaks nothing else. A click on a result whose place a render has replaced since runs the search again. The search keeps the places it read between searches (story 5.16, finding N5 of the audit, where every key read the whole document anew): a `MutationObserver` on the root drops them on any change of its nodes or its text (`childList`, `subtree`, `characterData`), a render, a diagram drawn after the panel opened, the decoder of `schlank` unpacking; the records it holds and has not delivered yet count as well, so a change made in the same task as a search is seen. A change of an attribute does not drop them: a filter hides a row by a class or by CSS, which `rowHidden()` reads when the search runs, and the open `<details>` of the metadata panel changes no text. So the search still knows nothing of the render. Two more savings: `groupResults()` does not walk into a diagram, a code block or the metadata panel, which hold no heading, so the inside of an SVG costs nothing; and `findHits()` lowers a text whole, one call in place of one per character, where that leaves its length as it is and the text holds no capital sigma: lowered whole, a `Σ` at the end of a word becomes `ς`, one character at a time always `σ`, and that stays so, as the audit found it ("ΟΔΟΣ" is found by "οδοσ" as before, not by "οδος"); light fuzzy compares one character at a time as before. `tests/messung-suche.mjs` measures it: probe 1 of the audit, built so that its counts are those of the audit's table, 284 187 characters of Markdown: "zitronenfalter" 1 600 hits at 1 600 places, "hirschkäfer" 400 at 400, "absatz" 4 400 at 1 600 places in 800 sections, "wespen" 3 000 in one paragraph with 23 marks in its result. In Chromium 153 on 2026-10-04, the built file of `6eb0b39` against the one of story 5.16, seven searches per term, two runs, median: before the story a search took 82 to 90 ms after the pause of 150 ms (the longest task on the main thread as much), after it 19 to 32 ms, and no task of 50 ms or more; the first search after the render, which reads the document, 94 to 96 ms before and 46 to 60 ms after. With the pause, which stays, 232 to 240 ms before and 169 to 182 ms after, the first search 245 to 246 ms and 196 to 210 ms.

**Opening and closing.** `/` opens the panel in read mode only, without Ctrl, Alt or Meta (Shift is allowed: on a German keyboard `/` is Shift+7), not while the large view of a diagram is open, which would lie over it (story 2.9), and not while the focus is in an input that takes text (any but a checkbox or a radio button, so a switch of the panel is no such input), a textarea, a select or an editable element, where the `/` is typed; it puts the focus into the panel's field. The close button "×" closes it, and so does Escape, which then does nothing else; where the focus was in the panel, it goes back to what had it when the panel opened, where that still stands in the page, else to the magnifier (story 5.16, finding M2 of the audit, where it fell to `<body>` and a reader with the keyboard had to start again from the top), and to the magnifier as well where that takes no focus now, hidden or in a closed `<details>`: to the magnifier when the magnifier opened it, or `/` from the body, to a link of the document when `/` was pressed on it, never to a result and never to a place of the document, which the search does not change, not even by a `tabindex`; with the focus outside the panel, closing leaves it where it is. That Escape does nothing else: the search's step comes before those of a table filter's field and of read mode in the one Escape listener, which stops the event, so read mode stays and a table filter's field keeps its term; only the next Escape leaves read mode; an open large view of a diagram is closed by Escape before the panel (see *Keys and events*). In the editor leaving read mode, in whatever way, closes the panel as well: "Editor ↩", or Escape with the panel closed. That close is `src/app.js`'s, which watches the class of `<body>`; `search.js` knows of no mode, it asks the predicate its caller hands it. A closed panel forgets its term and results; opened again it starts empty and reads the preview afresh. The switches keep their state as long as the page runs. `openSearch()` and `closeSearch()` are exported for the stories that call them.

**Keys in the panel** (story 5.16, finding N3 of the audit). Enter in the field follows the first result, as a click on it does, and the focus stays in the field; Down puts the focus on the first result, and on a result Down and Up go to the next and the previous one, across the groups, Up from the first back to the field. Both keys of the field first run a search that still waits for the pause, so a reader who types and presses Enter at once reaches what the term finds. Without a result they do nothing. The switches stay in the order of tabbing between the field and the list; Escape is as above. See *Keys and events*. `tests/vergleich.mjs` checks Enter from the end of the document, and Down, Down, Up, Up.

**Where it stands** (decision of 2026-10-03). Fixed on the right, below "Editor ↩", whose lower edge is at about 52 px: 64 px from the top, 16 px from the right and from the bottom, 380 px wide or the window less 32 px where that is narrower. It lies over the document, and from 1500 px over the rail while it is open (`z-index` 45, above the rail's 40 and the magnifier's 42 and below "Editor ↩" at 50, which it never covers). Where no rail stands beside the text, in a window up to about 1680 px, its upper edge lies over the lower part of the link "license information" (the link stands at 56 px, at the right edge of the text column); beside a rail the link is left of it.
