The results scroll inside the panel. It is not printed. Its styles are `src/search.css`, one source for the page and the exports, under plain names (`search-panel`, `search-result`, …), not `dokufix-` ones: the style check, which reads that file as a stylesheet outside the block, allows no other `dokufix-` names there. A closed panel is `hidden` and not shown; an open one is shown wherever it is, without asking for `body.mode-view`, because in the editor it is never open outside read mode.

**The magnifier** (story 10, FR48). The key `/` is no help to a reader who does not know it, so a button opens the panel as well: the magnifier, the 16 px Octicon "search" (`@primer/octicons` 19.38.0, see *Licence information*) in a white box of 35 × 35 px, as high as "Editor ↩" (35 px in Chromium 153), with its border and shadow, the symbol alone, the tooltip "Suchen (/)", `aria-label` "Suchen" and `aria-keyshortcuts` "/". A click opens the panel and puts the focus into its field, `openSearch()`; on an open panel it does no more than that, it closes nothing, and the term and its results stay; the panel closes by "×" and Escape as before. `registerSearch()` makes it at load beside the panel, a `<button>` appended to `<body>`, its SVG with `createElementNS`, with `data-dokufix-transient`: a saved `Mit Editor` file has none (`tests/speichern.mjs`, step 8), and `nur-lesen`, which runs no script, has none either. One element, placed by CSS alone, `src/search.css` and, in the editor, `src/app.css`, at the same place at every width, with a rail or without (Ben, 2026-10-04): fixed at the top right, in `schlank` and `kompakt` in the corner where "Editor ↩" stands in the editor, 16 px from the top and from the right (`src/search.css`); in the editor and a `Mit Editor` file left of "Editor ↩", 8 px from it, and only in read mode (`src/app.css`, in selectors that outweigh those of `src/search.css`, which stands behind it). "Editor ↩" is 80.8 px wide in Chromium 153 and Firefox 153, so the magnifier's right edge is 105 px from the window's there. Its lower edge is at 51 px, above the rail, which starts at 80 px, and above the link "license information" in a wide window, which stands at 56 px; up to 820 px, where the link stands at 40 px, the link moves left past it: in the editor 148 px from the right instead of 120, in the exports 59 px instead of 22 (`READONLY_FRAME_CSS`, which `nur-lesen` shares), and its view reaches back to the right edge of the text column. `z-index` 42: above the rail and the link (40), under the open panel (45), which covers it where they meet, and under the large view of a diagram (100). Not printed. `npm run pruefung` on 2026-10-04, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153, all runs green: reference document 843 and 838 assertions, demo text 864 and 859, save round trip 188, failure cases 910; `npm test` 603. Control (Chromium, demo text): with the magnifier 30 px from the corner in the exports and 95 px instead of 105 in the editor, over "Editor ↩", and with a click that closes an open panel, the run fails on exactly the magnifier's place and the second click in `Mit Editor`, `schlank` and `kompakt`, and on the licence link at 320, 600 and 820 px in `schlank` and `kompakt`, which the magnifier then overlaps: 8 checks.

**In `schlank` and `kompakt`** (story 5.3). Both files carry the reader bundle, `src/reader.js` (see *Tables*, the free-text filter, for how it is built and written into a file), every one of them, with a filter table or without. When it runs it gives each filter table its field, adds the panel's styles to the head of the file, a `<style>` behind the file's own stylesheet, and calls `registerSearch()` with `main.reader-body` as the root and read mode always on: `/` opens the panel as in the editor, not from a field, and "×" or Escape closes it; there is no read mode to leave. The panel stands where it stands in the editor, 64 px from the top, and the gap above it, where "Editor ↩" stands in the editor, stays empty. The file's own `<style>` does not carry the rules, and neither does the export frame (`READONLY_FRAME_CSS`): `nur-lesen`, which shares both, stays without `<script>` and without a rule of the panel. `kompakt` runs the bundle after it has unpacked its document, as it ran the filter's; it reads the block `#f` before it unpacks. In `schlank` the bundle runs behind the document and in front of the decoder of its diagrams. The decoder unpacks one diagram after the other, asynchronously, and when it has gone through all of them, unpacked or failed, it dispatches `dokufix-diagrams-unpacked` (`UNPACKED_EVENT` in `search.js`) on the document. Where `schlank` writes its decoder, it writes the content container with a mark beside it, `<main class="reader-body dokufix-doc" data-dokufix-unpacking>` (`UNPACKING_ATTR` in `search.js`), and the decoder takes the mark when it has gone through every diagram and source link, before it sends its event. A search that runs while the root carries the mark lists nothing, says "Diagramme werden entpackt …" in the summary, so the empty panel is explained, and runs on the event; a diagram the decoder could not unpack keeps its attribute, and the search runs all the same. Because the bundle stands in front of the decoder, it listens before the decoder can send. A file without diagrams and source links has no decoder and no mark and never waits; nor does any root without the mark, whatever it holds (story 5.16, finding M1 of the audit): until then the search waited while the root held a packed diagram, `.dokufix-diagram-svg[data-gz]`, and the editor, `Mit Editor` and `kompakt`, which have no decoder, waited for ever, without a word, over one line of raw HTML that wrote such an element. Packed markup an author writes is markup; the decoder decides. The bundle was 11 603 B (4 721 B gzipped) as story 5.3 built it, the filter's alone was 3 173 B; what that does to the files is under *Download variants*. Since story 2.9 it also registers the keys of the large view of a diagram (`registerLargeViewKeys()`, see *Diagrams*), 12 758 B (5 033 B gzipped); story 5.4 put the grouping into it, 15 342 B (5 888 B gzipped), and story 5.5 the highlight of the hits, 17 190 B (6 558 B gzipped); story 5.6 the table rows as places, with the hidden rows and the listener for a changed filter, 18 653 B (6 938 B gzipped); story 2.10 the picture button below a diagram (`attachSvgDownloads()`, see *Diagrams*, the downloads), 20 526 B (7 692 B gzipped), above the bound of 20 000 B `tests/build.test.mjs` set, which went to 25 000 B (Ben, 2026-10-03); story 2.11 the `change` the keys of the large view dispatch, 20 652 B (7 740 B gzipped); story 5.7 the diagrams as places, 21 574 B (8 091 B gzipped); story 5.8 the metadata panel and code blocks, with the click that opens the panel and centres a hit, 22 971 B (8 577 B gzipped); story 5.15 the diagram languages from one module, `src/app/diagram-kinds.js`, 22 978 B (8 571 B gzipped); story 5.10 the magnifier, 24 271 B (8 981 B gzipped), 24 251 characters, which is what the bound of 25 000 in `tests/build.test.mjs` counts: 749 left. Story 5.16 raised the bound to 30 000 characters before it added to the bundle (finding N6 of the audit of the search, `docs/audit-suchfunktion.md`): the bound guards against growth nobody decided, not against growth, and the stories of epic 5 still to come, 9, 11 and 14, need room of about 3 000 characters beyond what story 5.16 itself adds. Story 5.16 itself, the eleven findings and the fixes of its review, took the bundle to 26 363 B (9 663 B gzipped), 26 340 characters: 3 660 left under the bound. The built file grew by 4 303 B (311 453 → 315 756 B), the search's code being in the page's script as well as in the bundle. Story 5.9, one order for Escape, took the bundle to 26 736 B (9 810 B gzipped), 26 709 characters: 3 291 left. The built file grew by 696 B (316 292 → 316 988 B), the steps and the one listener in the page's script and in the bundle.

**What the search in `schlank` and `kompakt` did to documents** (2026-10-03, `tests/vergleich.mjs` with `--compare`, the built file before story 5.3 against the one after, both before story 2.8, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153; what the files are now is under *Download variants*). Reference document: all assertions green, all 68 screenshots pixel-identical, `nur-lesen` byte-identical, and `schlank` and `kompakt` identical outside their scripts; `Mit Editor` +8 780 B, `schlank` +8 493 B (the bundle's 8 430 B more and the decoder's 63), `kompakt` +8 430 B, in both browsers (sizes of `npm run pruefung` after the review fix that narrowed the search's wait to the decoder's diagrams; the screenshots and the byte comparison are of the run before it, whose bundle was 36 B shorter). Demo text: the 68 screenshots differ by the one sentence on the search in the exports, which moves what follows it down by 25 to 26 px. Control: the new sources with the demo text as it was give all 68 screenshots pixel-identical to before and the same increases. The comparison run itself fails on the old file in `schlank` and `kompakt`, where it now expects the search. `npm run pruefung`: reference document 538 and 533 assertions green, demo text 533 and 528, save round trip 134, failure cases 684.

### Keys and events

Every key the page or an export handles, and every event a module listens for beyond a click on its own control. A new handler goes into these tables, and before it is written the table says whether its key or event is taken in the same place and mode.

**Keys.**

| Key | Where, when | Handler | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ctrl+Enter, Cmd+Enter | editor, focus in the source (`#source`) | `src/app/editor.js`, `registerEditorInput()`, on the textarea | renders the preview; `preventDefault` |
| `/` | read mode (editor, `Mit Editor`), always in `schlank` and `kompakt`; not from a field that takes text, a select or an editable element, not with Ctrl, Alt or Meta | `src/app/search.js`, `registerSearch()`, document, bubble | opens the search panel and puts the focus into its field; `preventDefault`. With the large view of a diagram open, in read mode or edit mode, it opens nothing (`largeViewOpen()`, asked before read mode) and is still `preventDefault`ed, so Firefox opens no quick find over the view |
| Escape | large view of a diagram open (editor in either mode, `Mit Editor`, `schlank`, `kompakt`), wherever the focus is | `src/app/escape.js`, `registerEscape()`, **window, capture**, its first step: `largeViewStep` (`src/app/large-view.js`) | closes the view, the last open one; `preventDefault`, `stopPropagation`, as every step: no later step and no other listener sees the event. Focus on a step goes to the view's checkbox |
| `+`, `-` | large view of a diagram open; not with Ctrl, Alt or Meta | `src/app/large-view.js`, `registerLargeViewKeys()`, window, capture | the next, the previous zoom step, held at "200 %" and "Einpassen"; focus on a step moves to the step chosen; `preventDefault` |
| Escape | search panel open, wherever the focus is | `registerEscape()`, its second step: `searchStep` (`src/app/search.js`) | closes the panel, a term in its field included; `preventDefault`, `stopPropagation`. A focus in the panel goes back to what had it when the panel opened, else to the magnifier (story 5.16) |
| Enter | focus in the search panel's field; not with Ctrl, Alt, Meta or Shift | `src/app/search.js`, `buildPanel()`, on the field (story 5.16) | runs a search that still waits for the pause, then follows the first result as a click on it does; `preventDefault`. With no result nothing, and the key goes on |
| Down | focus in the search panel's field; not with Ctrl, Alt, Meta or Shift, so Shift+Down selects in the field | `src/app/search.js`, `buildPanel()`, on the field (story 5.16) | runs a search that still waits for the pause and puts the focus on the first result; `preventDefault` where there is one, else the key goes on |
| Down, Up | focus on a result of the search panel; not with Ctrl, Alt, Meta or Shift | `src/app/search.js`, `buildPanel()`, on the list (story 5.16) | the focus to the next result, to the previous one, from the first back to the field; on the last Down does nothing. `preventDefault`. The results are buttons: Enter and Space on one are the browser's click |
| Escape | focus in a table filter's field that holds text | `registerEscape()`, its third step: `filterFieldStep` (`src/app/filter.js`) | empties the field and fires `input` on it, as typing does, so its table is filtered anew; `preventDefault`, `stopPropagation`. In an empty field the step does not apply |
| Escape | download menu open (edit mode) | `registerEscape()` of the page, its fourth step: `menuStep` (`src/app/downloads/menu.js`) | closes the menu; `preventDefault`, `stopPropagation` |
| Escape | read mode in the editor or a `Mit Editor` file | `registerEscape()` of the page, its fifth step: `readModeStep` (`src/app/editor.js`) | leaves read mode, focus to the source; `preventDefault`, `stopPropagation` |
| Escape | version dialog open (edit mode) | the browser: `<dialog>` opened with `showModal()` (`src/app/persistence.js`); no step of `registerEscape()` applies then, so the event goes on to it | closes the dialog (its `cancel`) |
| Arrow keys, Space | focus on a facet control | the browser: radio buttons (`src/app/facets.js`, no script) | chooses a value; works without script |
| Space | focus on the checkbox of a diagram's large view | the browser: a checkbox (`src/app/diagrams.js`, no script) | opens and closes the view; works without script, in `nur-lesen` too |
| Arrow keys | focus on a zoom step of an open large view | the browser: radio buttons (`src/app/diagrams.js`, no script) | chooses a step; works without script |
| Ctrl+arrow keys (Cmd+arrow keys), with Shift faster | the live viewer of a BPMN diagram open (editor, `Mit Editor`), focus on its canvas | `bpmn-js`, its keyboard bound to its canvas SVG (story 2.11) | moves the diagram; `preventDefault`. Escape on the canvas goes to `registerEscape()` first, which stops it, `+` and `-` to `registerLargeViewKeys()` |
| Enter, Space | focus on the link "license information" | the browser: `<summary>` of `<details>` (`src/app/licences.js`, no listener) | opens and closes the view |

**The order of Escape** (story 5.9). One listener handles every Escape the page and the reader bundle know: `registerEscape(doc, steps)` in `src/app/escape.js`, on `window` in the capture phase, so it hears the key before every listener on the document and before the keyboard of `bpmn-js` on the live viewer's canvas. It is handed one list of steps, each a pair `applies(e)` and `close(e)`, both given the event, and `runEscape()` runs the first step that applies: that step closes, the event gets `preventDefault` and `stopPropagation`, and no later step runs. One Escape closes one thing. Where no step applies nothing happens and the event goes on, to a `<dialog>` of the browser for one. A key with `isComposing` and one already `defaultPrevented` run no step. `src/app.js` lists five steps, in this order: an open large view of a diagram, wherever the focus is (`largeViewStep`); an open search panel, wherever the focus is, which closes even when its field holds a term (`searchStep`; Ben, 2026-10-04, it does not empty the field first); a table filter's field that holds text, when the focus is in it (`filterFieldStep`); in edit mode, the download menu (`menuStep`); in read mode, read mode itself (`readModeStep`). `src/reader.js` lists the first three: `schlank` and `kompakt` have no menu and no read mode. The modules export their step and keep no Escape listener of their own. The download menu and read mode never apply at once, because the menu is in the toolbar, which read mode hides. So with a view and the search panel open, the first Escape closes the view, the second the panel, and in the editor the third leaves read mode; `tests/vergleich.mjs` checks that order in `Mit Editor`, `schlank` and `kompakt`, `tests/escape.test.mjs` the rule of the list and that no other module names the key. In `nur-lesen`, without a script, Escape does not close a view. A new Escape is a new step: it goes into the list at its place, into the table above and this paragraph, and gets a check in `tests/vergleich.mjs`.

**Events and observers.**

| Event, observer | Where | Handler | What for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `input` on `#source` | editor | `editor.js`, `registerEditorInput()` | autosave and the dirty-state badge |
| `paste`, `dragenter`, `dragover`, `dragleave`, `drop` | editor, the source and its pane | `src/app/assets.js`, `registerImageInput()` | images into the document |
| `click` on the document | editor | `editor.js`, `registerHamburger()`; `menu.js`, `registerDownloadMenu()` | a click outside closes the hamburger panel and the download menu; their buttons stop their own click |
| `click` on the inline table of contents and the rail | editor, `Mit Editor` | `src/app/toc.js`, `tocLinkHandler()`; `src/app/rail.js` | scrolls to the heading |
| `scroll`, `resize` on `window` (passive) | editor, `Mit Editor` | `src/app/rail.js` | the rail's current entry and its fit |
| `beforeunload` | editor | `src/app/assets.js`, `registerAssetUrlCleanup()` | revokes the object URLs of images |
| `MutationObserver` on the `class` of `<body>` | editor, `Mit Editor` | `src/app.js` | leaving read mode, in whatever way, closes the search panel |
| `MutationObserver` on the search's root (`childList`, `subtree`, `characterData`) | wherever the search runs, from its first search | `search.js`, `placesOf()` (story 5.16) | drops the places it keeps between searches when the root's nodes or text change; changes of attributes are passed over |
| `input` and `change` on the search panel's field and switches | wherever the search runs | `search.js` | searches, after 150 ms of no typing or at once on a switch |
| `input` on a table filter's field, `change` on its facet group | wherever a filter runs | `filter.js` | filters the rows |
| `input` and `change` on the document, from a table filter's field or a facet control in the root | wherever the search runs | `search.js`, `registerSearch()`, bubble, after the filter's own listeners | while the panel is open and holds a term, searches again after 150 ms, so the mark "(ausgeblendet)" of a row follows the filter |
| `click` on the magnifier of the search | read mode (editor, `Mit Editor`), always in `schlank` and `kompakt` | `src/app/search.js`, `registerSearch()`, on the button | opens the search panel and puts the focus into its field; on an open panel it only puts the focus there. Its own control, a `<button>` with no key of its own (Enter and Space are the browser's); the click goes on to the document's click listeners, which close the hamburger panel and the download menu, both hidden in read mode |
| `dokufix-diagrams-unpacked` on the document | `schlank` | dispatched by the decoder of `src/app/downloads/readonly-slim.js` after its last diagram and its last source link (story 2.10), unpacked or failed, once it has taken the mark `data-dokufix-unpacking` from `<main>`; heard by `search.js` | a search over a root with the mark waits for it, and runs on it while the panel is open and holds a term |
| `input` dispatched on `#source` | editor | `assets.js`, after inserting an image | runs the `input` listeners above |
| `change` on the checkbox of a BPMN figure's large view; `change` and `click` on its radio buttons | editor, `Mit Editor` | `src/app/live-viewer.js`, `attachLiveViewers()`, on the controls (story 2.11) | opens and closes the live viewer, sets its scale; `click` as well, so a step chosen again applies again. Nothing is prevented: the controls work as without it |
| `change` dispatched on the checkbox or a radio button of a large view | wherever `src/app/large-view.js` runs (editor, `Mit Editor`, `schlank`, `kompakt`) | `closeLargeView()`, `stepLargeView()`, bubbling, after setting the property | Escape, `+` and `-` change a control as a click does, so whoever follows the view hears every way; heard by the live viewer. The `change` listener of `search.js` on the document lets it pass, as it takes only the controls of a table filter |
| `touchstart`, `touchmove`, `touchend`, `touchcancel` on the viewer's container | the live viewer open, a touch screen | `src/app/live-viewer.js`, `attachTouch()` (story 2.23) | one finger moves the diagram, two zoom it at their middle (`touchStep()`, `canvas.scroll()` and `canvas.zoom()`); the fingers are read from all touches, `e.touches`, those that began in the container, paired by identifier (`countedFingers()`), not from `e.targetTouches`, which splits two fingers on two shapes; `touchmove` is prevented (not passive), so the page neither scrolls nor zooms; a tap stays a tap. The listeners go with the container |
| `wheel` on the viewer's container; `mousedown` on its canvas, then `mousemove` and `mouseup` on the document while dragging; `keydown` on its canvas; `click` on its logo | the live viewer open | `bpmn-js` itself (story 2.11) | Ctrl+wheel zooms at the pointer, the wheel alone moves the diagram; a drag with the left button moves it from 15 px on, with the class `djs-cursor-grab` on `<body>`; Ctrl+arrow keys move it; the logo opens the library's lightbox in `<body>`. All of it ends with the viewer, the lightbox and the class by name (see *Diagrams*) |
| `click` on the picture button below a diagram | editor, `Mit Editor`, `schlank`, `kompakt` | `src/app/diagram-downloads.js`, `attachSvgDownloads()`, on the button | saves the picture of the diagram as `.svg`. Its own control, a `<button>` with no key of its own (Enter and Space are the browser's); it stands outside the stage label and the view, so the click opens no view and reaches no key handler; the click of the hidden link it hands the file to reaches the document's click listeners, which close the hamburger panel and the download menu, as every download does. The source link beside it is the browser's: a link with `download`, no listener |

`kompakt` runs no listener of its own: its decoder unpacks the document and then runs the reader bundle. `nur-lesen` runs no script; what works there is the browser's: the large view of a diagram opens, zooms and closes by its checkbox and radio buttons there too.

### Version history

The history block (`#dokufix-history`) shape:

```json
{
  "uuid": "987d2a3b-d26c-4d07-b3ff-b4e3ae0c6615",
  "version": 3,
  "history": [
    { "v": 1, "t": "2026-05-13T14:32:00.000Z", "m": "Erste Fassung",   "s": "<gzip+base64>" },
    { "v": 2, "t": "2026-05-13T15:45:00.000Z", "m": "",                "s": "<gzip+base64>" },
    { "v": 3, "t": "2026-05-14T09:12:00.000Z", "m": "Audit ergänzt",   "s": "<gzip+base64>" }
  ]
}
```

The `s` field of each entry is `gzipB64()` of the markdown source at that version — captured at commit time. Every prior version is fully recoverable from the file alone (basis of the audit story).

Two ways to create a new entry:

1. **"Mit Editor" download (canonical save).** Asks for an optional message via `prompt()`. Cancel aborts entirely; Enter on an empty input proceeds without a message. The counter bumps, the entry lands in the history list (including a gzipped snapshot of the current source), and the file is emitted with everything baked in. Counter increment and persistence happen *after* `triggerDownload` returns, so a popup-blocker or serialization failure can't leave a phantom version with no on-disk artifact.
2. **Commit-only (button in the version modal).** Same prompt, same bump, same history append — but no file is produced. The new state is persisted to `localStorage` (under the UUID-scoped versions key) so it survives a reload. Later "Mit Editor" downloads pick up all accumulated commits in a single file.

Both flows are **skipped silently when the source matches the last committed/saved state** (the `commitBaseline`). Clicking "Mit Editor" on an unchanged document re-emits the same version without a prompt or counter bump; clicking the commit button when nothing has changed shake-animates instead of prompting. By design, every prompt corresponds to actual uncommitted changes.

On load, version state comes from whichever of two sources is more recent: the file's baked-in `<script type="application/json" id="dokufix-history">` block, or `localStorage`. localStorage wins when commit-onlys have happened since the last download. History entries are shape-validated on load (well-formed `{v, t}` minimum); malformed entries are silently filtered out rather than crashing init. `null`, negative, or fractional `version` values normalize to `0`. Files without the history block (older dokufix files predating this feature) load as `version: 0, history: []`; the first save bootstraps the history at `v1`.

Every `<` in commit messages or source snapshots is written as `\u003c` before it goes into the JSON block, so a `</script>` in a message cannot close the block early, and a `<!--` followed by `<script` cannot keep it from closing (see *`</script>` escaping*).

If `localStorage` setItem fails (quota exhausted, private-mode disabled), the version badge gains a red `.persist-failed` class with a pulse animation and an accessible title attribute — silent data loss is no longer possible.

Read-only downloads do *not* bump the version — they ship a `Version N · DD.MM.YYYY HH:MM · message · exportiert <date>` footer at the end of the document. Files with `version: 0` still emit a footer carrying just the export timestamp (the spec wants timestamps as always-present metadata). For the `kompakt` variant the footer lives inside the gzip payload so it survives decompression.

### Dirty-state baseline

The dirty indicator compares the live editor against `cleanBaseline`, which is set to the file's document, or to the demo text where the file has none, at load time — i.e. whatever is actually baked into *this* HTML file on disk. Consequences:

- An IndexedDB draft that differs from the baked content reads as **geändert** immediately on open. That's the intended "you have unsynced work" signal after a crash or tab close.
- A successful "Mit Editor" download resets `cleanBaseline` to the just-saved content → badge flips to clean. The downloaded HTML file also carries the new baseline, so the receiver opens it as clean.
- The other download variants (read-only) don't touch the baseline, since they don't carry editable source out the door.

### Persistence (IndexedDB)

One database per origin, named `dokufix-v1`, with two object stores:

- **`docs`** (keyPath: `uuid`) — one record per dokufix file: `{ uuid, source, version, history, commitBaseline, updatedAt }`. The whole record is rewritten on every persist; markdown + history JSON is small enough that this is cheap.
- **`assets`** (keyPath: `hash`) — image Blobs, keyed by SHA-256 hex. Content-addressed → uploading the same image twice deduplicates automatically; no GC needed in the PoC.

On first load of a doc whose UUID has a pre-existing `dokufix-doc-<uuid>-source` / `…-versions` pair in the old localStorage layout, the data is migrated into the IDB doc record and the legacy keys are deleted. Idempotent — the migration only fires when no IDB record exists yet for the UUID.

The heading-numbering preference (`dokufix-poc-numbering`) deliberately stays in localStorage. It's doc-independent UX state and has no business cluttering the per-document IDB record.

If IndexedDB is unavailable (some browser private modes, restrictive site settings), the init code surfaces a banner and continues in a degraded read-only mode — the baked document or demo text is still loaded into the editor so the user can read what they just opened, but `persistDoc` calls will keep flipping the persist-failed flag. There is no localStorage fallback in this PoC, since storing images there would be a non-starter anyway. The banner lies over the top of the page, the "Editor" button included, so it has a close button; closing hides it for as long as the page is open.

### Image assets

Images live in IndexedDB and are referenced from the markdown source via `![alt](#asset-<sha256>)`. Three input pathways converge on a single pipeline:

1. **Paste** — clipboard `paste` event on the textarea. Captures `it.kind === 'file' && it.type.startsWith('image/')`. Lets normal text paste through untouched.
2. **Drag & drop** — `dragenter`/`dragover`/`drop` on the source pane. A drop overlay highlights the target during the drag.
3. **`+ Bild` button** — hidden `<input type="file" accept="image/*" multiple>` triggered by a toolbar button.

The pipeline: `createImageBitmap({ imageOrientation: 'from-image' })` decodes and applies EXIF orientation. Two size guards run before allocating the canvas: a 5 MB per-file input cap (cheap to check, blocks pathological inputs early) and a 25-megapixel decoded-pixel cap (a 4.9 MB heavily-compressed JPEG can decode to 12000×8000 = 96 MP and OOM the tab during `drawImage`; the encoded-byte cap doesn't protect against that). If the bitmap is wider than 1600 px, it's downscaled. The result is drawn to an `OffscreenCanvas` and re-encoded as WebP @ 0.85 (fallback to `<canvas>.toBlob` if OffscreenCanvas isn't available). The output bytes are SHA-256-hashed via `crypto.subtle.digest`; the hex digest becomes both the IDB key and the markdown reference. The decoded `ImageBitmap` is released via `bitmap.close()` in a `finally` block so a failed encoding pass doesn't leak the native buffer. Alt-text derived from the filename is sanitized — characters that would otherwise break out of the markdown image syntax (`[`, `]`, `(`, `)`, `\`, `` ` ``, `<`, `>`) are stripped, so a malicious filename like `x](http://attacker.com/track.png).png` can't inject an attacker-controlled `src`.

**Render-time resolution.** After `marked.parse(md)`, the HTML string is run through `resolveAssetRefsInHtml`, which `idbBatchGetAssets`-fetches every `#asset-<hash>` referenced in the document. Each match is rewritten to a Blob URL via `URL.createObjectURL`. Unresolved hashes get a transparent 1×1 GIF data URL as the `src` plus a `data-missing-asset` attribute; CSS turns those into a red "Bild fehlt" placeholder. (Using `src=""` for missing assets would trigger the browser to fetch the document URL itself, which is a footgun.) After each render, `pruneAssetUrlCache` revokes Blob URLs whose hashes are no longer referenced from the source — without this, every distinct image inserted in a session would hold its decoded pixels in memory until tab close. The missing-asset rule is a document style, so every read-only export carries it too: a recipient who opens an export with a stale or missing asset sees the same "Bild fehlt" placeholder rather than an empty image element.

**Heading-slug guard.** Heading anchors and asset references share the `#`-fragment namespace. `slugify` explicitly rejects any heading slug that would start with `asset-` (or be exactly `asset`), prefixing it to `h-asset-…`. Without this, a heading literally titled "Asset Inventory" could otherwise be resolved as an image ref.

**Baking on "Mit Editor" download.** `bakeAssetsForDocument` collects every asset hash referenced by the current source, *by every history snapshot* (each snapshot is gzipped markdown — we decompress and re-scan) *and by the demo text*, so that "Demo zurücksetzen" shows the demo text's images in every saved file (Ben, 2026-10-03; every `Mit Editor` file carries them, about 60 KB for the photo today). An asset that IndexedDB does not hold, or every asset when IndexedDB is not there, is taken from the page's own `#dokufix-assets` block if it is there: a file passes on what it carries, and a page without storage still saves its demo text's images. `tests/speichern.mjs` saves a file with a document that names no image, opens it with fresh storage, resets the demo text and expects every image of the demo text shown; control: with the demo text left out of the bake, that check and the two checks of the saved blocks fail. Every matched Blob is Base64-encoded into a JSON map `{ hash → { m: mime, d: base64 } }` and serialized into the `<script type="application/json" id="dokufix-assets">` block in the document clone. If the bake fails (transaction abort, base64 conversion error on a corrupt asset), the download is aborted with a user-facing message rather than silently emitting a broken file. On the receiver's first open, `seedAssetsFromBakedBlock` parses that block and writes each entry into IndexedDB — but only after **re-hashing the decoded bytes** and verifying the result matches the asserted hash. Mismatched entries are logged and skipped: a hostile sender can't pair an attacker-supplied blob with an arbitrary lookup key. Content-addressed dedup keeps re-seeds of identical hashes idempotent.

**The images of the demo text** lie in `src/assets/`, each named by the SHA-256 of its bytes: `src/assets/5d04449a…b556.webp`, 640 × 435 px, 45 128 B, metadata stripped. The demo text refers to it as every inserted image is referred to, `![…](#asset-<sha256>)`, and the build writes every file of the folder into the `#dokufix-assets` block of the built file, `{ "<sha256>": { "m": "image/webp", "d": "<base64>" } }`, in the order of the names, so a second build is byte-identical. On first open `seedAssetsFromBakedBlock` puts it into IndexedDB like any baked asset. The build fails when a file's bytes do not give its name (the page would refuse the entry), when a name is not `<sha256>.<ext>` of `.webp`, `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg` or `.gif`, and when the demo text refers to an `#asset-` the folder does not hold. The photo: Daria Yakovleva (Pixabay), via Wikimedia Commons, CC0 1.0; the demo text names author, source and licence below it. It is content of the demo text, not a tool of the file, so it is not in the licence information.

**Read-only export inlining.** `inlineAssetRefsAsDataUrls` runs on the cloned preview HTML before serialization for all three read-only variants. It handles both `src="#asset-<hash>"` (unresolved) and `src="blob:<url>"` (already resolved by the live render pass — the cache's reverse map gives back the hash). Output is `src="data:image/webp;base64,…"`. Missing assets keep the same transparent placeholder + `data-missing-asset` shape used in the live preview, so the receiver sees the same "Bild fehlt" treatment in the export. For the `kompakt` variant this is further gzipped along with the rest of the body; binary image bytes don't compress meaningfully a second time, but the Base64 envelope itself shrinks by ~30 %.

### Compression

All compression uses native browser **`CompressionStream('gzip')`** — no library dependency.

Brotli would compress 15–25% better, but is currently *missing* from `CompressionStream` in Chrome / Edge as of May 2026 (Firefox 147 and Safari 18.4 have shipped it). We stay on gzip until Chrome catches up. Migration is a one-line swap.

### `</script>` escaping

Template literals embedded inside the main `<script>` element use `<\/script>` to avoid the HTML parser prematurely closing the outer script tag. Classic gotcha — escape both the payload-holder tag and the inner decoder script.

The build guards the same thing twice. esbuild writes `<\/script` wherever it meets `</script` in the script, and `build.mjs` refuses to write a file whose minified script still contains `</script` or whose minified styles contain `</style`.

`<!--` is the second trap, and the quieter one. Inside a script element, `<!--` followed by `<script` keeps the real `</script>` from closing the element: the page's script then never runs, and a data block swallows the blocks behind it. The script contains `<script` in the templates of the read-only exports, and esbuild does not guard this case; minifying, it even prints `"<"+"!--"` and `"\x3c!--"` as `"<!--"` again. So `build.mjs` writes every `<!--` of the bundled script as `\x3c!--`, which is the same character in a string, a template and a regular expression, and refuses a script that still contains one. In the data blocks every `<` is written as `\u003c`: by the build for the demo text, by `encodeJsonForScript()` for everything a save writes. A demo text, a document or a version description may therefore contain `</script>`, `<!--` and `<script>`.

### Self-replication mechanism

"Mit Editor" clones the page (`document.documentElement.cloneNode(true)`), takes out what belongs to the running page, writes four data blocks of the clone through the DOM, and serialises the clone. What is taken out: every element marked `data-dokufix-transient` (see *Render passes*), the two elements of the link "license information" among them, what a diagram library leaves in `<body>` (Mermaid's `.mermaidTooltip`, and from the live viewer of a BPMN diagram the lightbox of the `bpmn-js` logo, `.bjs-powered-by-lightbox`, and its cursor class `djs-cursor-*` on `<body>`, all named by `removeRendererLeftovers()` in `src/app/diagrams.js`), the rendered preview and the rail; and state on elements of the page itself is reset one by one: an open menu, the open hamburger panel of a narrow window, disabled download buttons, the dirty mark with the title and the badge's text, the drop highlight, the storage banner with its reason, the warning on the version mark. The data blocks:

| Block | What a save writes into it |
|---|---|
| `#dokufix-history` | UUID, version and history, each entry with its gzipped source |
| `#dokufix-assets` | every image the source or a history entry refers to |
| `#dokufix-demo` | `{"gz": …}`, the demo text; taken over as it is from a file that already carried it gzipped |
| `#dokufix-source` | `{"gz": …}`, the current source |

The receiver's async init reads both text blocks before the first render. The script travels as it is: in a saved file, and in a file saved from that one, it is the script of `dist/dokufix.html` byte for byte. `tests/speichern.mjs` checks exactly that, and that both generations hold their document.

A saved file is the built file with other data blocks, and nothing else: that is what keeps a file from growing with every generation. `tests/speichern.mjs` compares the two as a whole and allows five differences, each listed with its reason (see *Save round trip*). Leftovers were found that way on 2026-10-02 and removed: the badge still read "geändert" in a file that opens clean, and Mermaid's tooltip element came along; a file saved in a narrow window opened with the hamburger panel open; a file saved while IndexedDB was unavailable carried the sender's storage reason and the warning on the version mark. Heading numbering travels with a saved file on purpose (the class `numbered` on `<body>` and the state of its button); that is among the five.

**Why not as the PoC did it.** The PoC kept both texts in the script, as `DEMO`/`DEMO_GZ` and `SAMPLE`/`SAMPLE_GZ` between comment marks, and a save rewrote the text of the script between those marks with a regular expression. That works as long as the script in the file is the script as written. Measured on 2026-10-02: once esbuild touches the script, saving breaks, because esbuild removes the comment marks; minified, the saved file opens without an error and carries the demo text instead of the document. Hence the rule for everything that follows: no code searches or rewrites script text. What a save has to change lives in a data block.

### `<script type="text/plain">` payload

The "kompakt" variant ships gzip+base64-encoded HTML inside a `<script type="text/plain">` element. This works because the browser does not execute the script (wrong MIME), but the contents are accessible via `textContent`. Decoder reads, decompresses via `DecompressionStream`, sets `innerHTML`.

## Build

```
npm install        # once, in the repository root: esbuild, eslint, globals, linkedom and playwright-core, all pinned exactly
npm run build      # writes dist/dokufix.html and dist/dokufix-showcase.html
npm run watch      # writes dist/dokufix.dev.html, readable, again on every change under src/
```

`build.mjs` puts eight sources into one file, and with `src/showcase.md` in place of `src/demo.md` into a second, the showcase:

| Source | What it is | What the build does with it |
|---|---|---|
| `src/index.html` | the page: head, markup, the data blocks, the three CDN tags | taken as written, not minified |
| `src/doc.css` | the document styles | minified, into `<style id="dokufix-doc-css">` |
| `src/app.css` | the editor's styles | minified, into the `<style>` after it |
| `src/search.css` | the styles of the search panel | minified once, behind the editor's styles in the same `<style>`, and into the reader bundle as the string `SEARCH_CSS` (an esbuild `define`), which adds it to an exported file when that opens (see *Search*) |
| `src/app.js` with `src/app/` | the script: the entry and the modules it imports | bundled into one IIFE and minified |
| `src/demo.md` | the demo text, plain Markdown | written as `{"text": …}` into `#dokufix-demo` |
| `src/showcase.md` | the showcase text, plain Markdown | written as `{"text": …}` into `#dokufix-demo` of the showcase, in place of `src/demo.md`; checked against `src/assets/` the same way |
| `src/reader.js` with `src/app/filter.js` and `src/app/search.js` | the reader bundle of `schlank` and `kompakt`: the table filter and the search | bundled into a second IIFE, minified also by `--dev`, into `<script type="text/plain" id="dokufix-reader-js">`, where it does not run (see *Tables*, the free-text filter, and *Search*) |
| `src/assets/` | the images of the demo text, each named `<sha256>.<ext>` | written as `{"<sha256>": {"m": …, "d": base64}}` into `#dokufix-assets`, checked against its name and against what the demo text names (see *Image assets*) |

The page names six of the others once each, as a slot: `{{slot:doc.css}}`, `{{slot:app.css}}`, `{{slot:app.js}}`, `{{slot:reader.js}}`, `{{slot:demo.md}}`, `{{slot:assets}}`; `src/search.css` has no slot of its own, it goes into `app.css`'s and into the reader bundle.

- **The built files are committed.** Two builds of the same sources are byte-identical, so `git status` stays clean after `npm run build` unless a source changed. Change a source, build, commit the source and both files. Never edit `dist/dokufix.html` or `dist/dokufix-showcase.html` by hand; the next build overwrites them.
- **The showcase** is a second `build()` of the same sources with `src/showcase.md` as the demo text (both run together: `node build.mjs --check` takes about 0.17 s instead of 0.155 s, measured 2026-10-04). It equals `dist/dokufix.html` outside the block `#dokufix-demo`; it carries every image of `src/assets/`, like the built file. Both are built before either is written, so a source that breaks one leaves both as they were. With `--out` (the tests) only that file is written or checked; the showcase then only where `--showcase-out <file>` names it.
- **`node build.mjs --check`** builds in memory, writes nothing, and exits 1 when `dist/dokufix.html` or `dist/dokufix-showcase.html` is missing ("is missing") or not what the sources give ("is stale"), naming each one.
- **The build exits 1 and writes nothing** when a source is missing, when a slot is missing from the page, stands there twice or is not one of the six, when an image in `src/assets/` does not match its name or the demo text or the showcase text names one that is not there (the message names the text), when the minified script or the reader bundle contains `</script` or `<!--` or a minified stylesheet `</style` (`search.css` named as itself), and when the modules do not fit together: one imports a name the other does not export, or a file that is not there, or assigns to a name it imported. The message names the module, line and name.
- **`node build.mjs --dev`** writes a second file, `dist/dokufix.dev.html`: the same page with script and styles not minified, and with a source map at the end of the script, as a `data:` URL, so it is still one file. It is for reading and debugging: the browser's debugger shows the modules under `src/app/` by name. The file is in `.gitignore`. It is of the demo text only: `--dev` builds no showcase and refuses `--showcase-out`. `--dev` refuses to write `dist/dokufix.html` or `dist/dokufix-showcase.html`, `--check` does not look at the readable file, and the two options do not go together. **`npm run watch`** is `node build.mjs --dev --watch`: it builds the readable file and builds it again whenever a file under `src/` changes; a build that fails prints why, leaves the last file, and the watch goes on.
- **Node.** `package.json` names what the tools need under `engines`: `^20.19.0 || ^22.13.0 || >=24`, the range of ESLint 10. The watch uses recursive `fs.watch`, the comparison run `zlib.crc32`; both are in that range.
- **`<!--` in the script is written as `\x3c!--`** after bundling; why is under *`</script>` escaping*.
- **esbuild 0.28.2**, through its API: `bundle`, `format: 'iife'`, `minify` and `charset: 'utf8'` for the script, `minify` and `charset: 'utf8'` for the three stylesheets, and `define` for the panel's styles in the reader bundle. Without `charset: 'utf8'` esbuild writes every non-ASCII character as an escape.
- **The code in the file is minified**, in the built file and in every `Mit Editor` file saved from it. The readable code is here, under `src/`.
- **The script's names are not global any more.** Bundled into an IIFE, `render()` or `initDone` cannot be reached from outside. Nothing in the page needs that; a check that wants to know what the page is doing looks at the DOM (see *Comparison run*).
- **The libraries are not embedded.** `marked`, `marked-footnote`, Mermaid and `bpmn-js` stay on the CDN at their pinned versions and stay free globals of the script (see *Libraries*).
- **The script is written as ES modules** and still arrives as one script. See *The script's modules*.

**What the build did to documents** (2026-10-02, `tests/vergleich.mjs`, PoC against built file, reference document and demo text, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153, light and dark, 1400 and 1600 px, at rest and with every state switched on): all 136 screenshots are pixel-identical, and all assertions are green in both. The same run on the PoC gives the screenshots the PoC's own harness gave before (`poc/tests/out/03-nach-review`, all 136 identical, byte counts equal), so the successor sees what its predecessor saw. Sizes are under *Download variants*.

### The script's modules

Until story 2.14 the script was one file of 2111 lines in which every function and every variable was visible to every other. It became `src/app.js` and nineteen modules under `src/app/`, cut along the sections the one file had; story 2.15 added five and renamed one, and stories 2.2, 2.18 and 2.3 added one each, story 2.4 four, story 2.5 two, story 2.6 one, story 2.7 two, story 5.1 three, stories 2.8 and 2.9 one each, story 2.10 one and story 2.11 one, so there are forty-three. esbuild bundles them into the one script of the built file, as before. The split moved code and rewrote none: every line of the old file stands in a module, apart from the three things described below (how shared state is reached, what runs at load, three comments that said "above" or "at the bottom").

| Module | What it owns |
|---|---|
| `app.js` | the entry: the library setup (`mermaid.initialize` where Mermaid is there, `marked.use`), the call of every `register…()` in a fixed order, the async init |
| `app/state.js` | `state`, the values that more than one module assigns to |
| `app/dom.js` | the elements more than one module works on: `sourceEl`, `previewEl`, `btnEl`, `resetEl` |
| `app/gzip.js` | `gzipB64()`, `ungzipB64()` |
| `app/idb.js` | the IndexedDB layer (`openDB()`, `idbGetDoc()` and the others) and the storage-error banner |
| `app/assets.js` | images: the pipeline from a file to a stored asset, resolving `#asset-` references in the rendered HTML, inlining them for the exports, baking and seeding them for `Mit Editor`; and the three ways an image comes in (paste, drop, `+ Bild`) |
| `app/document.js` | reading a data block (`readTextBlock()`), the document's identity (`generateDocUuid()`, `fallbackUuidFromLocation()`) |
| `app/persistence.js` | loading and persisting the document record, the migration from localStorage, versions and the history dialog, the dirty state |
| `app/html.js` | `escapeHtml()` |
| `app/warning.js` | `buildWarning()`, the warning a reader sees where something could not be rendered |
| `app/passes.js` | `runPasses()`, the runner of a list of passes, each in its own containment |
| `app/transient.js` | the attribute `data-dokufix-transient` and `removeTransient()` |
| `app/frontmatter.js` | the YAML subset, `splitFrontmatter()`, the metadata panel as a pass, `deriveDocTitle()` |
| `app/callouts.js` | callouts as a pass: recognising the alert marker of a blockquote, the callout element with its label; the class by which `toc.js` leaves out headings inside one |
| `app/chips.js` | status chips as a pass: what counts as a status (`readChip()`), the chip element with its word for assistive technology; the class by which `toc.js` leaves that word out of a heading's label |
| `app/nodes.js` | what the marker components ask of a node: the `nodeType` values, `isBlank()`, `firstReal()`, and `itemHost()`, where the content of a list item starts |
| `app/markers.js` | block markers as a pass: reading a comment as a marker (`readMarker()`), the list of known markers (`MARKERS`) with the block each expects, what a marker comes to (`judgeMarker()`), applying them and the warnings |
| `app/cards.js` | cards: the entry for the list of markers, and `buildCards()`, which marks the list and the title of each card |
| `app/steps.js` | step lists: the entry for the list of markers, and `buildSteps()`, which writes the number of each item and marks its actor |
| `app/facets.js` | the facet filter: the entry for the list of markers, `FACET_MAX`, the value of a cell, finding the column, grouping the values (`planFacets()`, which the comparison run asks as well), and `buildFacets()`, which puts the controls above the table and the keys on its rows, or refuses it with a reason |
| `app/tables.js` | tables as a pass: the wrapper every table gets, and the class of a sub-line |
| `app/filter.js` | the free-text filter: the entry for the list of markers, which marks the table (`markFilter()`), the text of a row, the match and the counter (`filterRowText()`, `filterMatches()`, `filterCountText()`, which the comparison run asks as well), the run-time pass that puts the search field above the table (`attachTableFilters()`, which `schlank` and `kompakt` run as well, from the reader bundle of `src/reader.js`), and the export steps that show every row (`showFilteredRows()`) and take the table's mark out of `nur-lesen` (`removeFilterMarks()`) |
| `app/search-match.js` | the search: where a term occurs in a place's text, with case and light fuzzy as options (`findHits()`), whether a term is too short (`tooShort()`), and the part of the text a result shows (`excerpt()`) |
| `app/search-places.js` | the search: the places of the preview, or of an export's content, their text and its map onto the text nodes (`collectPlaces()`), the ranges of a hit in the document (`nodeRanges()`), and the results under their group headings (`groupResults()`) |
| `app/search.js` | the search panel of read mode: the key `/`, the magnifier, the results, the highlight of the hits in the document (`CSS.highlights`), `openSearch()`, `closeSearch()`, `registerSearch()` with the root and the read mode of its caller, the page or the reader bundle, and the wait for the decoder of `schlank` (`UNPACKED_EVENT`) |
| `app/licences.js` | the licence information: the list of notices (`NOTICES`), the licence texts (`LICENCE_TEXTS`), and `licencesHtml()`, the link with its view as markup |
| `app/render.js` | `render()`: parse, the two pass lists (`DOCUMENT_PASSES`, `RUNTIME_PASSES`), the rail; one render at a time |
| `app/diagrams.js` | the figure every diagram stands in, with the controls of its large view (story 2.9), and its title (`diagramTitle()`), the width as drawn (`drawnWidth()`), the kinds with their renderers, the pass `Diagramme`, and what a library leaves in `<body>` for the save to take out (story 2.7; the lightbox and cursor class of `bpmn-js`, story 2.11) |
| `app/diagram-kinds.js` | the languages a fenced block is drawn in, `mermaid` and `bpmn`, each with what the search calls a diagram of it (`DIAGRAM_LANGUAGES`, story 5.15): the one source of these names; `diagrams.js` keys its renderers by them, and the search reads them, so the reader bundle carries the names and no renderer |
| `app/diagram-downloads.js` | the downloads below a diagram (story 2.10): the names of the files (`diagramFileNames()`), the source as a `data:` URL (`sourceDataUrl()`), the line with the source link (`downloadsLine()`), which the pass `Diagramme` writes, the picture button as a run-time pass (`attachSvgDownloads()`, which `schlank` and `kompakt` run as well, from the reader bundle), and the picture made at the click (`pictureOf()`, `resolveCustomProperties()`) |
| `app/large-view.js` | the keys of the large view of a diagram: Escape as the step `largeViewStep`, `+` and `-` on `window` in the capture phase (`registerLargeViewKeys()`), each dispatching `change` on the control it set (story 2.11), and `largeViewOpen()`, which the search asks; in the page and in the reader bundle (story 2.9) |
| `app/escape.js` | the one Escape listener (story 5.9): `registerEscape(doc, steps)` on `window` in the capture phase, and `runEscape()`, which runs the first step that applies and stops the event; the steps come from their modules, the order from `src/app.js` and `src/reader.js`; in the page and in the reader bundle |
| `app/live-viewer.js` | the live viewer in the large view of a BPMN diagram (story 2.11): the XML of the source link (`sourceXml()`), the diagram it opens (`diagramToOpen()`), the viewbox of each zoom step (`stepViewbox()`), the fingers that count on a touch screen and a move of them (`countedFingers()`, `touchStep()`, story 2.23), and the run-time pass `BPMN-Ansicht` (`attachLiveViewers()`), which starts and destroys the viewer |
| `app/bpmn.js` | BPMN diagrams: the renderer with its host, its refusals and the classes by type (`addBpmnTypeClasses()`, which the live viewer uses too), the viewer's configuration, `finishBpmnSvg()`, the credit (story 2.7); for XML without coordinates the layout's browser part, Mermaid's rendering in the host and what its SVG says (story 2.8) |
| `app/bpmn-layout.js` | BPMN without coordinates: reading the XML into lanes, flow nodes and flows (`readProcess()`, which the comparison run asks as well), the Mermaid text, the geometry with the routing corrections, the diagram part written into the author's XML, `MERMAID_LAYOUT_VERSION` (story 2.8) |
| `app/toc.js` | `documentHeadings()`, heading ids and the inline `[[toc]]` as passes, `headingLabelText()`, the label of a heading for its entries and its anchor, `tocLinkHandler()` and the run-time pass that attaches it |
| `app/footnotes.js` | footnote previews and return paths as passes; also `buildStaticRailHtml()`, which stood in that section of the one file |
| `app/rail.js` | the scrollspy rail |
| `app/editor.js` | the toolbar: render button and typing, "Demo zurücksetzen", heading numbering, hamburger, the switch between view and editor; the link "license information", put into the toolbar and into the page of read mode |
| `app/downloads/menu.js` | the download menu: it opens, closes and starts the download that was chosen |
| `app/downloads/download.js` | what every download needs: `safeFilenameBase()`, `triggerDownload()`, the in-flight gate |
| `app/downloads/with-editor.js` | `Mit Editor` |
| `app/downloads/export-body.js` | what the three read-only exports share: `buildExportBody()` with the export steps, the export frame, `readonlyCss()`, the footer |
| `app/downloads/readonly-open.js`, `readonly-slim.js`, `readonly-compact.js` | one read-only export each: its template, and what only it does to the copy |

**Five rules.**

- *A module imports what it uses and exports what others need.* Nothing is shared as a global, and no `window.` or `globalThis.` property passes a value from one module to another. Imports are named (`import { render } from './render.js'`), never `import * as`; the lint fails on one, see *Lint*.
- *Shared state is one object.* An imported name cannot be assigned to. So a value belongs in `state` when a module other than the one it belongs to assigns to it. That is true of nine: `docUuid`, `currentVersion`, `versionHistory`, `commitBaseline`, `storedSource` and `cleanBaseline`, which the async init in `src/app.js` sets and, all but `storedSource`, `Mit Editor` as well; and `demoText`, `demoGz` and `initDone`, which only the async init sets, for the modules that read them. They are the properties of `state` in `app/state.js`; a module imports `state` and reads and writes `state.currentVersion`, which a search finds. A value that only its own module assigns to stays a `let` there (`_dbPromise`, `saveTimer`, `persistFailedFlag`, `mermaidId`, the rail's two handlers, `downloadInFlight`). Such a `let` may be exported: `Mit Editor` imports `saveTimer` and reads its current value, and could not assign to it.
- *A module does nothing when it is loaded.* Its top level holds functions, constants and element lookups. What acts at load stands in `src/app.js`: the library setup and the async init are written there, and every listener and the restoring of the numbering preference is a `register…()` function of its module, which `src/app.js` calls in the order the statements had in the one file: `registerAssetUrlCleanup()`, `registerVersionDialog()`, `registerRailClicks()`, `registerEditorInput()`, `registerImageInput()`, `registerReset()`, `registerDownloadMenu()`, `registerNumbering()`, `registerHamburger()`, `registerViewToggle()`; and behind them the two that came later, `registerStorageBanner()` and `registerLicences()`. Without that the order would follow from who imports whom, and change whenever an import is added; two listeners on `document` for the same event (the download menu and the hamburger on `click`; until story 5.9, which gave Escape one listener and one list, the menu and the view mode on `Escape`) would swap silently. One thing did move: the element lookups of the modules now run before the library setup, because a module's top level runs before the entry's. They only read the page.
- *Modules may import each other in a circle,* as long as it is for functions that are called later. One circle is left: `render()` calls the asset pipeline, and the image input calls `render()`. That works because of the rule before: no module reads another's export while it loads. Until story 2.15 there were two larger ones. `escapeHtml()` stood in `render.js`, and six modules imported it from there; it is in `html.js` now. And the download helpers stood in the menu, which imports the downloads; they are in `downloads/download.js` now.
- *A module of pure logic loads without a page.* It imports no module that looks up an element when it is loaded (`dom.js`, `rail.js`, `persistence.js`, `editor.js`, the downloads), and nothing at its top level touches the page. What a test is to reach works on what it is handed: a pass gets its root and makes elements with `root.ownerDocument`. A function in such a module that only ever runs from a listener may use the page's globals, and says so in its comment: `tocLinkHandler()` in `toc.js` uses `document` and `history`. One exception more: the renderers of the diagrams, `renderMermaid()` in `diagrams.js` and `renderBpmn()` in `bpmn.js`, run inside the document pass and use the page's library (`mermaid`, `BpmnJS`) and, for BPMN, the page's `DOMParser`; they need a page by their nature, say so in their comments, and take every document they work on from the holder they draw into. So does what the picture button of a diagram does at a click (`pictureOf()` in `diagram-downloads.js`): the computed styles, the serialiser and a Blob of the page the SVG stands in. And the page part of `live-viewer.js`, which starts the page's `BpmnJS` from a listener. Today these modules are `html.js`, `warning.js`, `passes.js`, `transient.js`, `frontmatter.js`, `toc.js`, `callouts.js`, `chips.js`, `nodes.js`, `markers.js`, `cards.js`, `steps.js`, `facets.js`, `tables.js`, `filter.js`, `licences.js`, `diagrams.js`, `diagram-downloads.js`, `bpmn.js` and `live-viewer.js`. A test imports such a module in Node as it is (`tests/frontmatter.test.mjs`, `tests/passes.test.mjs`, `tests/callouts.test.mjs`, `tests/chips.test.mjs`, `tests/markers.test.mjs`, `tests/facets.test.mjs`, `tests/tables.test.mjs`, `tests/filter.test.mjs`, `tests/licences.test.mjs`, `tests/diagrams.test.mjs`, `tests/diagram-downloads.test.mjs`, `tests/bpmn.test.mjs`, `tests/live-viewer.test.mjs`; the diagram and BPMN tests with renderers or a library of their own); where it needs a DOM it takes `linkedom`. So logic that a test should reach is written into such a module. What needs layout, Mermaid or storage stays in the browser runs.

**Where a new component goes.** Into a module of its own under `src/app/`. It exports its pass, a function of `(root, context)` that works on the rendered document as `attachFootnotePreviews()` does, and `render.js` gets one line: the pass in `DOCUMENT_PASSES`, with a German name, at its place in the order (see *Render passes*). Every export copies the preview, so the component reaches all four downloads from there. Its styles go into `src/doc.css` (see *Document styles*). What it needs only in a running page, a listener or a control, is a second function in `RUNTIME_PASSES`; an element that function adds carries `data-dokufix-transient`. What it has to change when a document leaves the page is a step in `EXPORT_STEPS`. Where its logic can be written without a page, it is, and gets a test in Node. `app/callouts.js` is the first component built this way, `app/chips.js` the second, `app/tables.js` the third. A component that an author switches on for one block, as `app/cards.js`, `app/steps.js` and `app/facets.js` are, has no pass of its own: it exports an entry for the list of block markers, and the pass `Markierungen` applies it (see *Block markers, cards and step lists*, a story that brings a marker). If the component brings a library or other work of somebody else into the file, it adds an entry to the list in `app/licences.js` (see *Licence information*). A listener that is attached once for the whole page is still a `register…()` that `src/app.js` calls, after the ones that are there. If it shares a value with another module that both assign to, the value becomes a property of `state`.

**What the split did to documents** (2026-10-02, `tests/vergleich.mjs`, the built file before the split against the one after, reference document and demo text, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153): all 136 screenshots are pixel-identical, all assertions are green, and the three read-only exports are byte-identical in both browsers for both documents. One thing about the run itself showed here: in Chromium the exports of the reference document come out in one of two forms from run to run, for the file before the split as for the one after. The run types the document and clicks `Mit Editor` about 250 ms later, which is the delay of the editor's debounced save; whether that save runs first decides how often the clock is read, and Mermaid takes its diagram ids from the clock (`mermaid-…016` or `mermaid-…018`). Nothing else differs, and runs that fell the same way are byte-identical, old file against new. The file itself and every `Mit Editor` file grew by 972 B (67 959 → 68 931 B): `state.currentVersion` cannot be shortened by the minifier the way a variable can, and ten functions were added. `tests/speichern.mjs` is green in both browsers.

**What story 2.15 did to documents** (2026-10-02, `tests/vergleich.mjs`, the built file before the story against the one after, reference document and demo text, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153): all 136 screenshots are pixel-identical, and all assertions are green. The three read-only exports are the text they were, in both browsers and for both documents, apart from two things: the ids Mermaid generates, because diagrams are drawn one at a time now, and 408 B in the stylesheet, which are the rules of the warning. With the ids masked, the payload of `kompakt` and the diagrams of `schlank` unpacked, each export equals its counterpart outside its `<style>`, and the new stylesheet is the old one with those 408 B put in. The file itself grew by 2 800 B (68 931 → 71 731 B). A `Mit Editor` file grew by 2 537 B, which is 263 B less: it no longer carries Mermaid's tooltip element. `tests/speichern.mjs` and `tests/durchlaeufe.mjs` are green in both browsers.

## Checks

Nine tools, all run from the repository root: eight in `tests/`, and ESLint with `eslint.config.mjs`; beside them a measuring script, `tests/messung-suche.mjs`, which checks nothing. They look at the sources and at the built file from outside; the product contains no hook for any of them. `npm run pruefung` starts the three browser runs of a verification together (below).

**Side by side.** Each of the three browser runs, `tests/vergleich.mjs`, `tests/speichern.mjs` and `tests/durchlaeufe.mjs`, drives Chromium and Firefox side by side; the comparison run reports per browser afterwards, in the order of their names, and the other two sort their results by browser before printing. The runs of one verification are independent of each other and are started side by side: each comparison run with its own `--out`, and the save round trip and the failure cases write to folders of their own. A run that fails is repeated alone before it counts as a fault.

**One command.** `npm run pruefung` starts the standard runs of a verification side by side: the comparison run on the reference document and on the demo text, the save round trip and the failure cases, each on `dist/dokufix.html`. At the end it prints a table: per run its exit, its time and what its log says (per browser the assertions, with `--compare` the screenshots; for the other two the count of checks).

```
npm run pruefung                                              # tests/out/pruefung/
npm run pruefung -- --out tests/out/x \
  --run nachher="--compare tests/out/vorher --strict" \
  --control poc="--file poc/dokufix-poc.html --demo"
```

`--out` is the folder of the verification; each run writes to a folder of its own in it, named after the run, and its output to `<run>.log` beside it. `--run name=<arguments>` adds a comparison run with these arguments of `tests/vergleich.mjs`, which counts like a standard run; `--control name=<arguments>` adds one that is listed with its exit and its screenshot comparison, for a human to judge, and not counted. The arguments are split at blanks and must not name `--out`. Exit 1 when a standard run or a `--run` fails; the command to repeat it alone is printed. Nothing is repeated by the command itself.

**Libraries, served locally.** The built file keeps its CDN URLs. The three browser runs answer every request to `cdn.jsdelivr.net` themselves, in every browser context, from `tests/.cdn/`, one file per URL: `https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/mermaid@12.0.0/dist/mermaid.min.js` lies in `tests/.cdn/npm/mermaid@12.0.0/dist/mermaid.min.js` (`tests/cdn.mjs`). A file of an exact version on jsDelivr does not change, so the URL is the key. The folder is not in the repository (`tests/.gitignore`); a file missing from it is fetched once from its URL before anything is built, and without the network the run stops there with the URL named. A URL in the file under test or in `src/index.html` without an exact version stops the run as well. A request the page makes for any other URL under `cdn.jsdelivr.net` fails, is never fetched live, and fails the run, which lists it. Each run prints at its end the files it served; the comparison run writes them into `sizes.json` with the version each URL names. With the folder filled, the runs need no network: a full verification in a network namespace without one (`unshare -rn node tests/pruefung.mjs`) is green.

**One browser per run, a context per opening.** Each run starts one browser process per browser and opens every file in a browser context of its own, closed after it: fresh storage, `colorScheme`, viewport and scripts on or off as context options, and in the comparison run the clock and `Math.random` frozen anew. Until 2026-10-03 the comparison run started a browser process for each of its about thirty openings per browser.

**Waits on conditions.** The comparison run waits for what it needs, not for a fixed time: after opening, for fonts and images and two animation frames; after a change of the window's size or a style, two animation frames, in which layout and the page's handlers for the next frame are done; after focusing a footnote marker, until its preview's computed opacity is 1 (it fades in over .12 s); after the smooth scroll of a click in the table of contents, until the browser fires `scrollend`. The position alone does not say that a smooth scroll is over: Firefox holds it for two and three frames in the middle of one and a pixel before its end. Two waits stay time windows, because what they prove is that something does *not* happen: the 500 ms in `tests/durchlaeufe.mjs` (`QUIET_WINDOW`) after two overlapping renders and after a download that failed.

**Times.** Measured on 2026-10-03 on the built file of `c0e878a`, the four standard runs side by side, Chromium 153 and Firefox 153:

| Run | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| comparison run, reference document | 119 s | 65 s |
| comparison run, demo text | 116 s | 58 s |
| save round trip | 33 s | 18 s |
| failure cases | 64 s | 36 s |
| all four side by side | 119 s | 65 s |

Before is the harness as it was, after is the harness with the local libraries, one browser per run and the waits. Both give the same assertions, all green, and the comparison runs the same screenshots, pixel for pixel (`--compare --strict` of the new run against the old one, reference document and demo text, both browsers), and on the PoC the same failed checks. With the network taken away (`unshare -rn`) the four took 67 s, all green.

| Command | What it answers | Needs |
|---|---|---|
| `npm run check` | Is `dist/` what the sources give, does every document style sit in `doc.css`, and does every module declare or import each name it uses? | Node, `npm install` |
| `npm test` | Do the build, the style check and the lint fail where they have to, do the frontmatter parser, the pass runner, the callouts, the status chips, the block markers with cards and step lists, the tables with their wrapper, sub-line and facet filter, the free-text filter, the figure of a diagram, the BPMN renderer and the layout of BPMN without coordinates do what they say, does every fixture of that layout give its expected XML and break no rule but the known ones, and is the list of licence notices complete and at the versions the file uses? | Node, `npm install` |
| `npm run fixtures` | Does every fixture of BPMN without coordinates still lay out to its expected XML? With `-- --write` the expected XML and the known breaks are written anew (*Diagrams*, *The net in Node*) | Node, `npm install` |
| `npm run capture -- <author.bpmn>…` | What are an input's raw positions and measured label sizes, taken from the built file? | `npm install`, Chromium, the libraries in `tests/.cdn/` |
| `node tests/vergleich.mjs …` | Does a document still look the same in all four variants? | `npm install`, Chromium, Firefox, the libraries in `tests/.cdn/` |
| `node tests/speichern.mjs` | Does a saved file hold its document, and a file saved from it, and is it the built file otherwise? | `npm install`, Chromium, Firefox, the libraries in `tests/.cdn/` |
| `node tests/durchlaeufe.mjs` | Does a failure end as a warning in the document and in every export, do two renders run one after the other, does a transient element stay out of a saved file, does a broken marker leave the working ones alone, does a facet chosen or a term typed in the page stay out of every file, does a BPMN block that cannot be drawn, one without coordinates that cannot be laid out, or a page without `bpmn-js` or without Mermaid, leave the rest of the document alone? | `npm install`, Chromium, Firefox, the libraries in `tests/.cdn/` |
| `npm run pruefung …` | The three runs above for one verification, side by side, with a table of exit and time | as the three runs |
| `node tests/messung-suche.mjs [--file <file>]` | How long a search takes in a document of 400 sections (probe 1 of the audit of the search, story 5.16), with and without the pause, for a build before a change and one after it; it measures and checks nothing | `npm install`, Chromium, the libraries in `tests/.cdn/` |

The libraries in `tests/.cdn/` are fetched from the CDN on the first run (see *Libraries, served locally* above).

### Style check (`tests/check-doc-styles.mjs`)

`node tests/check-doc-styles.mjs` — fails when a style for document content sits anywhere but in `src/doc.css`. Described under *Document styles (one source)*. It reads the sources and the built file as text and needs neither a browser nor `npm install`. `npm run check` runs `node build.mjs --check` first, so a stale `dist/` is reported before the styles are looked at.

### Lint (`eslint src`)

`npx eslint src`, the third step of `npm run check`: ESLint 10.11.0 with four rules over every `.js` under `src/`. It is there for what esbuild does not see. Bundling, esbuild takes a name that a module neither declares nor imports for a global and says nothing, and the page fails when that line runs, which for a download or an error path may be never in a test.

| A module … | The build | The lint |
|---|---|---|
| uses a name it neither declares nor imports | passes | fails: `no-undef` |
| imports a name the other module does not export, and uses it | fails | passes |
| imports a file that does not exist | fails | passes |
| assigns to a name it imported | fails | fails: `no-import-assign` |
| imports a name and does not use it, exported or not | passes, the import is dropped unread | fails: `dokufix/no-unused-imports` |
| imports another as a namespace (`import * as x`) and reads `x.name`, which that module does not export | passes with a warning; `x.name` is `undefined` when the line runs | fails on the `import * as` itself: `no-restricted-syntax` |

- **Globals.** A name counts as declared when it is one of the browser's (the list of the `globals` package, 17.13.0) or one of `marked`, `markedFootnote`, `mermaid`, `BpmnJS`, which the page loads from the CDN.
- **`dokufix/no-unused-imports`** is written in `eslint.config.mjs`, a dozen lines on ESLint's own scope analysis. ESLint's `no-unused-vars` has no option that limits it to imports; it would also report a function nobody calls and a local nobody reads, and those are not this check's business.
- **`import * as` is refused as a form,** used or not, by `no-restricted-syntax` on `ImportNamespaceSpecifier`. Neither tool can tell which member of a namespace exists, so the modules import by name, where both can.
- **One thing it cannot see.** A missing import of a name that is also a browser global (`name`, `status`, `open`, `history`) passes `no-undef`; none of the script's top-level names is one (checked 2026-10-02), and a new one should not be.

### Tests (`npm test`)

`node --test tests/*.test.mjs`, 798 cases. The cases about the build, the style check and the lint work on a copy of `src/` in a temporary folder; the sources and `dist/` are never written to. The cases about the product's logic import its modules as they are (see *The script's modules*, the rule for modules of pure logic).

- `tests/check-doc-styles.test.mjs` proves the style check: it breaks a copy once per case (a `#preview h5` or `.dokufix-x` rule in `app.css` or the export frame, `.reader-body h5` in the export frame, each named with the module the frame stands in, `.footnotes li` in `app.css`, `body.mode-view .dokufix-doc h5` in `doc.css`, a rule of the link "license information" that is not listed: `.dokufix-licences-x` and `.dokufix-licences[open] summary` in `app.css`, `.dokufix-licences h5` in the export frame, and `.dokufix-licences .dokufix-callout`, which names a document construct, an export without `readonlyCss()`, the block's element missing or doubled in the page, a document rule in a `<style>` of the page or in the block's element, a built file without the block, with an empty one, with two, and in `doc.css` a root rule other than the one of the large view, `html:has(.dokufix-callout)`, and that one with a descendant) and expects exit 1 with file, line and culprit named; that one root rule, written once more, passes.
- `tests/build.test.mjs` proves the build: unchanged sources give the committed file byte for byte, and with `--showcase-out` the committed showcase as well, while `--out` alone writes one file and needs no `src/showcase.md`; the showcase equals the built file outside its data blocks `#dokufix-demo` and `#dokufix-assets`, its demo block holds `src/showcase.md`, which is short, without special cases, and writes its BPMN diagram without coordinates; `--check` passes on both files, names the showcase alone as stale when `src/showcase.md` changed and as missing when it is not there, and checks both without `--out`; a showcase text that names an image the folder lacks, or a missing `src/showcase.md`, ends the build with it named and neither file written; `--dev` refuses `--showcase-out` and refuses to write the committed showcase; `--check` fails with "is stale" when any of the sources or a module under `src/app/` changed; a module that imports a name another does not export, or a file that is not there, or assigns to an import ends the build with module and name and nothing written; a slot missing, doubled or unknown ends the build with the slot named and nothing written; so do a missing source and `</style` in a minified stylesheet, `search.css` among them; a script with `<!--` in a string, a template and a regular expression is built without it and gives the same values; the build runs when started through a symlink; a demo text containing `</script>`, `<!--` and `$&` leaves its block and everything behind it intact; the built file's asset block holds every image of `src/assets/` under the hash of its bytes, each named by the demo text; an image whose bytes do not give its name, a demo text that names an image the folder lacks or a folder without it, and a file of another name or type each end the build with the culprit named and nothing written; the reader bundle stands in its block, the filter's block alone gone, one minified IIFE without `</script` or `<!--`, without the editor's elements and without a renderer of a diagram, the same with `--dev`, and run in a document as an export has it gives a marked table its working field, adds the panel's styles, the text the page carries, behind the file's stylesheet, opens the search with `/`, lists a term's places with their count and closes with "×"; over a packed diagram (`data-gz`) a typed term waits, nothing listed, until the decoder's event, which comes after the decoder has also set back a packed source link's `href` and logged the one it could not (story 2.10), whose diagram stays packed, and then runs, and without one it does not wait;
