`</script` or `<!--` in it is refused on the step that assembles the page; `--check` fails when `src/reader.js` or `src/search.css` changed. One case cannot be reached through the sources: esbuild escapes every `</script` it meets, so the refusal of a script containing it is shown on the step that assembles the page, and so is the refusal of a script that still contains `<!--`. For the readable build: `--dev` writes a file that is the committed page outside its script and its styles, not minified, with a source map that names the modules; it never writes the committed file; `--dev --watch` builds again when a source changes and goes on after a build that failed.
- `tests/frontmatter.test.mjs` runs the frontmatter parser in Node: what is read (pairs, nested maps, sequences, quoting, comments, JSON), what is not frontmatter and stays untouched (a thematic break, one prose line, a block that is never closed), what is shown raw with its reason, the five inputs of *Frontmatter* among them, and `deriveDocTitle()`.
- `tests/passes.test.mjs` runs the pass runner in Node over a fragment that `linkedom` 0.18.13 parses: the order of the list, a pass that returns a promise, a pass that throws or whose promise is rejected (the others run, one warning each at the top in the order of the list, the error on the console, the runner does not reject), the markup of the warning, `removeTransient()`, and real passes driven by the runner: metadata panel, heading ids, inline table of contents; and with the callout pass in front, which headings count: one inside a callout gets no id and no entry, at any depth, one in an ordinary blockquote does. And with the chip pass between the two: a heading with a chip has the entry and the anchor of its text with the chip's label, for each of the five colours; a heading that is only a chip has both, never empty; the anchors are the ones the same headings have without the chip pass, also where the status touches the text before it (`Bestellung:`, `Stand` or a slash directly before the code span), and those of other headings do not move; with the passes in the wrong order the entry carries the dot. And with the marker pass behind the chip pass, as in `render.js`: a document with broken markers among working ones comes through the runner with no pass failed and nothing on the console, each warning at its marker's place and not at the top, the cards, a chip in one of them, the step list and a step list inside a callout built, and heading ids and table of contents as they are without markers; a document without a marker leaves the pass as it went in. And with the pass `Tabellen` behind the marker pass: every table wrapped once, the one in a callout as well, a facet filter over a column of chips with their labels as values, a refused `facets` and `cards` before a table each a warning at its place; with the tables wrapped before the markers are applied, `facets` no longer finds its table.
- `tests/callouts.test.mjs` runs the callout pass in Node over markup as `marked` 18.0.14 emits it; `marked` comes from the CDN and is not installed, so each case carries the measured markup with its Markdown beside it. The table of *Callouts* line by line: the five types with class, label and role, the marker in any case, a list, a paragraph, a code block or a hard break behind the marker, the marker alone, a callout in a quote, in a list item and in another callout; eleven inputs that stay as they are, byte for byte; the masked marker as the known limit. And the symbols: each type has a `data:` URI in `src/doc.css` that is an escaped SVG of 16 px with one path in the colour of its edge and label, and the built file carries each URI unchanged.
- `tests/chips.test.mjs` runs the chip pass in Node over markup as `marked` 18.0.14 emits it, measured like that of the callouts. The table of *Status chips* line by line: the five colours with class, word and label, the dot gone; the variation selector; blanks around the label; a label with `<` and `&`; a chip in running text, a table cell, a heading, a link, bold, emphasised and struck text, a list item, a quotation and a callout; fifteen inputs that stay as they are, among them the dot at the end, no label, no blank, a blank before the dot, a tab, a no-break space, another dot, code blocks, a `<code>` that holds markup; the same label in two colours; the false positive as the known limit. That `render.js` lists the pass directly after `Hinweise` and before `Überschriften`, and that no other module under `src/` uses a colour dot outside a comment. And the look: each colour has a rule with a text colour on a tint at a contrast of at least 4.5 : 1, a mark whose shape no other colour has, drawn with no image and no character; tint and mark are kept in print; the word is hidden from the eye and not from a screen reader; the built file carries the rules.
- `tests/markers.test.mjs` runs the block markers in Node over markup as `marked` 18.0.14 emits it, measured like that of the callouts; `linkedom` keeps a comment as a node, as a browser does. Reading a marker: name, argument, an argument in double quotes, any case, a comment over several lines, no name that can be read, a comment that is no marker, a long marker cut for its warning. The list: its four entries, each complete, and `judgeMarker()` with each of its answers. A component that refuses its block, with a list of its own: the warning in the marker's words with the component's reason, the block as it was, nothing logged; a refused marker does not count as applied; a component that moves its block into a new parent, and the marker behind it still acts on the block. Cards: the class on the list, the title in a list with and without blank lines between its items, bold text elsewhere, a colon directly behind the title and one that stays, a hard break behind the title, what else an item may start with, a nested list. Step lists: the numbers from 1, from 3 and from 0, with a `value` attribute, no inline style, the actor with and without blank lines, emphasis without a colon, the colon taken from the end of whatever the emphasis holds, a list inside an item. Where a marker may stand, and two markers before one block, in both orders, on one line and with a list of two markers that both act; the same marker twice and three times before one block, where the component is applied once and every further one is a warning. Fourteen markers that cannot take effect, each with the warning at its place in its words and the block as it was; a marker inside a paragraph, a heading, a list item and between the items of a list; a broken marker among working ones; names that are class names of the product or properties of every object; a component that throws; markup in a marker. Nine inputs that stay as they are, byte for byte, the marker as code and other comments among them. That `render.js` lists the pass after `Status-Chips` and directly before `Tabellen`, that the tile floats and no rule of the two components positions anything, that one rule makes number and actor plain text in the preview of a footnote, that the styles of both components go by the classes the pass sets and by no `<strong>` or `<em>`, that they use no colour the document styles did not have, and that the built file carries the rules and both entries.
- `tests/facets.test.mjs` runs the facet filter in Node over markup as `marked` 18.0.14 emits it, measured like that of the callouts, and over tables written as HTML, with and without `<tbody>`. The entry and its place in the list; the controls of a column with their counts, order, keys, names and the one `checked`, the table otherwise as it was, the group of a second table; nothing generated: no `<style>`, no inline style, no id, and no `innerHTML` in the module. The value of a cell: `C`, `C++`, `C#`, two scripts and an empty cell apart, case kept, white space collapsed, the first line, a column of chips by their labels, a footnote marker left out, markup in a heading or a cell as text with no element made of it. The column: any case, white space collapsed, quotes, the first match, `colspan` in the header row and in the data rows, a header row of `<th>` without `<thead>`, `<tfoot>`, a second header row and a nested table left alone. Seven refusals, each a warning at its place with the table as it was, byte for byte, and nothing logged; exactly `FACET_MAX` values filtered, one more refused with both numbers; the wrong block; `cards` beside `facets` in either order; `facets` twice; a table in a table that filters. The pure core: `groupFacetValues()` and `planFacets()`. And the document styles: exactly one hiding selector per key from 1 to `FACET_MAX`, in one rule that only hides, inside `@media not print` and `@supports selector(:has(a))`, the bar hidden outside them; no rule of the wrapper or the group that makes a containing block; the radio button hidden from the eye and not from the keyboard, placed against its label; the chosen control marked by more than a colour; no colour the document styles did not have; the built file carries every hiding selector and the entry.

The entry and its place in the list; the mark on the table and no field in the document; the field above the wrapper, outside it, with placeholder, name, counter as a status, transient, no style, no id; the default placeholder for no argument, `""` and blanks; markup in the placeholder as text. Typing: the rows with the term and "3 von 14 Zeilen", an empty field, a term nothing contains; case and blanks; the term as one phrase, across two cells; a line break as a blank; the chip's word and a footnote's preview no text of the row; transient elements, the controls of a facet filter and a table in a cell; an SVG, a stylesheet, a script and a template in a cell no text of the row (story 5.16); header row and `<tfoot>` never hidden; the counter's words. Beside a facet filter in either order of the markers: the field between controls and wrapper, a row shown when both hold, the counter counting both. The wrong block, `filter` twice; a second run of the pass and of all passes; no wrapper; Escape with and without text; the export steps, the class of a hidden row out of all three read-only exports and the table's mark out of `nur-lesen` alone, with the table as it is without the marker afterwards and a field built anew from the mark that `schlank` and `kompakt` keep, and their places: `showFilteredRows` in `EXPORT_STEPS`, `removeFilterMarks` handed in by `nur-lesen` alone, the reader bundle in `schlank` and `kompakt`, without a gate on a filter table, and no script in `nur-lesen`; the pass in `RUNTIME_PASSES` and in no other list. And the document styles: field and hidden rows only inside `@media not print`, nothing of the filter positioned or made a containing block, the field at most 320 px, the focus outline in the link colour; the built file carries the rules, the entry and the pass. `tests/passes.test.mjs` drives the filter through the runner as well, beside a facet filter over chips with a footnote preview in a cell.
- `tests/tables.test.mjs` runs the pass `Tabellen` in Node: one wrapper around a table and the table as it was; several tables; a table in a list item, a quotation, a callout, a footnote and a cell; a table written as HTML, with attributes and a caption, in a `<div>` of the author's; a second pass that changes nothing; a document without a table as it went in. The sub-line: after a hard break, with blanks around it, in a header cell, two in one cell, around bold text, behind a chip; eight cells and four places outside a table where emphasis stays as it is. That `render.js` lists the pass directly after `Markierungen` and before `Überschriften` and the footnote previews; the rules of wrapper and sub-line, by class and never by element; the built file carries them.
- `tests/search.test.mjs` runs the search in Node: the hit ranges, in order, not overlapping, case ignored, an empty term and blanks; ranges behind a character whose lowered form is longer (`İ`), the capital sharp s, an emoji; case-sensitive, and both switches together; the minimum length: too short, umlauts, blanks, the four exempt pairs in any case, another character, and the rule on what light fuzzy leaves; the part a result shows, whole or cut at blanks around the first hit, with the hits in it; a code block one place of the kind "Code", white space collapsed, a range over a line break of the code, inline code text of its paragraph, a code block in a list item no text of the item, a diagram's fenced source, a warning's detail, the footer of an export and a transient block no code. Of story 5.16: raw HTML, text directly in a `div`, `dt`, `dd`, `summary`, `figcaption` and bare `blockquote` a place each with its ranges, an image's `alt` no text, such a block read without the places, lists and tables in it and with a block in it, none in a place and none of the page's own (a filter's field with its counter, a warning, the inline table of contents, a callout, a facet group, a table's wrapper, a transient element, the footnotes); an SVG, a stylesheet, a script and a template in a cell no text of the row, as `filterRowText()` reads it; `groupResults()` walking into no diagram, code block or metadata panel; `findHits()` lowering a text whole, a handful of calls in place of one per character, and one character at a time where that would differ, the capital sigma, a longer lowered form, a character outside the BMP. On a page `linkedom` parses, the steps of the large view and of a table filter's field with one after them that stands for read mode: the open view first, with the focus in a filled field, then the field, which fires `input`, then the step after it; the field's step only for the pass's own field, with text and the focus in it. And the renderer, with a stand-in for `bpmn-js` as the global `BpmnJS`: the host in `<body>`, transient, fixed off-screen, gone afterwards; import, export and destroy in that order; the refusals without the library and for XML the library cannot import, each with the host removed and a line on the console; import warnings on the console; and a `bpmn` block in its figure with the line of its downloads and the credit, its source the XML as drawn, beside a refused one that is the warning naming its title. XML that does not parse is the browser runs', since `linkedom` reports no parser error.

the axis map, the docking and each of the seven corrections on positions made up for it; a flow that keeps 12 px to its own symbol before it turns, and the flow back within a row (story 2.20): four points around the row, nested flows back on distinct levels and disjoint ones on the innermost, a twin's ring kept off, an event's port, a middle lane that grows;
