and the seams of the routing, `portEnds()`, `portCandidates()`, `conflictScore()` and `growLane()`, one case each.
- `tests/bpmn-fixtures.test.mjs` lays out each of the 36 fixtures of `tests/fixtures/bpmn-layout/` in Node, with the measured label sizes and with the estimate, and compares the XML byte for byte with the expected; that the folder holds the five files of each input, the index and the known list and nothing else; that the raw positions are of the Mermaid the layout is made with; that a difference names the element and the line; and that an input without labels lays out the same in both modes. And the two commands from outside, by their absolute paths: `npm run fixtures` on a copy of the folder (`DOKUFIX_FIXTURE_DIR`) names an expected XML changed there, and `--write` writes it back and leaves the known list as it is; `npm run capture` stops with exit 1 without Chromium, and skips an input with a diagram part, naming it, without needing Chromium for that.
- `tests/bpmn-rules.test.mjs` checks the rules of `tests/bpmn-rules.mjs` on every measured fixture against `known-breaks.json`, each rule on a diagram made up for it, a fixture with a piece made slanted (a new break, named), and a listed break that is gone.
- `tests/lint.test.mjs` proves the lint: a module that lost an import, one that uses a name of the shared state without `state.`, one that uses a name nobody declares, one that assigns to an import, one that imports what it does not use and one that imports a namespace each give exit 1 with module, line and name; a function nobody calls and a local nobody reads pass.

### Comparison run (`tests/vergleich.mjs`)

Builds all four download variants from one document in one run, reopens each the way a recipient would, and records what they look like and how big they are. It is the tool for any change that must not alter the look of a document.

```
node tests/vergleich.mjs --out tests/out/vorher                              # before the change
node tests/vergleich.mjs --out tests/out/nachher --compare tests/out/vorher  # after it
```

- **File under test.** `dist/dokufix.html`; `--file <file>` (alias `--poc`) takes any other dokufix editor file. The built file against the PoC:

  ```
  node tests/vergleich.mjs --file poc/dokufix-poc.html --demo --out tests/out/00-poc-demo
  node tests/vergleich.mjs --demo --out tests/out/01-dist-demo --compare tests/out/00-poc-demo
  ```

  The first exits 1, on the checks of the licence information, which the PoC does not have; its screenshots and sizes are written all the same. The second exits 0 and lists every screenshot as differing: since the footnotes heading reads "Fußnoten", the built file no longer looks like the PoC. So this pair is run without `--strict`, which would make the second exit 1 too, and with the demo text: with the reference document the PoC run fails on the checks of callouts, status chips and block markers as well, because the PoC has none of them. `--strict` is for the file before a change against the file after it.
- **Document.** `tests/referenz.md`, an invented text that contains every construct dokufix styles: frontmatter, a footnote cited three times, `[[toc]]`, headings down to h4, table, code, blockquote, the five callouts, one of them with a list and one with a heading inside, status chips in the five colours, in running text, a table, a list, a link, bold text, a callout, a footnote and two headings, beside code spans that stay code, cards with and without a title, in a list with and without blank lines, step lists from 1 and from 3 with and without an actor, a footnote cited in a step and in a plain numbered list directly below it, two markers before one block, nine markers that end as warnings, the marker as code and a comment that is no marker, a table wider than the text column, tables in a callout and a list item, sub-lines and emphasis that is none, a footnote cited in a cell and in a paragraph directly below it, six facet filters (a column of text with a first line under a sub-line, a column of chips, values that look alike or stand in other scripts or are empty, markup in a heading and a cell, a table written as HTML with a merged cell, `cards` beside `facets`), five facet markers that end as warnings (no such column, no column named, a list, a paragraph, thirty-three values), three free-text filters (one beside a facet filter over fourteen rows with a chip, a line break and a sub-line, one alone with a footnote cited in a cell and in a paragraph directly below the table, one without a placeholder), three filter markers that end as warnings (a list, a paragraph, the second of two before one table), lists, an image as `data:` URI, a missing `#asset-` reference, two Mermaid diagrams, and since story 2.7 a section of BPMN: a diagram with two pools, three lanes, events, gateways, tasks of eight types, a call activity, sequence and message flows, a second one without a pool, and two blocks that end as warnings (XML that does not parse, XML without coordinates). `--demo` builds from the built-in demo text instead, `--doc <file>` from any other Markdown file.
- **Screenshots.** Each variant in light and dark at 1400 and 1600 px, once at rest and once with every state switched on (`-zustand`: heading numbering, open metadata panel, a revealed footnote preview, landing highlight with its marked arrow), plus the preview pane inside the editor; and per variant one picture of the large view of the first diagram open, at 1400 px in the light scheme, in `<browser>/grossansicht/`. Those are compared only where the baseline has that folder too; against a baseline from before story 2.9 they are listed as not compared and do not count, so that the pictures of the closed page can be compared with `--strict`. `--compare` reports the differing pixels per image and writes a red-on-white mask of them to `<browser>/diff/`. Two runs of the same file are pixel-identical in Chromium and in Firefox, so every reported pixel is a real difference.
- **Sizes.** `sizes.json` per browser, with the libraries the page loaded and the version each one's URL names. `--compare` prints the delta per variant. Two runs of the same file give the same byte counts.
- **Images.** An `#asset-` reference counts as an image to be shown when the file under test carries that hash in its `#dokufix-assets` block (the photo of the demo text, as built), and as a missing image otherwise, as the reference document's one is.
- **Assertions.** In every variant: metadata panel, footnote preview on focus, landing highlight and marked return arrow, heading numbering, rail at 1600 px and not at 1400 px; callouts, status chips, block markers with cards and step lists, and the licence information (below); in `Mit Editor`, `schlank` and `kompakt` the search (below); `nur-lesen` contains no `<script>` and nothing of the search; no read-only export carries a rule of the editor interface. A failed assertion exits 1. Differing pixels do not, unless `--strict` is given, because some differences are decided ones. An image that only the run or only the baseline has counts as differing. A `--compare` folder without a run for the browser, or one that is the `--out` folder itself, stops the run with exit 1 before anything is built or deleted.
- **Callouts.** The run reads from the Markdown how many callouts of which type the document has and which headings stand inside one, as far as the reference document needs it: a quote that starts at the beginning of its line and opens with a marker, and the `#` headings in it. It does not read a callout in a list item or in a nested quote, nor a masked marker, which the product turns into a callout; a document given with `--doc` that has one of these fails the count although the build is right. The run expects in every variant: one callout per alert blockquote, in order, and no blockquote left that opens with a marker; each labelled with the word of its type, visible, as its first line; an edge on the left only, edge and label in the colour of the type; a symbol that the browser loads as a picture of 16 × 16 px, one per type; the heading inside a callout without an id and in neither table of contents nor rail; a `[!WARNING]` callout and the product's warning, which the run puts beside it for the measurement, apart in more than colour. The checks of the numbering run when a callout holds a heading of level 2 to 4, the levels that are numbered. For the numbering a browser does not say which number a counter shows. So the page is photographed three times with numbering on: as it is, with the heading inside the callout forced out of the count by a style, and with it forced in. As it is, the page has to equal the first and differ from the second. A document without callouts is checked for having none. A file that predates callouts, the PoC among them, fails these checks on the reference document; its screenshots and sizes are written all the same.
- **Status chips.** Which code span of the Markdown is a status, with which colour and label, the run asks `readChip()` of `src/app/chips.js`, and which anchor a label gives `slugify()` of `src/app/toc.js`: the run does not say a second time what a status is. It reads the Markdown as far as the reference document needs it: a code span on one line, outside fenced code, and a heading written with `#` whose only inline markup is code spans. It does not read an indented code block, a code span across two lines or a `<code>` written as HTML; a document given with `--doc` that has one of these fails the count although the build is right. The run expects in every variant: one chip per status code span, in the order of the document, with the colour of its dot and its label, and no inline code left that is a status; the chips of the footnotes among the footnotes, and as many in each footnote's preview as in its definition; every chip visible and styled, a pill in small capitals of the monospace stack with label and mark in the colour of its class on its tint, the mark drawn without an image; in every chip the name of its colour as text, before the label, rendered, at most one pixel large and clipped, and the accessible text of a chip, as Playwright reads it from the page, the word and the label; two chips with the same label and different colours with different words; the word placed against its chip (the chip positioned, the word's offset parent the chip); a chip in a link underlined, and no other, as many as the Markdown has links whose text is a status. In `Mit Editor`, in edit mode, the preview is scrolled to its end and the page itself must not be scrollable. The footnote previews are in the page for that measurement: they are placed against a positioned ancestor themselves, and in edit mode that is the box below the toolbar, which cuts off what reaches past it (see *Footnotes*, in the editor). And a click on an entry of the table of contents in the preview has to scroll the preview to its heading and nothing else: toolbar and pane header stay where they are, the page and the box below the toolbar are not scrolled. Both are measured with the page as it ships: the style by which the run switches the fallbacks off in Playwright's Firefox is out of action for them, because with it in the page the file before the rule passed in that browser. Control: on the built file before the rule both checks fail in both browsers, with both documents (page 8 679 against 1 000 px in Chromium, 8 702 in Firefox; toolbar 100 and 102 px out of the window). For the marks it puts a copy of the first chip of each colour into the page, all with the label "x" and all in black on white, each at a whole pixel of its own, and photographs them: two copies of the same chip have to be the same picture, and every two colours have to differ in at least eight pixels. The copies and the style are removed again; the pictures stay beside the exports as `marken-<variant>-<colour>.png`. For a heading with a chip: its entry in the table of contents and in the rail reads the heading's text with the chip's label, its anchor is the one of that text, and the heading itself still holds the word. No entry of the table of contents or the rail is empty. A document without chips is checked for having none. A file that predates chips fails these checks on the reference document, as one that predates callouts fails theirs.
- **Block markers, cards and step lists.** What a comment says as a marker, and what a marker comes to before a block of a given kind, its component or the text of its warning, the run asks `readMarker()` and `judgeMarker()` of `src/app/markers.js`: it does not say a second time what a marker does, and the words of the warnings are pinned in `tests/markers.test.mjs`. What it reads itself is the Markdown, as far as the reference document needs it: a comment on one line; a marker on a line of its own at the beginning of the line, alone or with other comments, has the block behind it, and so has one on a line of its own indented in a list item, whose block is read in the item's lines; any other has none; a list whose items start at the beginning of a line with `-` or `1.`, each item on one line, with blank lines between items or indented lines behind them; a table that starts with `|` or `<table`, which a facet marker reads further (see *Tables* below); the title of a card as `**…**` at the start of its item, the actor of a step as `*…:*` or `***…:***`. It does not read a comment across several lines, a marker in a quotation, an item that goes on in a line that is not indented, or the same marker twice before one block; a document given with `--doc` that has one of these fails the counts although the build is right. The run expects in every variant: no marker left as a comment, the other comments of the document still there, and a marker written as code still code; one list of cards per `cards` marker that can act, in the order of the document, each item with the title the Markdown gives it or none; one step list per `steps` marker that can act, each item with its number, counted from the first number of the list, and its actor without the colon; one warning per marker that cannot act, in the order of the document, with the text `judgeMarker()` gives, visible, styled as the product's warning and inside no paragraph or heading. Every card with a thin border all round in a grid without list markers; a title a `<strong>` shown as a line of its own with the card's text below it; the cards of a list beside each other at 1400 px and below each other at 400 px, as wide as their list, always inside it. Every step with its number as the first thing in its item, visible, on its tint, left of the step's text, neither it nor its item positioned, the item without an inline style; a list with its start value and its first tile showing it; an actor an `<em>` shown as a block in capitals, above the step's text and right of the tile. Where a step cites a footnote, the preview of that footnote is revealed at 900 px beside the one of a footnote in a numbered list without the component, both at one scroll position, which is why the reference document puts that list directly below the step list: each has to stand above its marker and over it, inside the window, and both as far above their marker and as far to its side. A document without such a list in the same window, as the demo text, has the step's preview judged on its own: above its marker and over it, inside the window; so has a document without a paragraph beside a footnote cited in a cell (see *Tables* below). Chromium carries this check. In the Playwright Firefox build it is not judged, and the run prints a note with the figures: there the preview of the plain list does not stand above its marker either once the page is scrolled (on the reference document both previews lie about 5 000 px above the window), so that browser says nothing about a step. A document without markers is checked for having none. A file that predates block markers fails these checks on the reference document, as one that predates callouts fails theirs.
- **Tables.** Which tables the document has, which are wider than the text column, which emphasis is a sub-line and which cells cite a footnote, the run reads from the Markdown, as far as the reference document and the demo text need it: a Markdown table whose rows start with `|`, at the beginning of the line, in a quotation or indented in a list item; one with seven columns or more is taken for a wide one, which is how both documents write theirs; a table written as HTML from `<table` at the beginning of a line to `</table>`, one `<tr>` per line, the header row in `<thead>`; a sub-line as `<br>*…*` in a cell of a Markdown table. What a facet marker comes to, its controls or the reason it refuses, it asks `planFacets()` of `src/app/facets.js` with the headings and values it read, and `refusedMarker()` for the warning: the run does not say a second time what a facet is. The value of a cell it reads itself: the first line, a status code span as its label, emphasis, links, a footnote marker and masking backslashes as their text. Not read: a sub-line written with `_`, one in a table written as HTML, a table in a footnote definition, a `|` in a code span. The run expects in every variant: every table in one wrapper of its own, not positioned and holding nothing placed in it, the table's bottom margin on the wrapper; the wide table scrolling inside its wrapper and no other, none scrolling up and down, the page not wider than the window at 1400 px, and at 600 px with the hidden footnote previews set aside, which make the page wider there with or without tables (663 px in a window of 600 on the built file before the story); every sub-line in order, upright, 12 px, grey, on a line of its own, and no other emphasis; one facet filter per marker that can act, with legend, controls, counts and the key of every row; as the file opens, "Alle" chosen by its attribute alone and all rows shown, although a value was chosen in the page while each file was written; each filter a group of its own, nothing an author wrote an element in it, no `<style>`, no inline style, no id; the controls styled as pills, the radio button not seen and placed against its label, the chosen one with a dark border and a filled ring; every line of controls starting at the same place beside the legend, at 1400 px and at 600 px, where two filters of the reference document take more than one line; choosing each control of each filter in turn shows the rows of its value; a real click and the arrow keys choose, and the focused control is marked; in print, with a value chosen, all rows and no controls; and without the block `@supports selector(:has(a))`, which the run takes out of the page for the measurement because no browser without `:has()` is run, no controls and every row. `nur-lesen` and `schlank` are opened once more with scripts switched off, and the filter has to be there and work by click and by keyboard. A footnote cited in a cell: at 900 px its preview stands above its marker as the one of a paragraph directly below the table does, reaches above the wrapper's upper edge, and is painted there: the page is photographed there with the preview revealed and without, and the two have to differ. Chromium carries this check; in the Playwright Firefox build it is not judged where the preview of the paragraph does not stand above its marker either, as with a step. A document without tables is checked for having none. A file that predates story 2.5 fails these checks on the reference document, as one that predates callouts fails theirs.
- **The free-text filter.** What a `filter` marker comes to the run asks `judgeMarker()` and, for a marker that stands before a table a second time, `repeatedMarker()`; the placeholder it asks `markFilter()`. The text of each data row it reads from the Markdown itself, as `filterRowText()` reads the cells: the cells joined by a blank, a line break as a blank, a status code span as its label, other code as its content, emphasis, links and masking backslashes as their text, a footnote marker as nothing. Which rows a term shows and what the counter says it asks `filterMatches()` and `filterCountText()`. The terms it types it chooses from those texts: per filter the first word of four letters or more that some rows contain and others do not; the word of each chip in the table and, for each footnote a cell cites, the first word of six letters or more of its definition that no row contains, which have to find what the visible text finds; and beside a facet filter a value and a term that together show fewer rows than each alone. In the editor, while the four files are written: a term typed before each download, the counter saying how many rows it shows, and after each read-only download the page's fields empty again with no row hidden; then the document rendered twice, one field per filter each time. In `Mit Editor`, `schlank` and `kompakt`: one field per marker that can act, in order, with its placeholder, the name "Tabelle filtern", empty, a search field, transient; directly above the wrapper of its table, outside it, at its left edge, and beside a facet filter between its controls and the wrapper; as the file opens all rows and the counter of all rows, a status; shown, at most 320 px wide, the counter 12 px and grey, nothing of it positioned. Typed by the keyboard as a reader types: each term as it is, in capitals with blanks around it and in small letters, the rows and the counter as expected, and an empty field shows all; the hidden words; the value and the term beside a facet filter; in print with a term typed, every row and no field; in `Mit Editor` Escape in the field with text empties it and read mode stays, Escape in the empty field leaves read mode; in `schlank` and `kompakt` Escape empties the field. The preview of the footnote cited in a cell of a filter table stands where the one of a paragraph directly below the table does, as for any table (see above). `schlank` and `kompakt` carry the reader bundle from the page's block once, with a filter table or without, `schlank` as a `<script>` in front of its decoder, `kompakt` as the data block its decoder runs; `schlank` holds no field and no hidden row outside its stylesheet and scripts, and opened with scripts switched off it has no field and shows every row. In `nur-lesen`: no field, no `data-dokufix-filter`, no hidden row, nothing of the filter outside the stylesheet. In a facet filter of a file with a field the group holds the field between bar and wrapper, in `nur-lesen` not. A file that predates story 2.6 fails these checks on the reference document, as one that predates callouts fails theirs.
- **Diagrams and BPMN.** The run reads every fenced `mermaid` and `bpmn` block from the Markdown with the heading before it, and expects in every variant: each diagram in its figure, in the order of the document, its title the heading's label, its accessible name that title as the accessibility tree computes it, the figure with the margin a diagram had. Whether a BPMN block can be drawn it asks the product (`hasCoordinates()`, and for a block without coordinates `readProcess()` of `src/app/bpmn-layout.js`, run in Node on the XML as `linkedom` parses it, which gives the layout's refusal or the process) and the browser's XML parser; the warning of one that cannot it asks `bpmnWarningText()`. The run assumes the page has Mermaid. For a BPMN diagram: an SVG of role `img` named by its title; every element its XML places (each `bpmnElement` of a `BPMNShape` or `BPMNEdge`; for a laid-out one its flow nodes, flows and lanes, and a pool only where a participant exists) drawn with the class of its kind; for a laid-out one its counts and a clean drawing, measured on the SVG by `data-element-id` (see *Diagrams*, BPMN without coordinates); no `foreignObject`, no hit area, `width="100%"` and no height; no colour written into the SVG, only the three custom properties, and the colours a browser computes those of the document styles; "Gezeichnet mit bpmn-js" below it, "bpmn-js" a visible link to https://bpmn.io; no id twice in the page; no host of the drawing left in it. A read-only file carries nothing of `bpmn-js`: no script tag or URL of it, none of its code (`BpmnJS`, the bundle's name), not the comment its export function writes; its name may stand as text, in the licence information, the credit and the author's prose; `schlank` carries the credit as readable text once per diagram and shows it with scripts off, beside the notice that the diagram needs JavaScript. The large view of a diagram (story 2.9), in every variant: every figure with its controls and no warning with any, the file opened with every view closed at "Einpassen" although each was written with the first view open at "150 %", and a read-only download closes the view in the page by its render; a click low on the tallest diagram while its top lies above a window 400 px high opens and closes it without moving the page, and in the editor file in edit mode without moving the preview pane; a click opens the view over the whole window and above everything, with the title, the steps and "Schließen", at "Einpassen", the whole diagram inside the stage at most at 150 % of its width as drawn, and the page does not scroll, by the wheel either; the steps give 1, 1.5 and 2 times that width and the view scrolls where it is larger; Tab reaches the diagram with a focus ring, Space opens it with the ring on "Schließen", Tab and an arrow choose a step with the ring on its label, Space on the checkbox closes it; "Schließen" and a second click close it; in print no control and the diagram in the column. In `Mit Editor`, `schlank` and `kompakt`: `+` and `-` held at the ends, ignored with Ctrl, Alt or Meta, and taking the focus of a step along, `/` opens no search and is `preventDefault`ed, in edit mode too, Escape closes the view and nothing else, before an open search panel; in the editor file the next Escape leaves read mode, and in edit mode the view lies over the toolbar, the pane does not scroll behind it, Escape closes it and Ctrl+Enter's render replaces the figure with the view closed; there, with the view closed, Escape closes an open download menu and edit mode stays. In `nur-lesen` `+` and Escape do nothing. With scripts off, in `nur-lesen` and `schlank`, a click opens it, "150 %" chooses its step and "Schließen" closes it; `schlank` shows the notice in place of the packed diagram. In Chromium the `nur-lesen` file is shown with two more system fonts and every label measured (see *Diagrams*, labels and fonts), and whether a label outside its symbol lies on a flow other than its own; what does not fit, or lies on a flow, is a note with a picture, not a failure.
- **Downloads of a diagram** (story 2.10). The names it asks the product (`diagramFileNames()` over the titles of every diagram block it read), and how a source stands in its link (`sourceDataUrl()`). It expects in every variant: below every drawn diagram, outside its view and below it, the line, a group named "Herunterladen", with the source link named after the title and the extension as its text, and none in a warning; no two names alike, the same title with `-2`; every source, read through its `data:` URL with `fetch()`, the block's text byte for byte, a laid-out BPMN diagram the author's XML with a diagram part (`BPMNShape`) put in; in `nur-lesen` each link written as the minimal encoding of the block's text, nothing escaped; in `schlank` each link written without `href`, its source gzipped in `data-gz-href`, and no source in the file as text, and once the file is open each link working as in the other variants; the picture button, transient, named by its file, in `Mit Editor`, `schlank` and `kompakt`, none in `nur-lesen`; each a small button of 12 px with the symbol. Then through the browser's download, per kind, the source (named and holding what it should), the source of the first diagram whose title has `%`, `/` or `?` (named as the product names it, in both browsers) and, where a script runs, the picture, which is opened alone in a page of its own and has to render at the size of its viewBox with no `var(` left, a BPMN picture in the colours of the document; the files and a picture of each `.svg` stay in `<browser>/exports/herunterladen/`. The clicks open no view, and with a view open the line lies beneath it. In `schlank` with scripts off no line of downloads is shown, every source link is packed and there is no picture button; the credit stays visible.
- **The live viewer of a BPMN diagram** (story 2.11). In the editor (the page that writes the files, in edit mode) and in `Mit Editor`: a Mermaid diagram opens in the static view; every BPMN diagram opens in `bpmn-js`, in a transient container directly before the stage, which is hidden, the logo visible and on top (`elementFromPoint` at its centre), the hint "Strg + Mausrad: zoomen · Ziehen: verschieben" transient in the bar; the viewer draws the elements of the picture (`data-element-id`), with its classes and every fill and stroke as the browser computes them in the picture, the laid-out diagram where there were no coordinates; at "Einpassen" the whole diagram inside the container, at most at 1.5; in a window 420 px wide "Schließen" fully inside the window and on top, closing the view by mouse; "Schließen" leaves nothing: no container, no hint, no `bjs-`/`djs-` container, no lightbox, no `djs-cursor-*` on `<body>`, no view open. On the first BPMN diagram: "150 %", "100 %", "200 %" and "Einpassen" set the scales 1.5, 1, 2 and that of the opening; Ctrl+wheel raises the scale, the view stays open and the step stays marked, and "Einpassen" chosen again fits it again; a drag of 140 px moves the diagram by as much, with the cursor class on `<body>` during it and none after, and a click moves nothing; neither closes the view; at "150 %" `+` gives "200 %" and scale 2, and Escape closes it, leaving nothing; so does Space on the checkbox; a click on the logo opens the lightbox, and Escape then closes the view and the lightbox; a render while it is open, the lightbox open, destroys both. Touch (story 2.23), Chromium only, by CDP (`Input.dispatchTouchEvent` with touch emulation on; Firefox has no such way, and its check says so in its name): at "Einpassen" one finger moves the diagram by its own 120 and 40 px, the scale kept; two fingers that begin on the two nearest shapes of their own inside the viewer (not labels, the shape each began on as the page heard it) pinch out to 1.6 times their distance in 16 steps, their middle shifted, and a third finger lands halfway and stays; at every step the diagram point under the fingers' middle before lies under their middle after, to 2 px, so the diagram moves by no more than the fingers did; at the end the scale is that of the start times 1.6, to 0.01, and the point under the start middle lies under the end middle; a second pinch to 15 times the distance stops at scale 4, and pinches to a thirtieth of it stop at 0.2; a tap on the logo opens its lightbox, as a click does, so `touchstart` lets a tap through; the view stays open; the container's computed `touch-action` is `none`, and every `touchmove` in the viewer is prevented, as a listener on `window` in the bubble phase hears it (`defaultPrevented`), so the browser neither scrolls nor zooms the page under it. Read from `e.targetTouches`, as the first version of the story did, the check fails: the scale stays far below 1.6 times, and the diagram jumps when the third finger lands. Without `preventDefault()` in `attachTouch()` it fails as well: none of the touchmoves is prevented. In the editor, `nur-lesen` and `Mit Editor` are written while it is open, the lightbox open and a drag going on (by events): neither file carries anything of it outside its scripts and styles, the read-only download destroys the viewer by its render, and after the `Mit Editor` save it runs on until Escape. In `nur-lesen`, `schlank` and `kompakt` a BPMN diagram opens in the static view, no viewer anywhere in the page. The click low on the tallest diagram of the large view (story 2.9) takes the tallest diagram that is not BPMN in the editor file, since a click in the live viewer closes nothing.
- **Search.** In `Mit Editor`, `schlank` and `kompakt` (since story 5.3). Which places the content container has, the preview or `main.reader-body`, how many hits of a term each holds and under which group headings they stand the run asks `collectPlaces()`, `findHits()` and `groupResults()`, over the container as the page shows it, read back into `linkedom`; that the two read the same paragraphs, items, headings and table rows is checked first. The run expects: `/` in read mode opens the panel, in `<body>` outside the container, transient, empty, the focus in its field; on the right below "Editor ↩" and not over it, in an export 64 px from the top as in the editor, at most 380 px wide and about as high as the window, at 1400 px and at 1600 px, where it lies over the rail; "Tabelle" lists one result per place that holds it, in order, each a part of its place's text with the term marked, and the summary gives the numbers with the sections; the results stand under the group headings Node expects, nested, each with the numbers of its branch, none a button, among them a group of the top with results of its own and one below a parent; "tabelle" finds the same; "xyzzy" says "Keine Treffer"; an emptied field says nothing; two switches below the field, both off; "statuschip" finds only itself so written, and light fuzzy flipped on lists without typing again every place with "Status-Chip" as well, as many as Node expects: one result marks all eleven characters of `Status-Chip`, and every mark is `statuschip` once blanks, hyphens and dots are taken out; "Ta" lists nothing and says it is too short; "oe" is searched, and in the demo text lists the one place that holds it; with case-sensitive on "tabelle" finds nothing and "Tabelle" the places that hold it so written; both switches off again; "die" lists more than the panel shows, and the list scrolled so that a result of the deepest group stands under the stack of its headings, the top of its group out of view, keeps that group's heading and those of its parents at the top of the list, stacked 28 px apart, with the result shown below them (a group below a parent, in both documents); and a group below a parent scrolled to its end while the parent goes on: its heading leaves through the band of its parent's heading, and the parent's heading lies over it there (`elementFromPoint`); a click on the last result scrolls its place into the window and the panel stays open; the close button closes it and `/` opens it again empty; in `Mit Editor` leaving read mode closes it, `/` in edit mode opens nothing; a `/` typed into the field of a table filter is a slash there. The magnifier (story 10): one in `<body>`, transient, visible, at least 32 px square, with the tooltip "Suchen (/)"; at 1600, 1400 and 1200 px, at the top of the page and scrolled down, in `Mit Editor` left of "Editor ↩" and in an export in the top right corner, 16 px from the edges, the same place each time, on top at its centre (`elementFromPoint`), over neither "Editor ↩", the licence link nor the rail; a click opens the panel with the focus in its field, and a click on the open panel holding "Tabelle" leaves it open with its term and results; in `Mit Editor` not shown in edit mode. With the large view of a diagram open the centre of the magnifier is part of the view, as are the window's centre and corners. The highlight (story 5), read from `CSS.highlights` as Ranges over its `StaticRange`s and compared with what `nodeRanges()` makes over the container read back: "Tabelle" highlights every hit, one range per hit, each covering the hit, all inside the container, and the container's DOM is the one it had before the panel opened; "xyzzy", an emptied field and "Ta" highlight nothing; light fuzzy flipped on, every hit of "statuschip", one range over `Status-Chip`; case-sensitive on, none for "tabelle" and every "Tabelle" so written; the close button, Escape and leaving read mode leave nothing highlighted; and with `CSS.highlights` taken away, "Tabelle" lists its results as with it, without an error on the console. Hits in tables (story 6): a result of a row reads "Tabelle: " before the row's text, unmarked, and the rest as above; then the checks listed under *Search*, *Hits in tables*: a term only rows hold, with its click and highlight, a row a facet filter hides, with its mark, its click, "Alle" while the panel is open and a term typed into the table's own field, and a row the field of a table without facets hides. The findings of the audit of the search (story 5.16): with a heading "Search Input" put into the container, a click on the label's text focuses the field, the field stands in its label and no element of the panel has an id (M3); closed with the focus in it, by Escape after the magnifier opened it and by "×" after `/` from the body, the panel leaves the focus on the magnifier (M2); in `schlank` the file writes `<main>` with the mark of its decoder, which the decoder has taken, and with the mark put back a search lists nothing and says "Diagramme werden entpackt …", and runs on the decoder's event (M1); Enter in the field from the end of the document brings the first result's place into the window with the focus left in the field, and Down, Down, Up, Up go to the first result, the second, the first and the field (N3); raw HTML put into the container, text directly in a `div`, `dt`, `dd`, `summary`, bare `blockquote` and `figcaption`, lists seven results with the paragraph in the `details`, the image's `alt` none (N1); and the first facet table put into a closed `<details>`, with a word put into its first row, lists that row without "(ausgeblendet)", and a click opens the `<details>` and brings the row into the window (N8); each of them taken back afterwards. Control (2026-10-04, Chromium, reference document): on the built file of `6eb0b39`, before the story, the run fails on exactly these checks, five in each of `Mit Editor`, `schlank` and `kompakt` and the mark in `schlank`, 16 of 1 025, and on none other. In `nur-lesen`: no panel, no magnifier, and no `.search-` rule in its stylesheet or in the open page. Control (2026-10-03, Chromium, reference document): built with the summary saying "Treffer in", with the click scrolling nothing and with `/` opening the panel from a field, the run fails on exactly those three checks, and on the licence checks that follow because the panel was left open. Control of story 5.3 (2026-10-03, Chromium, demo text): with the reader bundle not starting the search, the run fails on "/" opening the panel in `schlank` and in `kompakt`, the two checks the rest of the search waits on.
- **Licence information.** What the view has to name the run takes from the list itself, `NOTICES` and `LICENCE_TEXTS` in `src/app/licences.js`; that the list is right is the business of `tests/licences.test.mjs`. The run expects in every variant: the link visible, reading "license information", in the empty top margin above the first line, inside the window, its right edge on the right edge of the text column and so never above the rail, at 1400 and 1600 px where the screenshots are taken and at thirteen widths from 320 to 1900 px on one page that is resized; the document standing where it stands with the element taken out of the page, with the view closed and with it open; a click on the link opening the view, which names the four entries with version, licence and copyright lines and the licence text, lies on top of the page and is inside the window, at 1400 and at 600 px; a second click closing it. A read-only export carries the element once in its text, directly after `<body>` and closed, and is opened once more with scripts switched off, where the link has to open and close as well. In `Mit Editor` the element is there twice, each marked transient; the link never overlaps "Editor ↩" and stands left of it and of the magnifier up to 820 px; in the exports it stands left of the magnifier's corner up to 820 px, at least 51 px from the right, and never overlaps the magnifier (story 5.10); in edit mode it stands in the toolbar, its view hangs below the toolbar and moves nothing; at 600 px it is in the hamburger panel and nowhere else, and its view opens inside the panel; with the download menu open as well the panel stays inside the window, and every action in it can be brought between the window's upper and lower edge by scrolling the panel alone. The right edge of an action is not judged there: in the panel the download menu stands beside its button and runs past the window's right edge, as it did before story 2.18. A file that predates the licence information fails these checks, as one that predates callouts fails theirs.
- **Browsers.** Chromium from `/usr/bin/chromium` and Firefox from the Playwright cache (`--browser chromium|firefox|all`; `CHROMIUM` and `FIREFOX` override the paths), one process per browser and run, a browser context per opening. WebKit is not run.

Five things the run does on purpose, each because the obvious way gave wrong results or none, or took longer than it had to:

- **It waits on the DOM, not on names of the script.** The PoC's harness read `initDone`, called `render()` and read `downloadInFlight`. A bundle has no such names. Instead: init is done when `#dokufix-rail` has the class `has-items`, because the rail is the last thing the first render builds. A render is finished when a marker element the run put into the rail before pressing "Rendern" is gone, because every render ends by rewriting the rail. A download is through when no `button[data-download]` is disabled. All three hold for the PoC as well, which is how the two can be compared by one run.
- **`Date` and `Math.random` are deterministic stand-ins.** The read-only exports print their export time, Mermaid derives its SVG ids from `Date.now()`, and Mermaid 12 draws node outlines with randomised control points. `bpmn-js` takes one value of `Math.random` when it loads, so a file with and a file without its script tag hand Mermaid different values (see *Diagrams*). The clock still ticks, one millisecond per reading: with a frozen `Date.now()` both diagrams of a document get the same id and Mermaid draws the second into the first.
- **The footnote preview is revealed by focus, not by hover.** Hover under Playwright's synthetic mouse is what produced the retracted Firefox numbers (see *Footnotes*).
- **It waits on conditions, not on fixed times** (see *Waits on conditions* above). The fixed pauses it had added up to about 24 s per browser, in a run of 73 s in Chromium and 103 s in Firefox (measured 2026-10-03, one browser at a time).
- **Known deviation in the Playwright Firefox build.** Once a page has been open for about a second, focusing a marker no longer reveals its preview: the host matches `:focus-within`, the rule on its child does not take effect. With `position-try-fallbacks` switched off on the preview it appears. This was measured on the file before any change of story 2.1 and has not been checked by hand in a real Firefox. Where it happens the run switches the fallbacks off with a style tag and prints a note instead of failing.

### Save round trip (`tests/speichern.mjs`)

`node tests/speichern.mjs` — the check for the one thing no screenshot shows: that a saved file carries its document. Per browser, each opening in a browser context of its own, so with fresh storage:

1. open `dist/dokufix.html`: the editor holds `src/demo.md`; type document A, save as `Mit Editor`;
2. open that file: the editor holds A, version `v1`, not marked as changed; type document B, save;
3. open that file: the editor holds B, version `v2`; "Demo zurücksetzen" gives the original demo text, and every image it names is shown, though neither A nor B names one: both saved files carry the images of the demo text in their `#dokufix-assets` block, each under the hash of its bytes;
4. build a copy of `src/` whose demo text contains `</script>`, `<!--` and `<script>`, open it: the editor holds that text unchanged.

Every save types a version description that contains `<!-- <script>` and `</script>`; the second save is made with heading numbering switched on, and the second generation has to open numbered. The run also reads both saved files as text: `#dokufix-history` parses and holds that description as typed, `#dokufix-source` and `#dokufix-demo` hold `{"gz": …}` that unpack to the document and to the demo text, and the script is the script of the file under test, byte for byte. A and B contain what could break a block or a replacement: `</script>`, `<!--`, backticks, `${…}`, `$&`, backslashes, quotes, non-ASCII. The saved files stay in `tests/out/speichern/`. Exit 1 when anything fails. Controls: with the line that writes `#dokufix-source` taken out of the save, the run fails in both generations; with `encodeJsonForScript()` escaping only `</script>`, as it did in the PoC, the run ends with a timeout while opening the second generation (Chromium).

**The saved file as a whole.** A save clones the running page, so whatever the page gained while it ran can end up in the file. The run compares each saved file with the built file. A saved file is the browser's serialisation of the clone, so its text is not the built file's even when nothing leaked: `hidden` becomes `hidden=""`, and the line breaks around `<html>` and `</body>` move. So both files are opened with scripts switched off and read back from the DOM, which gives two texts in the same form. They must be equal apart from five differences, which the run lists with their reasons (`ALLOWED`): the content of the data blocks, the version mark, heading numbering, which is the class `numbered` on `<body>` and the state of its button, and where `hidden` stands among the attributes of the storage banner, because a banner that was shown lost the attribute and the save puts it back behind the others; that it is there is compared. Five more saves visit states that leave marks on the page's own elements, and each is compared the same way (the fifth, the large view open, also checks the picture button below each diagram: in the running page, transient, and there again when the saved file is opened, story 2.10): a narrow window saved with the hamburger panel open, a page without IndexedDB, which the run takes away with an init script before the page's script runs, a page whose licence view is open, in read mode and in the toolbar, a page whose search panel is open in read mode with a term, its results and its hits highlighted, and a page whose large view of a BPMN diagram is open in read mode at "150 %" (story 2.9), its live viewer running at scale 1.5, the lightbox of its logo open in `<body>` and a drag going on, the cursor class of `bpmn-js` on `<body>` (story 2.11): that file too is the built file but for the listed differences, as every saved file is; the run compares it with the built file, not with a second file saved with the view closed. The licence file has no licence element; opened, it makes both again, closed. The search file has no panel, no magnifier and no highlight; opened, its panel is closed and empty. The large-view file opens with its diagram closed, at "Einpassen", no viewer running and nothing of `bpmn-js` in `<body>`, and the page scrolls. From the page with the search open the run also writes `nur-lesen`, `schlank` and `kompakt`, each started with the term typed anew and its hits highlighted, and again after the panel is closed, the page's clock standing still (`page.clock.setFixedTime()`) so that the export time in them is one: each file written from the page with the panel open is the file written after it is closed, byte for byte. That shows the open panel changes nothing of the files; it does not show that the highlight stays out of the document, because a read-only download renders the preview anew before it clones it and empties the clone's preview, so the ranges are over replaced nodes by then. That no highlight reaches the document's DOM is the check of `tests/vergleich.mjs` with the panel open (the container's DOM unchanged, *Comparison run*, Search). The same holds for the metadata panel a click on its result in the search opened (step 10): the saved file is the built file as every other and opens with the panel closed, and `nur-lesen`, `schlank` and `kompakt` written after such a click are byte for byte the files written with the panel and the search closed. Anything else fails the run, which prints the place and both texts. A difference that is not on the list is a leftover; the place to remove it is the clean-up of the clone in `with-editor.js`, not the list. Controls: with the line that resets the badge's text taken out of the save, or the one that removes Mermaid's tooltip element, the comparison fails in both generations and shows the text or the element; with the clean-up as it was before the two states were visited, it fails in both of them; and in every run a copy of the first generation with one element more has to be reported as different.

### Failure cases (`tests/durchlaeufe.mjs`)

`node tests/durchlaeufe.mjs` — the check for what a render and an export do when something fails. The comparison run shows that a valid document looks as it did; it says nothing about one that is not valid. Per browser, thirteen cases:

1. *A diagram with an error.* One Mermaid block with a syntax error between two valid ones: that block is a warning with Mermaid's message, the other two are drawn, there is no error picture, and nothing of Mermaid's is left outside the preview. Then all four downloads, reopened: the warning is in each, styled.
2. *Overlapping renders.* A second render is requested while the first is in its passes. Each rail is built from the document the preview shows at that moment, and the preview ends as the second one's.
3. *A transient element.* Elements marked `data-dokufix-transient` in `<head>`, in `<body>` and inside the interface: none is in the saved `Mit Editor` file. An element that is not marked is. The two elements of the link "license information" are transient elements of the page itself; their views are open while the files are written. The saved file has neither, and each of the three read-only exports carries the element it writes itself: once, directly after `<body>`, closed.
4. *Markdown cannot be parsed.* `marked` is given a hook that throws: the warning is all the preview holds, and the rail is rebuilt and empty. The three read-only exports carry the warning.
5. *A pass throws.* A copy of `src/` is built with two passes more, as the save round trip builds a copy with another demo text; the product has no switch for this. A document pass that throws stands in the middle of the list: one warning names it at the top, every other pass did its work, the rail is built, and the three read-only exports carry the warning. A run-time pass adds a transient element and then throws: its warning and its element are in the page and in no export.
6. *An export step throws.* A second copy is built with one export step more. Each of the three read-only downloads ends in a dialog that says the download failed, with the step's message; no file is handed over, and the download buttons are enabled again.
7. *A broken marker among working ones.* Three block markers, the one in the middle with a misspelt name: that one is a warning at its place and its list stays a list, the cards before it and the step list after it are built and styled, the passes around them ran, and no pass failed: nothing is on the console. Then all four downloads, reopened: the same in each. It runs on the file under test, after case 4.
8. *A facet filter.* A facet marker that names a column its table has, one that names a column its table does not have, and one in a footnote definition: the second is a warning at its place and its table stays a table in its wrapper, the first and the third filter, and the preview of the footnote holds the table's text and none of the controls. Nothing is on the console. With a value chosen, a copy of the preview, as a download writes it, carries `checked` on the controls for all rows alone. Then a value is chosen in the page before each of the four downloads; each file, reopened, shows all rows with "Alle" chosen, and filters when a value is chosen there.
9. *A free-text filter.* A `filter` marker before a table, one before a paragraph, and one in a footnote definition: the second is a warning at its place and its paragraph stays a paragraph; the first gives its table a field, the third its table in the list of footnotes, and the preview of the footnote holds the table's text and no field. Nothing is on the console. Typing shows only the rows with the term, and the counter says "2 von 5 Zeilen". Then a term is typed in the page before each of the four downloads; each file, reopened, shows every row. `nur-lesen` has no field and, outside its stylesheet, nothing of the filter; `schlank`, `kompakt` and `Mit Editor` keep the marks of both filter tables, open with two empty fields, and filter there. The checks of transient elements in cases 3 and 5 count those fields, which the two exports make themselves, and read a file's text without its scripts, whose filter code names the attribute.

10. *A BPMN block that cannot be drawn.* A block that is no XML between two valid ones: it is the warning that names its title and the reason, the two others are drawn with "Gezeichnet mit bpmn-js" below them, the name a link, the passes around them ran, and nothing of the drawing is left in `<body>`. Then all four downloads, reopened: the same in each, and no read-only file carries anything of `bpmn-js`: no tag, no URL, no code, not the comment of its export.
11. *`bpmn-js` cannot be loaded.* The page's request for the library fails. The BPMN diagram is the warning that the library was not loaded, the Mermaid diagram beside it is drawn in its figure, and the passes around them ran; the three read-only downloads carry the same.
12. *BPMN without coordinates.* A block laid out by dokufix beside two it cannot lay out, one with two pools and one with nodes in no lane: the first is drawn with its credit, the other two are warnings naming their titles and the layout's reasons, at their places; nothing of the drawing or the layout is left in `<body>`, no element of Mermaid's layout is anywhere in the page, and there is no Mermaid error picture. Then all four downloads, reopened: the same in each, and no read-only file carries anything of `bpmn-js` or of Mermaid's layout (its URL, `swimlane`, its node ids, the render id).
13. *Mermaid cannot be loaded.* The page's request for Mermaid fails, `bpmn-js` loads: the script runs and the rail is built, the BPMN diagram with coordinates is drawn, and the Mermaid diagram and the BPMN diagram without coordinates are each the warning that says Mermaid was not loaded; the three read-only downloads carry the same.
14. *The live viewer cannot start* (story 2.11). `bpmn-js` is taken from the page after the render (`BpmnJS` undefined), then replaced by one whose constructor throws, then by one whose import fails: each time the large view of the BPMN diagram opens in the static view of story 2.9, the picture shown, no viewer and no warning, one line "BPMN viewer failed" on the console, and "Schließen" closes it; with the library put back, the same click starts the viewer and hides the picture. No page error.

In the diagram cases, 1 and 10 to 13, every figure, in the page and in each file, has the checkbox of its large view, and no warning carries a control of one (story 2.9); every figure has its source link and, except in `nur-lesen`, its picture button, and no warning carries a download; in case 13 the drawn BPMN diagram keeps its source link and the refused diagrams have none (story 2.10). The checks of transient elements in cases 3 and 5 count the picture buttons that `schlank` and `kompakt` make themselves, one per diagram, and the search panel and its magnifier, one each. In cases 1 to 6 and 10 to 14 the error is on the console and no promise is rejected; in cases 7 to 9 there is no error: a marker that cannot act is no failure. The run waits on the DOM as the comparison run does; `marked` is the library's global and not a name of the script, which is why case 4 can reach it from outside. Exports and saved files stay in `tests/out/durchlaeufe/`. Exit 1 when anything fails. Control: run on the built file as it was before story 2.15 (`--file`), cases 1 to 4 fail. The error picture is in the preview and in all four downloads; the first render builds its rail while the preview already shows the second document; the marked elements are in the saved file; and case 4 ends with a timeout, because that render never rebuilt the rail.

## Known PoC limitations (deferred to MVP)

- **CDN-loaded libraries** — pinned to fixed versions (see *Libraries*), but still loaded from the network. Production target is single-file inline. That turns the editor variant from about 240 KB into several megabytes: `mermaid.min.js` 12.0.0 alone is 5.6 MB before compression.
- **No File System Access API integration** — Chromium-only, optional power-user path. Not in PoC. See product brief distillate for design.
- **Mermaid SVG bloat unaddressed** — each SVG ships a redundant 1.5–3 KB `<style>` block. Future optimization: dedupe to a single document-level `<style>`.
- **BPMN SVG bloat unaddressed** — `bpmn-js` writes every style on every element; the demo text's diagram, 27 elements, is 30 486 B in `nur-lesen` (see *Diagrams*). Not shortened: the exports carry the output of its export function as it is, apart from what `finishBpmnSvg()` does.
- **Heading ID stability** — slugify is deterministic per heading text, but reordering or renaming headings shifts the `-N` dedupe suffix for other slugs. External bookmarks to `#einleitung-2` go stale when an earlier colliding heading is renamed. Tracked in `_bmad-output/initiative-dokufix/deferred-work.md`; a content-addressed slug (hash of text + position) would be the principled fix.
- **Per-version history grows linearly with snapshots** — full gzip snapshots per version dominate file size once a document accumulates many versions. A diff-based encoding (gzipped patch against prior version, ~10× smaller) is in the backlog for the MVP build pipeline.

## File layout

```
build.mjs               The build. One command, see "Build".
package.json            esbuild, eslint, globals, linkedom and playwright-core, pinned exactly; scripts build, watch, check, test, vergleich.
eslint.config.mjs       The lint: four rules over src/. See "Lint".
dist/
├── dokufix.html        The product. Built, committed, never edited by hand. Open in browser.
├── dokufix-showcase.html  The same page with the showcase text, for a first look. Built, committed.
└── dokufix.dev.html    The readable build (node build.mjs --dev). Not in git.
src/
├── index.html          The page: head, markup, data blocks, CDN tags, one slot per other source.
├── doc.css             Document styles, the one source for everything that travels with a document.
├── app.css             Styles of the editor interface.
├── search.css          Styles of the search panel, in the page behind app.css and in the reader bundle.
├── app.js              The script's entry: library setup, the register…() calls in order, the async init.
├── reader.js           The second entry: the reader bundle for schlank and kompakt, table filter and search, bundled into a data block of the page.
├── app/                The script's modules. See "The script's modules".
│   ├── state.js        The values more than one module assigns to.
│   ├── dom.js          The elements more than one module works on.
│   ├── gzip.js, idb.js, assets.js, document.js, persistence.js
│   ├── html.js, warning.js, passes.js, transient.js
│   ├── frontmatter.js, callouts.js, chips.js, licences.js, render.js, toc.js, footnotes.js, rail.js, editor.js
│   ├── nodes.js, markers.js, cards.js, steps.js, facets.js, tables.js, filter.js
│   ├── diagrams.js, large-view.js, escape.js, bpmn.js, bpmn-layout.js
│   ├── search-match.js, search-places.js, search.js
│   └── downloads/      menu.js, download.js, with-editor.js, export-body.js, readonly-open.js, readonly-slim.js, readonly-compact.js
├── demo.md             The demo text: every variant and special case, for the regression checks.
├── showcase.md         The showcase text: each core feature once, the demo text of dist/dokufix-showcase.html.
├── assets/             The images of the demo text, each named by its SHA-256.
└── README.md           This file.
tests/                  Checks that look at sources and built file from outside. See "Checks".
├── check-doc-styles.mjs       Fails when a document style sits outside doc.css.
├── check-doc-styles.test.mjs  Breaks copies of src/ and expects the check to fail.
├── build.test.mjs      Breaks copies of src/ and expects the build to fail; same sources, same file; the showcase; the readable build.
├── lint.test.mjs       Breaks copies of src/ and expects the lint to fail.
├── frontmatter.test.mjs  The frontmatter parser, in Node.
├── passes.test.mjs     The pass runner over a parsed fragment, in Node; which headings count; headings with a chip; broken markers among working ones.
├── callouts.test.mjs   Callouts over markup as marked emits it, in Node; the symbols in the document styles.
├── chips.test.mjs      Status chips over markup as marked emits it, in Node; colours, contrast and marks in the document styles.
├── markers.test.mjs    Block markers, cards and step lists over markup as marked emits it, in Node; the warnings in their words; the rules in the document styles.
├── facets.test.mjs     The facet filter over markup as marked emits it, in Node; the refusals in their words; the fixed rules in the document styles.
├── filter.test.mjs     The free-text filter in Node: the mark, the field, typing, the text of a row, beside facets, the export step; its rules in the document styles.
├── tables.test.mjs     The wrapper and the sub-line over markup as marked emits it, in Node; the pass's place in the list.
├── escape.test.mjs     The one Escape listener in Node: the first step that applies wins and stops the event; the order of src/app.js and src/reader.js.
├── search.test.mjs     The search in Node: hit ranges, case, light fuzzy, the minimum length, lowering that changes the length, the part a result shows, which places there are with which text, the results under their headings, the node ranges of the hits, table rows and diagrams as places.
├── licences.test.mjs   The list of licence notices against the versions the sources pin, in Node; the markup of the link and its view.
├── diagrams.test.mjs   The figure of a diagram and its title, with renderers of its own, in Node.
├── bpmn.test.mjs       The BPMN module in Node: coordinates, classes, the finished SVG; the renderer with stand-ins for bpmn-js and Mermaid.
├── bpmn-layout.test.mjs  BPMN without coordinates in Node: reading, the Mermaid text, the geometry and its corrections, the diagram part.
├── bpmn-fixtures.mjs   The fixtures of BPMN without coordinates: loader, layout in both modes, "npm run fixtures".
├── bpmn-fixtures.test.mjs  Every fixture laid out in Node, byte for byte against its expected XML.
├── bpmn-rules.mjs      The rules a laid-out diagram keeps, and the known list of today's breaks.
├── bpmn-rules.test.mjs  The rules on every measured fixture, against the known list.
├── capture-bpmn.mjs    "npm run capture": raw positions and measured label sizes of an input, from the built file in Chromium.
├── fixtures/bpmn-layout/  36 inputs of BPMN without coordinates, each with raw positions, sizes and the laid-out XML expected; index.json, known-breaks.json.
├── vergleich.mjs       Comparison run: four variants, screenshots, sizes, assertions.
├── speichern.mjs       Save round trip: two generations of "Mit Editor", each compared with the built file; saves in a narrow window, without storage, with the licence view open, with the search panel open, after the search opened the metadata panel; the read-only exports with the search open and closed, and after the search opened the metadata panel, byte for byte.
├── durchlaeufe.mjs     Failure cases: a diagram with an error, a BPMN block that cannot be drawn, a page without bpmn-js, BPMN without coordinates beside blocks it cannot lay out, a page without Mermaid, a pass that throws, two renders at once, transient elements, the licence view open while files are written, a broken marker among working ones, a facet chosen and a term typed while files are written.
├── referenz.md         Neutral reference document, the one input of every comparison.
└── out/                Exports and screenshots of the runs. Not in git.
poc/                    The hand-written single file of story 2.1 with its README and checks. Frozen.
```

The product is a single file. Everything you see when you open it (HTML, CSS, JS, demo content, assets) lives inside `dist/dokufix.html`, and a file saved from it needs nothing from this repository. `src/`, `build.mjs` and `tests/` are how that file is made and checked, not part of it.
